This file is the R4-0 deliverable of M7.4 (PLAN 14.10, DEC-144, brief `briefs/audit-2026-11/R2-R3-R4-people.md`
section R4). It is the hard gate for every later M7.4 slice: **no erasure code is written, and no later
slice starts, until an independent privacy and safeguarding reviewer, plus the four-concern review in
`AGENTS.md`, have reviewed this exact file and their sign-off is recorded in the R4-0 pull request.**
Nothing in this file declares itself approved, authorizes implementation, or records a legal conclusion.
Anything the source could not settle is marked **OPEN** (section 13: *Proposed* or *Gated*) with its reason and the evidence that would settle it.

**What "complete" means here.** Every one of the 39 tables, every personal column and JSON/text payload in them,
every production writer of `audit_logs` (section 6), every production `notify.Send` caller (section 9.2), and every
store outside the database that the repository itself writes (section 8) has a row with a source citation and a
proposed disposition. It does **not** mean a population scan of any real database (none was made and none is
needed), knowledge of rows written by older releases whose code is gone, knowledge of copies held by mail
providers, push services, logs, backups or downloaded files, an attribution for every stored IP address (6.5.1 names
the later teen-completion rows whose triggering caller no stored key identifies, and the transfer rows of any
database whose audit history is not shown to have been written by the current binaries), or an attribution for rows addressed to a past address
with no retained id link (O-17). Those are named, bounded limits (sections 8, 11, 14). **Naming a residue does not authorize it.** DEC-144 leaves only "an anonymous tombstone needed for audit and safeguarding integrity" and requires the inventory to name what stays and why; a stored IP address is not anonymous. So the IP residue of 6.5.1 (the unattributed completion rows, the transfer rows of an unestablished history, and any conversion group retained because a candidate is malformed or ambiguous) is a **completion gate for M7.4 (G-9, 6.5.2)**, not a limitation a reviewer can accept. Staged slices may merge with the residue disclosed; the milestone may not be called complete.

**Reading guide for the signer.** Three kinds of statement appear below, and they are labelled:

- *Traced*: read in the source at this commit, with a `file:line` cite (cites drift; re-open the file).
- *Proposed*: a disposition this draft recommends, **pending the independent review**. Never self-approving.
- *OPEN / gated*: not settled by source or by the five recorded owner decisions. Section 13 says which.

A fourth word is used for stored IP addresses: **Class A/B/C** (section 3.2) says who made the HTTP request that
wrote the address, which is not always the person the row names.

How should Fold handle this during account erasure?"** The owner's answer, verbatim: **"Allow a narrow exception to scrub only the erased person’s identifying text while preserving request decisions and provenance (Recommended)"**. The other option offered, a named PLAN retention exception (B), was not chosen.
5. Conversion-audit IP addresses (6.5.2 option P2; recorded 2026-11-04T02:38:12Z, evening of Nov 3, 2026, America/Boise). The question put to the owner, verbatim: **"Some household-conversion audit entries contain an IP address but do not identify whose it is. When an erased person may have caused the conversion, may Fold clear the IP from those entries—even if it might belong to another living participant—provided it is not essential safeguarding evidence? Decisions, actors, dates and provenance would remain. Some hard-to-identify records would still need a separate solution."** The owner's answer, verbatim: **"Yes, clear those IPs (Recommended)"**. The other option offered, **"No, keep uncertain IPs"**, was not chosen. The answer was given by the owner; nothing is inferred from elapsed time or from any other answer. Its bounded scope is stated in the next list and in 6.5.2.

How this draft applies them, and what it does not claim:

- Decision 5 authorizes **one bounded scope**, nothing wider: for **identified household-conversion audit entries** (the

  erased person may have caused the conversion**, Fold may **clear only the `ip` field**, **even if that address might belong to a
  different living participant**, **provided it is not essential safeguarding evidence**. The decision, the actors, the dates and
  the provenance of the conversion are preserved. It is the owner's authorization of the *concept* of P2 within exactly that
  scope. It is **not**: an approved implementation or design; a resolution of the per-action safeguarding review that the proviso
  requires; authority to blank every person's audit `ip`; authority to change the separate step rows of living people

  solution, and this file does not claim they are covered.

- Decision 1 is applied to *ordinary* `consents` rows only (section 7.2). `teen_consents` and the essential
  guardian evidence are a different, safeguarding domain; this draft proposes *retaining* them and lists the
  retention as a named exception that the safeguarding reviewer must sign (O-4). Decision 1 does not by itself

- Decision 2 is applied as: anonymize and soft-delete only the `household_people` row that *is* the erased
  individual, and **never clear or null `converted_user_id`** (section 4, T15). That row is found by exactly two
  deterministic links, either of which is the same individual and not a second person: a completed conversion
  (`converted_user_id` = the erased account) or an **approved but not yet completed teen conversion** (an

  erased account and `household_person_id` = the person; `converted_user_id` is still NULL, section 4 T15 and 3.1).

- Decision 3 is **implemented by R4-H** (section 12), not complete erasure. `DELETE /admin/users/:id` now takes

  and the later slices), recounts no admins (F-1), and disabling keeps host rows (DEC-38).
- Decision 4 settles **authority only**, for one narrow thing: Fold may have a single, erasure-only exception to the

  `contact_requests` can be scrubbed while the request's decision, purpose, subjects, actors, provenance, consent and
  every other protection stay intact. It is recorded as a dated, owner-approved amendment to **DEC-141** in `PLAN.md`

  system and does not lift the rest of the freeze; it is not permission to clear non-identifying data, another
  person's evidence, or free text only because its author (an actor) was erased; it is not permission to delete or

  **proposals** until the privacy/security and schema (DEC-138) reviews and exact tests in R4-2a confirm them. Those
  reviews, not this record, decide the trigger shape.

`models.All()` (`models/models.go`) lists **39** models. The baseline has **37** `CREATE TABLE`
statements (`migrations/00001_baseline.sql`, first at line 383, last at 1906). Migration
`00005_email_address_optouts.sql` adds the 38th table, classified as T38 below. Migration
`00006_admin_operation_attempts.sql` adds the 39th table, classified as T39. The complete list is:

account_requests, account_tokens, admin_operation_attempts, audit_logs, calendar_tokens, consents, contact_grants, contact_requests,
email_address_optouts, event_hosts, event_occurrences, events, groups, guardian_links, guest_signups, household_accounts,
household_people, households, invitations, jobs, login_throttles, mail_outboxes, manager_add_blocks,

registrations, room_bookings, room_feed_tokens, room_requests, rooms, series_undo_receipts, settings,

## 3. Ownership vocabulary and selectors

Every row of every table is exactly one of these, decided by **what the data is about**, not by who typed it:

- **Subject-owned**: about the erased person (their name, address, typed notes about them, their devices).
- **Address-owned**: keyed by the email address the person had at the moment of erasure (guest signups, invitations,
  account requests, outbox copies, throttles). The address is captured *inside* the erasure transaction before
  the tombstone rewrite and held in memory only; it selects the **unlinked remainder** of these rows. A row that carries
  a retained id link to an account is selected by that link instead (rule 6, 3.1), which is what survives an address change.
- **Other person's record**: authored by or merely acted on by the erased person, but about someone else
  (a removal note a leader wrote about a registrant, an invitation the person sent). Never scrubbed because the
  actor is the erased person.
- **Attribution**: only a numeric id of the erased person (`created_by`, `registered_by`, `granted_by`,
  `decided_by`, `actor_id`). Kept; renders as `User.PublicName()` = "Member" once the tombstone has no name
  (`models/models.go:177-185`).
- **Church record**: church-owned content or fabric (groups, events, rooms, announcements, settings).
- **Authorization input**: data a trigger or policy reads to decide whether someone may act. Never scrubbed.

Selector rules that apply everywhere:

1. Select by captured numeric id or by the exact normalized address captured in the same transaction. Never match
   by name, never match by IP, never match by the later holder of a reused address.
2. A returning person with the same address is a **new** relationship. Old work is not re-attached to a new
   account, and the old person's consent is not copied onto it (section 7).
3. The tombstone user is **never hard-deleted** and `converted_user_id` links are **never cleared** (T15, T37).
4. The unique `deleted-<id>@deleted.invalid` address (`api_admin.go:864`; unique constraint `uni_users_email`,
   baseline line 2538) is kept so the real address is free for a new account at once.
5. Soft deletes exist on exactly four tables: `users` (baseline 1920), `groups` (870), `events` (797) and
   `household_people` (1025). A soft-deleted row keeps every column it had, and `Unscoped` readers see them
   (`api_admin.go:214`, `api_contact_reads.go:49`, `api_notifications.go:257`, `api_add_request_reads.go:53`), so
   "soft-deleted" is not "erased".
6. **An exact identity link beats an address match.** The address in step 1 is the address the account holds *at
   erasure*. A row that names a *different* account through a retained id link (an invitation accepted by another
   user, a request approved for another user) is that account's row even if it carries the same address, so it is
   never selected by address. An address match is used only for a row with **no** retained link to any account (an
   unaccepted invitation, a pending or rejected request, a guest signup). A past address of the erased account is
   never matched against whoever holds it now; the only way to reach a row addressed to a past address is a retained
   id link to the erased account (3.1). Rows with neither a link nor the current address are a named limit (O-17).

### 3.1 Sets captured inside the erasure transaction

Selectors that need a join are computed once, **before** any scrub, from columns erasure keeps (ids, `status`,
`source`), and held in memory for the rest of the same transaction. No scrub statement removes a column another
selector reads (T01 keeps `approved_user_id`, `household_person_id`, `invitation_id`, `source`, `status`;
`invitation.accepted` audit rows keep `meta_json.userId`). Let `U` be the erased user id and `A` the normalized
address `U` holds at erasure.

| Set | Exact definition | Why it is exact |
|---|---|---|
| `REQ` (requests of `U`) | `account_requests.id` where `approved_user_id = U`; **or** (`approved_user_id` is NULL **and** `email = A`) | the first is a retained id link; the second is the unlinked remainder, by 3, rule 6. A request with `approved_user_id` set to someone else is excluded even when its email is `A` |
| `INV` (invitations of `U`) | the union of (a) `invitations.id` = `audit_logs.target_id` where `action = 'invitation.accepted'`, `target_type = 'invitation'` and `meta_json->>'userId' = U` (plain acceptance, `api_invitations.go:433,455-456`); (b) `invitations.id` = `account_requests.invitation_id` for `REQ` rows with a non-NULL `approved_user_id = U` (conversion); (c) `invitations.id` where `household_person_id` is in `PERS`; (d) `invitations.id` where `email = A` and `accepted_at IS NULL` (the unlinked remainder: open, revoked or expired rows nobody accepted) | (a) to (c) are retained id links, so they survive an address change `A0 → A`; (d) is the current-address rule, limited by rule 6 to rows nobody accepted. An **accepted** row with no (a)-(c) link (an acceptance audited by an older release that wrote no `userId`) is not matched by address, because the account that accepted it may be a previous holder of `A`: O-17 |
| `PERS` (the erased individual's own household people) | `household_people.id` where `converted_user_id = U` (completed conversion), **or** where `id = account_requests.household_person_id` for the single request with `source = 'household_conversion'`, `status = 'approved'`, `approved_user_id = U` (staged teen conversion, `converted_user_id` still NULL). Include soft-deleted rows | two partial unique indexes make the staged link at most one request per person and per user (baseline 2588-2596), so it cannot match a sibling |

`PERS` is the only set that makes a household person "the erased individual". It is never widened by household,
`kind = 'teen'`, birth date, surname or position in a household (T15).

### 3.2 Whose IP address is it?

`audit_logs.ip`, `account_requests.ip`, `guest_signups.ip` and `consents.ip` store the `ClientIP` of the **HTTP
request that wrote the row**. That is not always the person the row names, and `actor_id` is not a reliable guide.
Three classes, used in 6.5 and T01:

- **Class A, a request that the named person's own account made or created.** A signed-in session user acting on their
  own account (a sign-in, a profile edit, an own consent choice), or the request that created or activated the named
  account by setting its first password (invitation acceptance: the browser that now holds that account). The address
  is that person's; the evidence is the account itself.
- **Class B, an unauthenticated or token-bearing request that carries the named person's address.** The public
  sign-up form, a guest registration, the click on an emailed invitation, a password-reset form. Whoever holds
  the mailbox or typed the form made the request; the system cannot prove that is the named person.
- **Class C, a request by a different party than any person the row names.** An administrator or guardian who
  triggered a step (approving a conversion, deciding a link, granting teen consent) while the row's actor is
  somebody else (the inviter). Class C has two parts, and they are not treated alike:
  - **C-approval**: written inside the transaction in which an admin approved a conversion request. The owner **is
    identified exactly** by a retained key: `account_requests.decided_by`, stored in that same transaction before the
    conversion helper runs (`api_account_requests.go:526-540`). It covers `account.approved`, a teen's
    `guardian_link.requested` rows with `source = household_conversion`, and an adult person's **transfer rows**. The first two
    are selected by the request alone. The transfer rows are two audit actions with **different stored metadata**: the one
    `household.person_converted` row stores `accountType` and `transferCount`, and each `registration.changed` row stores
    neither (6.5.1). Only the `household.person_converted` row can be classified; each `registration.changed` row is
    reached by an exact key tuple back to it and inherits its class. The class is "approval" only when the
    `household.person_converted` row is unique, says `accountType = adult` and no age-rollover marker exists, **and** the audit
    history is shown to have been written by binaries that contain the marker and the staged teen conversion (the
    history precondition, 6.5.1). It is never read from `household_people.kind`, which the age job changes before
    completion. Where any of that is missing or ambiguous, the transfer rows are **retained as residue** (gate G-9, 6.5.2: staged progress, not milestone completion), and the
    report says so.
  - **C-completion**: written when a staged teen conversion completes later, before the birthday (`accountType = teen`) or
    after it (`accountType = adult` plus the marker). The caller is one of an admin deciding a link, an admin enabling the
    account, an admin or confirmed guardian recording consent, or the nightly age job with an empty address, and **no stored
    key says which**. This part, and the transfer rows of any history not shown to be current, are unattributable.

Blanking a Class A address for the erased person is attributable. A Class B address is *probably* that person and
unprovably so. A **C-approval** address is the approving admin's: it is attributable to that admin and to nobody else
(not the inviter, not the converted person). A **C-completion** address belongs to whichever of those parties ran the
step; it is the erased person's only if that party is the one erased, and no key shows it. That party can be the same person
as the approving admin, and the approving admin's other addresses are scrubbed while these stay: **an erased administrator's
address is not claimed to be scrubbed from every row.** Section 6.5.1 records the residue for C-completion, and for transfer rows whose
history or join is not established, and 6.5.2 makes it a completion gate for M7.4 (G-9); it never assigns that address to the inviter, the subject or the actor, and it does not use
the time of the row, the address value, a neighbouring audit row, the actor of a neighbouring audit row, the current
account type or `household_people.kind`, or the **absence** of a marker in a history not shown to be current, as a key.

## 4. One row per table (39)

Legend. **FK** = the foreign key to `users` (or the stated parent) with its delete rule and baseline line. Because the
user row is only ever soft-deleted, **no `ON DELETE CASCADE` or `SET NULL` action ever fires**; every
cascade-tabled row listed below must be removed by an explicit statement. **Disp.**: DEL delete rows, ANON keep
the row and blank named fields, RET retain, N/A holds nothing about an account holder, MIXED see section 5.
**Slice** names are defined in section 12. `file:line` cites are to this commit. "Writers" are the production
call sites found by grep; "readers" are the production sites that would show or use the data.
Fields not named are not personal.

### T01 `account_requests` (baseline 383; model `models.go:690`)
- **FK**: `approved_user_id`→users SET NULL (3275); `requested_by`→users SET NULL (3307); `decided_by` none; `household_person_id`→household_people (3291); `invitation_id`→invitations (3299); `group_id`→groups SET NULL (3283).
- **Personal**: `name`, `email` (citext), `phone`, `birth_date`, `message`, `guardian_email` (a third party's address), `password_hash` (staged, nulled at decision `api_account_requests.go:529` and expiry `:757`, but a still-pending row keeps it), `decision_note` (admin free text), `ip`; `flags` is booleans only.
- **Writers**: `api_account_requests.go:280` (self-signup, member invite), `api_guest.go:527` (guest upgrade; audit IP is the signup IP), `api_household_conversion.go:393`. **Readers**: admin list and bell, `mail_jobs.go:590` `prepareAccountRequestMail`, `notify_deliver.go:133` `queueReconciledNotifications` (reads `flags` only).
- **Disp.**: MIXED. Subject-owned rows are the set `REQ` (3.1: `approved_user_id` = erased, or an unlinked row whose `email` = the captured address; **a request approved for a different account is excluded even if it carries the same address**) → ANON: clear `name`, `email` (NOT NULL: neutral placeholder, see O-10), `phone`, `birth_date`, `message`, `guardian_email`, `password_hash`, `decision_note`; a pending row becomes `withdrawn`. Keep `id`, `source`, `account_type`, `status` (except pending→withdrawn), `decided_by`, `decided_at`, timestamps and every id link. Rows the erased person merely requested or decided → RET (attribution). Rows on another person's request whose `guardian_email` equals the captured address → clear that field only (address-owned).
- **`ip` is a separate decision from the row.** The column is the `ClientIP` of the submitting request (`api_account_requests.go:273` for sign-up and member invite, `api_household_conversion.go:387` (called with the acceptor's `ClientIP` at `:560`) for conversion, `api_guest.go:521` for guest upgrade), so ownership follows the **submitter by `source`**, not whom the row names or who `requested_by` is:

  | `source` | Who made the request (Class, 3.2) | Erased person is the named subject | Erased person is `requested_by` |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `self_signup` | the public form's submitter, unauthenticated (B) | blank `ip` with the row (**Proposed**, limitation O-19: the submitter may be someone else) | not applicable (`requested_by` NULL) |
  | `member_invite` | the inviting member, signed in (A, `requested_by`) | **keep `ip`**: it is the inviter's address, not the friend's | **blank `ip`** and nothing else: the friend's name, email and note are the friend's (RET) |
  | `guest_upgrade` | the guest form's submitter (B; copied from `guest_signups.ip`, `api_guest.go:521`) | blank `ip` with the row (**Proposed**, O-19) | not applicable |
  | `household_conversion` | the person who clicked the emailed invitation (B); **not** `requested_by`, which holds the inviter (`api_household_conversion.go:387`) | blank `ip` with the row (**Proposed**, O-19) | **keep `ip`**: the inviter did not make this request |

  Nothing reads `account_requests.ip` except `nominateTeenGuardian`, which copies it into a `guardian_link.requested` audit row (`api_teen_guardians.go:60`, see 6.5). The helper's other conversion-step `ip` values are the approving admin's and are reached through `decided_by` (6.5.1).
- **Selector/txn/slice**: `REQ` and the `source`-keyed `ip` rule above, captured id + captured normalized address; same erasure transaction; **R4-2a**. Do not null `household_person_id`, `approved_user_id`, `invitation_id`, `source` or `status = 'approved'`: they carry the conversion link, three other selectors (3.1) and the unique indexes at baseline 2588-2623. R4-2a tests: member-invite with the inviter erased (friend row intact, `ip` blank), with the friend erased (row anonymized, `ip` kept), and a request approved for another account that shares the erased address.
- **Implemented by R4-2a** (merged in PR #275; `captureErasureSetsIn`, `eraseAccountRequestsIn` in `account_erasure.go`, called from `apiAdminDeleteUser`): `REQ` is read once, with plain reads, before the first scrub. A pending `REQ` row becomes `withdrawn` and drops its password hash, and its waiting `AccountRequested` task is closed for every admin with `notify.Resolve(tx, notify.AccountRequested, id, &admin, 'withdrawn')`. Every `REQ` row then loses name, address (the placeholder `deleted-account_requests-<id>@deleted.invalid`, O-10), phone, birth date, message, guardian address, password hash and decision note, and keeps `ip` only for `member_invite` (M39); every id link, `source`, `status`, decision field, expiry and flag stays. A `member_invite` row the erased person requested loses only its `ip`. A `guardian_email` equal to the held address is cleared on any row (M13). All `account_requests` statements run before the `notifications` DELETE, so this path and `expirePendingRequests` both take request rows and then notification rows (L2, 9.5). No per-request audit row is written: `user.deleted` plus the `withdrawn` resolution is the record (a documented Consistency exception, 9.4). `prepareAccountRequestMail` sends nothing to a placeholder address. Tests: `TestErasureCapturesRequestInvitationAndPersonSets`, `TestAccountErasureAnonymizesAccountRequests` (member invite with the inviter erased and with the friend erased, a request approved for another account that shares the address, another person's request naming the address as guardian, a staged conversion that keeps every id link), `TestAccountErasureRequestAuditDisposition`, `TestAccountRequestApprovalLosesToErasure`, `TestAccountRequestExpiryRacingErasure` (both orders, own request and guardian-named request), `TestQueuedDependentMailForErasedAccountSendsNothing`.

### T02 `account_tokens` (baseline 437; `models.go:590`)
- **FK**: `user_id`→users CASCADE (3339; never fires).
- **Personal**: `payload` jsonb: `new_email` for `email_verify` (`api_profile.go:236`); for `teen_consent` the guardian's address, `guardianId`, `teenId`, `linkId`, `consentId` (`api_guardian_email.go:23-33`, written `:67-77`); `token_hash` is a capability hash.
- **Writers**: `tokens.go:63` `issueAccountToken`, `api_guardian_email.go:77`. Only unused rows are ever deleted (`account_security.go:126`, `tokens.go:69`); **no code prunes used or expired rows**, so their payloads persist (finding F-6).
- **Disp.**: DEL every row with `user_id` = erased, used or not (R4-1). A `teen_consent` row owned by a *living guardian* whose `payload.teenId` is the erased teen: DEL if unused (revoke); a used one stays the guardian's own record (R4-3).
- **Selector/txn/slice**: `user_id = id` and `kind = 'teen_consent' AND payload->>'teenId' = id::text AND used_at IS NULL`; same transaction; **R4-1**, **R4-3**.
- **Implemented by R4-1** (`account_erasure.go`, `eraseAccountCredentialsIn`): the delete callback removes every `account_tokens` row whose `user_id` is the erased account, used or not and of every kind, in the slot `revokeAccountTokens` held before the handoffs (that function is unchanged for its other callers). This closes F-6 for the erased account only; pruning living people's used tokens is not in T02's disposition and is not decided here. A living guardian's unused `teen_consent` token whose `payload.teenId` names the erased teen is **still R4-3**; R4-1 asserts it is left alone. Tests: `TestAccountErasureDeletesEveryCredential` (a used and an unused token of each kind for the erased account; another user's rows and the guardian's token byte-identical), `TestAccountErasureRollsBackWhole`.

### T03 `audit_logs` (baseline 473; `models.go:535`)
- **FK**: none, by design (`models.go:532-534`).
- **Personal**: `meta_json` keys, `ip`, and ids. Mapped by action, target and key in section 6.
- **Disp.**: MIXED, never row-deleted. RET all rows. Scrub only the keys section 6.2 names and, as proposed in 6.5, the `ip` of the erased person's own requests. The three authorization snapshots (`occurrence_access.added`, `occurrence_access.ended`, `occurrence.blocked`) keep their `meta_json`, `action`, `target_*` and `actor_id` untouched (6.7); `added`/`ended` are read by the provenance trigger, `blocked` only by the admin contact history.
- **Slice**: **R4-4a** attribution and metadata scrub, staged locally (6.9); **R4-4b** conversion minimization, gated (6.5.2). `user.deleted` still has empty metadata. The DELETE response carries integer counts only, including `activityEntriesKept`; no diagnostic action, class, reason, id or IP is exposed.

### T04 `calendar_tokens` (baseline 508; `models.go:567`)
- **FK**: `user_id`→users CASCADE (3347; never fires). **Personal**: only `token_hash` (a live credential for the feed). **Writers**: `api_feeds.go:427,518`.
- **Disp.**: DEL by `user_id` (R4-1). The link then 404s.
- **Implemented by R4-1**: `eraseAccountCredentialsIn` deletes the row by `user_id`, so the old feed URL answers 404 (`TestAccountErasureDeletesEveryCredential`). Link creation and scope change now take the contact gate and the user lock (`lockSessionCredentials`, which also compares `logout_version`), so a session loaded before an erasure, a disable, a credential change or a logout answers 401 and saves nothing (no token, no audit row), and a scope change whose link is gone under the lock answers the existing 404 with no audit row. Tests: `TestCalendarLinkChangesFromAStaleSessionSaveNothing`, `TestCalendarScopeChangeRaces`. `revokeCalendarToken` is unchanged: it holds only the calendar row, then writes an audit row with no foreign key, so it can delay an erasure but never waits on it.

### T05 `consents` (baseline 542; `models.go:920`)
- **FK**: `user_id`→users, no action (3315). **Trigger**: `consents_append_only` BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE rejects every change (baseline 3232 → function 352-359). **No address column.**
- **Personal**: `ip`, `user_agent` (clipped to 500, `email_unsubscribe.go:150`), `evidence_text` (≤1000; for push it embeds a device label, `api_push.go:274`). The decision (`channel`, `scope`, `status`, `source`, `at`) is the record.
- **Writers**: `email_unsubscribe.go:123,133` `setEmailConsent`/`setConsent` (one-click `:110`, settings via `api_email_settings.go:73`, `api_notifications.go:1000`); `api_push.go:273,332,481`. SMS is not built, so no `sms` row is written even though the CHECK (baseline 553-556) allows the channel and sources `sms_keyword`, `sms_reply`, `admin_import`. **Readers**: `api_notifications.go:821,896`, `push_deliver.go:519`, `messages_push.go:61`, `api_push.go:432`.
- **Disp.**: ANON, by owner decision 1: blank `ip`, `user_agent`, `evidence_text` only; keep every other column. Needs a focused new migration permitting exactly that update after the owner is soft-deleted (R4-2d). The address-level opt-out is implemented locally by R4-2d2 (O-1, 7.6 and 15.8); closure on master remains pending.
- **Implemented by R4-2d1b** (merged as PR #278, `4bb3e1e`): migration `00003_consent_erasure_scrub.sql` and `eraseConsentEvidenceIn` (`account_erasure.go`), called from `apiAdminDeleteUser` right after the soft delete. Tests: `TestConsentTriggerAllowsOnlyTheErasureScrub`, `TestConsentErasureScrubDownAndUp`, `TestConsentScrubMigrationTakesNoConsentsLock` and `TestConsentScrubKeepsThePreviousWriterWorking` (`migrations/migrations_test.go`); `TestAccountErasureScrubsConsentEvidence` and `TestAccountErasureRollsBackWhole` (`account_erasure_test.go`); the "real erasure" subtests of `TestConsentWritersRefuseAfterTheOwnerIsSoftDeleted` (`email_unsubscribe_test.go`, both orders, all five writers); and the opt-in `TestAccountErasureScale` (`FOLD_ERASURE_SCALE=1`, not run in CI). It blanks only `ip`, `user_agent` and `evidence_text`; the address-level opt-out (O-1) and `teen_consents` are untouched.
- **Slice**: **R4-2d**.

### T06 `contact_grants` (baseline 584; `models.go:1006`)
- **FK**: composite to `contact_requests` (3323); `leader_id`, `subject_user_id`, `ended_by` have none. **Personal**: ids only (no text). **Trigger**: `contact_grant_successful_request` on insert/update (baseline 77-89).
- **Disp.**: RET ids. Live grants where the erased person is leader or subject are ended by the contact reconciliation already inside `contactMutation` (`contact_lifecycle.go:49-72`); R4 asserts that and adds no second ending path. **Slice**: R4-2a (assertion).
- **Implemented by R4-2a** (assertion only, merged in PR #275): the erasure adds no ending path of its own; the contact reconciliation inside `contactMutation` ends the live grants once each. Test: `TestAccountErasureEndsContactGrantsOnce`.

### T07 `contact_requests` (baseline 624; `models.go:966`)
- **FK**: none to users (retained ids, `models.go:961-962`). Two foreign keys reference `contact_requests` and prevent deletion of referenced rows: `registrations_access_request_id_fkey` (baseline 3619) and the composite `contact_grants` key (baseline 3323). They do not prevent every unreferenced-row deletion. Fold preserves request rows under DEC-141; the adopted R4-T07A source guard rejects the recognized delete forms within its stated parse-only limits. **Triggers**: `add_request_identity`, BEFORE UPDATE FOR EACH ROW (function baseline 52-69, installed 3225), and the constraint trigger `add_request_completed`, AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE, DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED, FOR EACH ROW (function baseline 24-43, installed 3218). `contact_grant_successful_request` (baseline 77-89, installed 3239) is on `contact_grants`, not on this table. (Corrected Nov 5, 2026: this line used to say no other trigger is on the table.)
- **Two free-text fields with different producers, limits and readers.** They are classified separately; nothing below treats the trigger, or DEC-141, as proof that either is safeguarding evidence.
  - `reason` (≤1000, `contact_requests_reason_check`, baseline 673): typed by the **requester** into the create form, for all three purposes (contact `api_contact.go:268`; group add and occurrence add `api_contact.go:754,766`, trimmed, no minimum). It is shown to the people deciding: contact history `api_contact_reads.go:214`, group-add `api_add_request_reads.go:194`; the occurrence-add read blanks it (`:197`) though it is stored. A rejected create does not log it (`api_contact.go:263-265`).
  - `add_childcare_ages_note` (≤200, baseline 672; empty by CHECK unless `purpose = 'occurrence_add'`, so `contact` and `group_add` rows always hold `''`): typed by the requester, about the **subject's** children's ages. Not a name field; still personal.
- **What the trigger permits** (read exactly; baseline 56-64). It raises when the purpose changes, or when `OLD.purpose <> 'contact'` and either (i) any of `requester_id, subject_user_id, context_type, context_id, occurrence_id, needs_admin, add_over_limit, add_over_capacity, add_attendance_mode, add_guest_count, add_childcare_count, add_childcare_ages_note, created_at, expires_at` differs, or (ii) `OLD.status <> 'pending'` and **any** column differs. `reason` is **not** in list (i). So:

  | Field | `contact` (any status) | `group_add`/`occurrence_add` **pending** | `group_add`/`occurrence_add` **terminal** (`granted`, `denied`, `withdrawn`, `expired`, `ended`) |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `reason` | UPDATE allowed | UPDATE allowed (also together with the `status` change that ends it, in one statement) | **rejected** (SQLSTATE 23514), every update |
  | `add_childcare_ages_note` | always `''` | **rejected** (it is in list (i)), at every status | **rejected** |

  A pending add request that erasure ends is still pending when `contactMutation`'s callback runs; the reconciliation that ends it runs *after* the callback (`contact_lifecycle.go:62-65`). R4-2a can therefore blank a pending row's `reason` **in the callback, before** the reconciliation, and must do it in that order. After the row is terminal no ordinary UPDATE can touch it; the erasure-only exception (owner decision 4) is the only proposed path, and it is also the only path for `add_childcare_ages_note` at any status (the trigger blocks that column even on a pending row). Rows already terminal when the person is erased are the usual case.
- **Superseded Nov 5, 2026 (section 15.3).** G-7a and G-7b are rejected, so no automated scrub of `reason` exists for any purpose or status, and no slice blanks a pending row's `reason` in the callback; the sentence above that says R4-2a can do so is history, not a plan. The licensed blank (migration `00004_ages_note_erasure_scrub.sql`, R4-T07A; implementation record below) is the one permitted change to `add_childcare_ages_note`: only on an `occurrence_add` row whose subject's users row is soft-deleted, only from a nonempty note to `''`, with every other column equal. The `reason` cells in the table above are unchanged by it. The table above is the identity trigger's baseline field restrictions, which hold in a database that has not yet run `00004`; CHECK/FK constraints and the deferred add_request_completed check also apply. An UPDATE allowed by that table can still fail completion validation.
- **Ownership and proposed disposition** (`subject_user_id` / `requester_id` = the erased person). Keeping a text is an exception to owner decision 1's "scrub where technically safe". Where a scrub *is* technically safe, only a finding by the safeguarding reviewer that the text is **essential safeguarding evidence** (decision 1's own standard, as in O-4) can justify keeping it, and without that finding the cell is ANON. Owner decision 4 now supplies the authority for the scrub that DEC-141 and the baseline trigger otherwise block (terminal add-request `reason`; add-request ages note), but **only** for the erased person's identifying text and only through the erasure-only exception below; it does not decide, field by field, whose text is identifying, and it does not authorize scrubbing another person's evidence because an actor was erased. Those field decisions are the proposals in this table and the review gates G-7a to G-7d. None of this is signed.

  | Field | Erased = subject | Erased = requester only |
  |---|---|---|
  | `reason`, `contact`, any status | ANON (blank). Technically safe | **RET (Proposed), only if** the safeguarding reviewer records it as essential safeguarding evidence under decision 1 (it is the stated basis on which a living subject and an admin decided the contact); **otherwise ANON**. A scrub is technically safe, so retention needs that finding |
  | `reason`, add, **pending** | ANON, in the callback before the reconciliation | same condition as the row above: RET only on that finding, otherwise ANON in the callback |
  | `reason`, add, **terminal** | ANON (blank), **through the owner-approved erasure-only exception only** (owner decision 4; PLAN DEC-141 amendment). Without the exception the trigger rejects every update. The owner authorized scrubbing the erased person's identifying text, so the exception covers this cell when the erased person is the subject; the cell is the requester's wording to or about the subject, and whole-field blanking is proposed because identifying words cannot be picked out deterministically (section 3, rule 1). **Review gate G-7a** (privacy/security): confirm that whole-field blanking is the narrowest technically safe scrub for a subject-erased row, or name a narrower one | the requester-only rule above decides RET or ANON, and the exception does not change who decides. If it decides ANON the scrub is executable only through the same exception, keyed to the **requester** being erased; if it decides RET nothing is written. An erased actor alone is **not** a reason to blank this text, and the exception is not permission to blank a living subject's decision basis without the reviewer's finding. **Review gate G-7b**: the safeguarding reviewer's RET/ANON finding for this cell, recorded before R4-2a |
  | `add_childcare_ages_note`, add, any status | ANON (blank), **through the same exception only**: it is about the subject's children, so it is the subject's identifying household information. **Review gate G-7c**: confirm blanking is the narrowest safe scrub (a bare number of children is `add_childcare_count`, which is not text and is untouched) | RET: it is about the subject's children, not the requester's own data, and the exception does not authorize touching another person's evidence because the requester was erased. No write |

  Status Nov 5, 2026 (section 15.3): every `reason` cell above is superseded by G-7a and G-7b (rejected; predicate FALSE). `reason` is left untouched on every row, which is staged retention, not an approved final disposition; R4-T07B (below) holds it. The `add_childcare_ages_note` subject cell is signed as narrowed (G-7c): `occurrence_add` rows only (other purposes hold `''` by CHECK), where the subject is the erased person. The requester-only cell stays RET with no write.

- **The O-7 exception: owner-settled authority, unreviewed design.** Owner decision 1 says scrub personal details *where technically safe* and keep the decision and *essential* safeguarding evidence. PLAN DEC-144 (`PLAN.md`, DEC-144) keeps only an anonymous tombstone needed for audit and safeguarding integrity. For the cells in the matrix that name the exception, no UPDATE is possible without a change to `add_request_identity`, and **DEC-141** froze `contact_requests`/`contact_grants` schema. **The owner has answered** (owner decision 4, Nov 3, 2026, quoted verbatim in the owner-decision list above): *"Allow a narrow exception to scrub only the erased person’s identifying text while preserving request decisions and provenance (Recommended)"*. The earlier option (B) (a named PLAN retention exception) was not chosen and is withdrawn. `PLAN.md` DEC-141 now carries a dated owner-approved amendment saying exactly this and no more. This draft still does not claim the text is essential evidence. **What the answer settles:** the authority to have one erasure-only exception to the DEC-141 trigger freeze, limited to scrubbing the erased person's identifying text in `contact_requests` while the original request decision, purpose, subjects, actors, provenance, consent and every other protection are preserved; the rest of the freeze is unchanged. **What it does not settle** (each is a future review gate, and none needs another owner question):
  - **Status superseded Nov 5, 2026 (section 15.3); this bullet and its sub-bullets are kept as history.** Its heading's 'unreviewed design' and its statement that none of the gates it lists needs another owner question describe the state before the T07 reviews. For current T07 status, section 15.3 governs: G-7a and G-7b are rejected, G-7c is signed as narrowed, G-7d is narrowed conditionally, and `reason` is held by R4-T07B, for which Grier has since chosen an admin review step during deletion to decide whether to blank each reason (still to be designed and independently reviewed, not implemented). The owner-decision quotation above is unchanged, and this note changes nothing earlier reviewers signed.
  - **Field ownership predicates (G-7a, G-7b, G-7c above).** Which party's erasure licenses which field is a proposal (matrix above). A reviewer may narrow it or find a field is not identifying; a reviewer may not widen it to non-identifying data, to another person's evidence, or to text only because an actor was erased.
  - **Superseded Nov 5, 2026 by the adopted R4-T07A specification below; kept as history.** **Trigger and migration shape (proposal only, unimplemented, G-7d).** A proposed shape for independent privacy/security and schema review, which may reject or replace it: a new numbered migration that replaces `add_request_identity` (the exception may not be added by editing the baseline unless the DEC-138 window evidence in section 10 is freshly refreshed and independently reviewed) and permits an `UPDATE` only when (i) the new values of `reason` and/or `add_childcare_ages_note` are `''` and the old are not, (ii) **every other column is unchanged** (a whole-row comparison, not an allow-list of columns that could grow), (iii) the party whose erasure licenses each changed field has a soft-deleted `users` row, read inside the trigger, and (iv) the target is a `group_add` or `occurrence_add` row (the `contact` row already allows `reason` updates). No `DISABLE TRIGGER` at run time, no session-settable flag a caller could set, no broad `UPDATE` or `DELETE` allowance, no deletion of the row (`registrations.access_request_id` and `contact_grants` reference it) and no loosening of the purpose, identity, decision or outcome checks. Whether reading `users.deleted_at` inside the trigger is safe against concurrent restore or erasure, and what lock it needs, is G-7d, not assumed here.
  - **Preserved access provenance (R9).** The `registration_access_provenance` trigger and the occurrence-add proof compare `access_request_id`, `purpose`, `status` and the audit snapshots, not `reason` or `add_childcare_ages_note` (baseline 168-305). R4-2a must prove with the real trigger that the blank leaves them untouched; this is source reading only, not yet a test.
  - **Superseded Nov 5, 2026 by the adopted R4-T07A specification below; kept as history.** **Concurrency and rollback.** The scrub runs in the erasure transaction (callback before the reconciliation for a pending row, section above); a concurrent decision, expiry or `contactMutation` on the same row must serialize on the request row (R19.2 locks) and a failed erasure must roll the scrub back with it. Exact tests are required.
  - **Superseded Nov 5, 2026 by the adopted R4-T07A specification below; kept as history.** **Exact tests.** R4-2a (below) lists them: a terminal add row blanked with every other column byte-identical, the trigger still rejecting every other update (purpose change, status change, any non-blank edit, a blank for a party that is not erased), and the erasure committing.

  **Superseded Nov 5, 2026 for current T07 status by section 15.3; kept as history. Its limits still hold:** Until the field predicates and the trigger/migration shape have had independent privacy/security and schema review, and the R4-0 sign-off is recorded in the pull request: the `contact_requests` statements of R4-2a do not start; nothing is implemented under O-7 (no trigger change, no migration, no code); and the exception is neither a general contact-system redesign nor permission to clear non-identifying data, an unrelated person's evidence, or an owner database. It is not permission to publish, tag, release or deploy.
- **Slice** (Nov 5, 2026): **R4-T07A** (the ages note, adopted below) and **R4-T07B** (reason; design now approved, behavior pending; see 15.4). R4-2a merged without any `contact_requests` statement (PR #275). The R4-2a test list that stood here is replaced by R4-T07A's tests below.

#### T07 reconciliation with DEC-141 (one exception, two functions)

- **The amendment.** `PLAN.md` DEC-141's Nov 3, 2026 amendment, as Grier dated it, records one erasure-only exception to the `add_request_identity` trigger. The amendment is unchanged and is not re-decided here.
- **Two functions put it into effect.** The reviewed design puts that one logical exception into effect by changing two functions. `add_request_identity` admits exactly the licensed blank. `add_request_completed` skips its historical completion recheck for that same licensed UPDATE on a granted `occurrence_add` row only, because the recheck would otherwise fail on historical granted rows whose registration was deleted or whose `access_request_id` moved to a newer request.
- **Ruling (Q-R1).** The reviewer ruled that this is within the narrowed scope, is not a second capability and needs no owner decision. The ruling does not authorize blanking `reason`, blanking on requester-only rows, a general completion bypass or a retention exception.
- **Renaming.** The amendment's "implemented later, in M7.4 slice R4-2a" now refers to R4-T07A (ages note) and R4-T07B (reason).
- **Superseded description.** The earlier single-function proposal (the O-7 sub-bullet on trigger and migration shape above) is not the implementation specification.

#### R4-T07A adopted specification (adopted Nov 5, 2026; implemented in the R4-T07A implementation PR, not merged; see the implementation record below)

This subsection restates revision 4 of the T07-A design, with the corrections T07A-R7 to T07A-R13 and the four specification notes of the review of revision 3, so the inventory does not depend on a host file (the inventory restates host records, as the sentence on line 64 says). The source is `results/t07-redesign-rev4-20261105.json`, an autoflow host record written by the design author claude-opus-5-5; a design is not a review signature. Each item names its path in that file (`design.T07-A.<key>` unless stated) so a reviewer can compare. **Adopted means the specification may be implemented once the gates in section 15.3 are met. Nothing here is implemented: no migration number is taken, no Go code is written, and the guard fixtures, trigger tests, concurrency evidence and scale evidence are required implementation work that no review so far has executed or approved as passing.**

- **Scope** (`scope`; `why_completion_is_needed`). The erased subject's `occurrence_add` rows, at every status (pending, granted, denied, withdrawn, expired, ended). The only field touched is `add_childcare_ages_note`. `reason` is not touched on any row. Rows where only the requester or an actor was erased are not touched. `contact` and `group_add` rows are never selected (`contact_requests_purpose_scope`, baseline 672, already keeps their ages note at `''`). A subject may have several selected rows for the same registration, including a request whose access was replaced by a newer request; all are in scope. Granted rows go through the live recheck at baseline 31-36, which fails when the original registration was later deleted or when its `access_request_id` moved to a newer request on the same registration (`registration_access_provenance`, baseline 186-198). Without the completion exception, erasing such a subject fails at commit with 23514. A granted row whose access only ended, with the matching registration still in place, can still pass the original lookup; ended access is not deleted or replaced provenance, and the tests cover both.
- **Predicate and assignment** (`authority.gate_dispositions_as_signed_by_review.G-7c`). `r.purpose = 'occurrence_add' AND r.subject_user_id = U AND D(U) AND r.add_childcare_ages_note <> ''`; assignment `SET add_childcare_ages_note = ''`; statuses pending, granted, denied, withdrawn, expired, ended. D(U) is: the exact subject's existing `users` row has `deleted_at IS NOT NULL`, checked under `FOR SHARE OF u NOWAIT`. Excluded: `reason` on every row; rows where only the requester or an actor was erased; `contact` and `group_add` rows; every other request column, including counts, decisions, identity, actors and provenance.
- **The two function shapes** (`migration.up_identity`, `migration.up_completion`). One new consecutive numbered goose file, written only after #278 merges, the token is released and R4-T07A takes it. The `<...>` placeholder stands for the body of the definition immediately preceding the migration; at e806cf3 that is the baseline text (identity baseline 56-65, completion baseline 28-41), and it is rechecked on the post-#278 base.

  ```sql
  -- identity
  CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.add_request_identity() RETURNS trigger LANGUAGE plpgsql AS $$ DECLARE v public.contact_requests%ROWTYPE; BEGIN IF TG_OP = 'UPDATE' THEN IF OLD.purpose = 'occurrence_add' AND OLD.add_childcare_ages_note <> '' AND NEW.add_childcare_ages_note = '' THEN v := NEW; v.add_childcare_ages_note := OLD.add_childcare_ages_note; IF v IS NOT DISTINCT FROM OLD THEN PERFORM 1 FROM public.users u WHERE u.id = OLD.subject_user_id AND u.deleted_at IS NOT NULL FOR SHARE OF u NOWAIT; IF FOUND THEN RETURN NEW; END IF; END IF; END IF; END IF; <the body of the definition immediately preceding this migration, byte for byte, rechecked on the post-#278 base> END; $$;
  -- completion
  CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.add_request_completed() RETURNS trigger LANGUAGE plpgsql AS $$ DECLARE v public.contact_requests%ROWTYPE; BEGIN IF TG_OP = 'UPDATE' THEN IF OLD.purpose = 'occurrence_add' AND OLD.status = 'granted' AND OLD.add_childcare_ages_note <> '' AND NEW.add_childcare_ages_note = '' THEN v := NEW; v.add_childcare_ages_note := OLD.add_childcare_ages_note; IF v IS NOT DISTINCT FROM OLD THEN RETURN NULL; END IF; END IF; END IF; <the body of the definition immediately preceding this migration, byte for byte, rechecked on the post-#278 base> END; $$;
  ```
- **Structure rules** (`migration.structure_rules`). The outer `IF TG_OP = 'UPDATE'` is its own statement, so no INSERT event ever evaluates an expression that reads `OLD`, because SQL does not guarantee the order in which an AND is evaluated. The completion exception names `OLD.purpose = 'occurrence_add'` explicitly; for valid rows with a nonempty note the purpose-scope CHECK already implies it, so this states the signed boundary without widening anything. Function attributes stay as in the baseline: plpgsql, VOLATILE, SECURITY INVOKER, no SET clause, the same owner; the only new name the bodies reference is `public.users`, schema-qualified. The whole-row comparison uses a `%ROWTYPE` copy with only the note restored from `OLD`, so a column added later is covered automatically; `IS NOT DISTINCT FROM` handles NULLs. No DROP TRIGGER, CREATE TRIGGER, ALTER TABLE, SET CONSTRAINTS, data backfill or table rewrite.
- **Route, DEC-138, DEC-141, down, fallback** (`migration.route`, `.dec_138`, `.dec_141`, `.down`, `.fallback`, `.completion_relies_on_identity`). No table change, no `-- contracts:` header (nothing is dropped or renamed), no backfill, no baseline edit; the number is taken under the migration token after #278. The change is expand-only: the previous binary never sends a blank of the ages note, so the new bodies behave like the old ones for every statement it can send. No purpose, field, lifecycle state, constraint or `contact_grants` change. Down is `CREATE OR REPLACE` of both functions with the definitions that come immediately before the migration (the baseline definitions at e806cf3, rechecked after #278; if anything between them redefines either function, down restores that definition); down never reconstructs scrubbed text. Every statement outside the exception runs the original body unchanged: a living subject, a missing `users` row, a deletion not yet committed in another transaction, a nonempty replacement, a blank combined with any other column change, a requester-only row, `contact` and `group_add` rows, and a note that was already empty. The baseline identity check then rejects any note change on a `group_add` or `occurrence_add` row with 23514. The completion exception does not recheck D(U) itself: it relies on `add_request_identity` staying installed, unchanged in timing, as the only BEFORE UPDATE trigger on `contact_requests` (hard constraint H2).
- **Erasure statement** (`erasure_statement`). A helper `eraseContactRequestTextIn(tx *gorm.DB, s erasureSubject) error` in `account_erasure.go`. Statement 1: `SELECT id FROM contact_requests WHERE purpose = 'occurrence_add' AND subject_user_id = ? AND add_childcare_ages_note <> '' ORDER BY id FOR UPDATE`, pluck the ids, as its own statement so the lock order is fixed whatever plan Postgres picks. Statement 2: if no ids come back, return nil without sending an UPDATE, so no no-op event is queued. Statement 3: `UPDATE contact_requests SET add_childcare_ages_note = '' WHERE id IN ? AND purpose = 'occurrence_add' AND subject_user_id = ? AND add_childcare_ages_note <> ''`; `RowsAffected` must equal `len(ids)` or it returns an error; the SET list names only the ages note, and the guard checks that. Lock strength is FOR UPDATE, the strength `reconcileContacts` uses (`contact_lifecycle.go:336`), so no lock is upgraded later in the transaction; every traced holder of a conflicting FK key-share lock also holds the exclusive gate. The index `contact_requests_subject` (baseline 2574) supports the candidate search, but the required evidence is the actual plan on representative data. Stale-struct rule: a `ContactRequest` loaded before the scrub and saved after it would turn `''` back into the old text; both triggers reject that (23514) and the whole erasure rolls back. In the current callback no request struct is loaded before the scrub and saved after it: `reconcileContacts` reloads FOR UPDATE after the callback. Position: inside the `apiAdminDeleteUser` `contactMutation` callback literal, after the soft delete, before `reconcileContacts` and not in a savepoint that can roll back while the transaction continues; placement relative to the consent scrub is decided on the post-#278 callback. This design does not presume that shape.
- **Lock inventory and the no-wait qualification** (`lock_inventory`, `no_wait_qualification`), as conclusions. Every contact-affecting writer takes the exclusive transaction-level advisory gate (`contactLockID`, `contact_lifecycle.go:27-31`) first and holds it to commit; the gate is an application convention that neither trigger checks, so arbitrary SQL clients are not covered. The request-row writers traced (contact and add-request create, decision and withdrawal; occurrence-add completion; expiry and ineligibility reconciliation; ending a pending `occurrence_add` when a registration is removed; the new scrub) are serialized by it. The contact-grant trigger and its FK check reach only `contact` requests, so they do not reach the scrub's rows. The `registration_access_provenance` FOR SHARE lookup and registration FK key-share check can reach the same `occurrence_add` requests as the scrub. Their traced Fold writers hold the exclusive contact gate through commit, so they cannot coexist with the scrub on those paths; the scrub has no referenced-side FK action. The NOWAIT lookup in the identity trigger cannot raise 55P03 when reached from the helper, because the erasure transaction itself holds the subject's `users` row FOR UPDATE, which is compatible with its own FOR SHARE. Outside the erasure, a licensed blank can raise 55P03 if a third transaction holds the row with NO KEY UPDATE or FOR UPDATE (only for a statement the baseline already rejected); KEY SHARE and SHARE do not block it; a living subject or an uncommitted deletion fails with 23514; and a SHARE the blank takes blocks another transaction's NO KEY UPDATE or FOR UPDATE of the user row until the blanking transaction ends. **Not claimed:** that the scrub or the erasure never waits; that the lock stops the owning transaction from clearing `deleted_at` later in the same transaction (the license is established at scrub time, and no such restoration exists in the reviewed callback); a single globally ascending lock sequence (the user-then-request and request-then-user prefixes differ and only the exclusive gate serializes them); or deadlock freedom for arbitrary SQL clients, code that bypasses the gate, or the future R19 lock redesign. No ungated production writer of `contact_requests` was found in the inspected source; that is a list of traced paths, not a proof by type or call graph. **Recheck on the post-#278 base: the callback, the writers, this lock inventory and the preceding function definitions.**
- **Preservation** (`preservation`). Across the helper alone, for every selected row `to_jsonb(row) - 'add_childcare_ages_note'` is identical before and after and the note is `''`; every other request row, `contact_grants`, `registrations`, `audit_logs`, notifications, deliveries and jobs are identical. Across a full erasure, compared against a fixed expected result per fixture class (not one whole-database snapshot, since the erasure deliberately changes users, grants, accesses, registrations, notifications and jobs): historical terminal rows differ only in the note (`reconcileContacts` selects only pending rows and rows with live `contact_grants`, and `occurrence_add` rows never have grants); replacement rows A and B for the same subject each end with note `''` and every other column equal, the registration keeps `access_request_id = B`, and any live access under B ends through the existing erasure path with the same columns and audit and notice rows as the pre-T07 erasure; subject-pending rows get note `''`, `reason` unchanged and lifecycle columns equal to what the pre-T07 erasure fixture produces; requester-only rows, `contact` and `group_add` rows and unrelated rows are unchanged (their pending rows follow the existing lifecycle); the scrub adds no audit row and writes none of the registration access columns.
- **Hard constraints H1 to H8** (`deferred_trigger_hard_constraints`). H1: `add_request_completed` stays CONSTRAINT TRIGGER AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED FOR EACH ROW (baseline 3218) and `add_request_identity` stays BEFORE UPDATE FOR EACH ROW (baseline 3225), both enabled; a migration test compares `pg_trigger` (tgtype, tgdeferrable, tginitdeferred, tgenabled, tgfoid) before and after up, and after down then up. H2: `add_request_identity` is the only non-internal BEFORE UPDATE trigger on `contact_requests`, and the set of non-internal triggers is exactly `{add_request_identity, add_request_completed}`; a test fails if a trigger is added without that proof being updated. H3: every CHECK, FK and unique constraint on `contact_requests`, `contact_grants` and `registrations` has an identical `pg_get_constraintdef`; `''` satisfies `contact_requests_purpose_scope`. H4: `pg_get_functiondef` of every other function is unchanged; for the two replaced functions only the documented prefix differs. H5: the exception is evaluated per deferred event against the OLD and NEW captured when the event was queued, so an earlier INSERT, an earlier move into granted or a later change to the same row keeps its own event and check. **H6 (T07A-R13 form):** under `SET CONSTRAINTS add_request_completed IMMEDIATE`, every completion event is checked at the end of the statement that queued it, and the licensed blank's event is skipped; an ordinary event passes the unchanged checks only when its completion facts already exist at statement end, so the positive immediate-mode control is a no-op UPDATE of an already-valid granted request; a fresh or replacement occurrence-add completion keeps its dependency on deferral (its request moves to granted before the registration `access_request_id` is written), so in immediate mode it fails at that request Save with 23514; production write order is not changed and the exception is not broadened to make it pass; an invalid event that is not the licensed blank fails at the end of its own statement; under default deferral, queued events are checked at COMMIT or at a `SET CONSTRAINTS add_request_completed IMMEDIATE` issued while they are pending, and that statement or the COMMIT fails with a failing event's error; the design does not rely on the order in which Postgres fires queued events; `add_request_identity` is a BEFORE trigger and never deferred. H7: `ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT` discards the scrub's row versions and its queued events together, so a later forced failure rolls back every note. H8: no `DISABLE TRIGGER`, `session_replication_role`, `set_config` or `current_setting` in the migration or in Go, and no Go string literal outside `migrations/` contains DROP TRIGGER, ALTER TABLE ... DISABLE TRIGGER, or CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION for either function (source guard).
- **Tests required** (`tests_required`), by group. *fixtures*: valid rows for all six statuses that respect every CHECK, granted rows built through the real completion path or with a registration and audit snapshots that satisfy the provenance trigger, every scrub fixture with a nonempty note; keep ordinary expiry values away from transaction clocks except in the deliberate crossing variant. *replace_existing*: `TestAccountErasureLeavesContactRequestTextForT07` is replaced by `TestAccountErasureScrubsSubjectAgesNote`, which covers every class in preservation, asserts `reason` is unchanged on every row of every purpose and status, and asserts the scrub is limited to the erased subject's `occurrence_add` rows. *statement_level*: call the helper inside a test transaction after the tombstone and `tx.Delete`, then assert statement-level preservation. *historical_completion*: (a) replaced provenance, request A replaced by B on the same registration, both notes nonempty, erasure commits with 200, both notes `''`, everything else equal, registration keeps B, audit snapshots identical, B's live-access ending asserted against the pre-T07 erasure; (b) deleted provenance, a granted row whose registration was retired and deleted with full audit snapshots; (c) ended-access control, a granted row whose access only ended, with a no-op UPDATE in a separate committed transaction showing the original lookup still passes. *group_add_and_contact*: a granted `group_add` with an erased subject commits unchanged, direct SQL changing `reason` on that terminal row still fails 23514, a `contact` row is not scrubbed and the baseline's rule for its `reason` is documented as not prevented by the database. *direct_sql_rejections*: through the real triggers with no partial write, a blank for a living subject (23514), for a subject whose `users` row is missing (23514), a nonempty replacement for a deleted subject (23514), a blank combined with a change to status, `reason`, a decision, an actor, `completed_target_id`, `expires_at`, `completed_at`, a count or a nullable column (23514), a blank on a requester-only row with a living subject (23514), a stale-struct Save restoring the old note after a blank (23514, everything rolls back), and a no-op UPDATE on a granted row whose live state has moved (still fails at commit, as today). *deferred_cases* D1 to D6, each asserting which call returns the error, its SQLSTATE and that nothing persisted, and expecting no execution past a statement that fails immediately (a later statement returns 25P02 and does not count): **D1** forged granted INSERT then a licensed blank (default deferral: both succeed and COMMIT fails 23514 from the INSERT's event; immediate: the INSERT fails 23514 at statement end and the blank is never sent); **D2** pending-to-granted without completion then a licensed blank (same two outcomes); **D3** licensed blank of a granted row then any other change to that row (the second statement fails at once with 23514 from `add_request_identity` in both modes, because that trigger is never deferred; **rollback may discard the unprocessed blank event**, and the test asserts nothing about the blank's deferred event); **D4** licensed blank of a pending row then an unlicensed move to granted (COMMIT fails 23514 from the second statement's event, or the statement fails at once in immediate mode); **D5** the D1 INSERT or D2 UPDATE, then a licensed blank, then `SET CONSTRAINTS add_request_completed IMMEDIATE` (**the SET CONSTRAINTS statement fails with 23514 from the queued forged event without assuming the blank event runs first**, and nothing persists); **D6** a licensed blank alone commits in both modes with every other column unchanged, the one case that proves the completion exemption and may assert whether the blank's own event was processed. *existing_protections*: forged contact grants, invalid occurrence provenance, reuse of a repeat request and a missing retirement audit snapshot still fail. *old_writer_behavior*: every request write the current binary makes behaves as before. *rollback*: `TestAccountErasureRollsBackWhole` now covers `contact_requests`, and a forced late failure restores every note. Separately exercise `ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT` and prove it discards the scrub and its queued events together. *migration*: up, down, up; after down `pg_get_functiondef` equals the preceding definitions, after up H1 to H4 hold, and down does not restore scrubbed text. *guards*: the guard fixtures below.
- **Concurrency acceptance** (`concurrency_acceptance`). Evidence rule: each case records every session's SQLSTATE and final outcome plus real blocking evidence while the wait is in progress (`pg_blocking_pids(waiter)` contains the blocker's PID; `pg_locks` shows the waiter's ungranted lock, for the gate an advisory lock with the classid and objid of `contactLockID`, for a row normally a `transactionid` lock whose xid correlates to the blocker); waits are bounded with `lock_timeout` or `statement_timeout`. **Failure rule:** any failure of a concurrency case, local or CI, is recorded with its full output (SQLSTATE through `erasureTestSQLStates`, PIDs, which barrier fired and when) and diagnosed to a stated cause; the cause is fixed in a task range inside the lane manifest; the case then reruns at `-count=8` with every iteration passing; the cases also pass in CI on the exact head; repetition supplements the required blocking and outcome evidence and never replaces it; a passing rerun, a later green CI run or a load explanation without evidence never waives an unexplained failure, which blocks the merge until diagnosed and fixed. Pre-existing failures outside the manifest follow the host plan's rule. Cases: **CA-1** erasure against an occurrence-add decision on the same request, decision first (the erasure waits on the gate, then scrubs the granted row through the completion exception; registration access columns end through the normal erasure path, compared with the pre-T07 result). **CA-2** the same, erasure first (the decision waits, then is refused or finds the request ended by existing behavior; the note is already `''`). **CA-3** erasure against the expiry sweep in each order, with fixtures for the subject's pending T, terminal H and an unrelated expired X: erasure first (the sweep's try-lock returns false with no wait and changes nothing; X stays pending) and sweep first (the erasure waits on the gate with the sweep's PID blocking; the sweep expires X as usual); a crossing-expiry variant requires the status and `end_reason` pair dictated by policy at the clock of the transaction that actually finished T, bounds `completed_at` to that transaction, and checks the note and `reason` invariants. **CA-4** two erasures sharing a request (requester and subject), both orders (serialized on the gate, note `''`, `reason` unchanged). **CA-5** erasure against removal of the subject's registration, and separately against age rollover, each in both orders, with the subject's pending `occurrence_add` P: removal first (removal ends P with `end_reason` `removed`; the erasure then scrubs the ended row through the identity exception and the completion branch is not entered); erasure first then removal (removal finds no pending request and changes no request row); rollover first (P keeps or loses pending status as the pre-T07 code does, then the erasure ends and scrubs it); erasure first then rollover: **the discovery barrier** (`rolloverAges` discovers targets with a soft-delete-scoped Pluck, `age_rollover.go:30-33`, before any per-user transaction, so the test must make that discovery run and include the subject while the erasure holds the gate and before it commits, by holding the erasure at a barrier before commit, or by driving the per-user transaction directly with the subject's id after the same pre-commit discovery), the per-user transaction then waits on the gate in `lockTeenLifecycleUsers` (advisory evidence, the erasure's PID blocking), and after the erasure commits the soft-delete-scoped Find normally omits the tombstone, so **`user == nil or DeletedAt.Valid` is accepted and neither is required**, and the transaction returns nil without `rolloverTeen` or `reconcileContacts`; **a run whose discovery happens after the erasure commits may omit the subject entirely, shows no gate wait, and is not blocking evidence for that order.** **CA-6** a direct-SQL blank on a committed tombstone while another session holds the user row with NO KEY UPDATE, and separately FOR UPDATE (55P03 at once, with no ungranted lock by the blank). **CA-7** the same with KEY SHARE and separately SHARE (the blank succeeds and holds SHARE; in both variants session C ends before probe D starts. D then requests NO KEY UPDATE and blocks solely on B, with its transaction-ID wait correlated to B through `pg_blocking_pids`; it acquires the lock when B ends). **CA-8** a living-subject control, and a deletion another session has not committed (23514, no hang). **CA-9** inside an erasure, a concurrent plain unlocked reader of the request rows is neither blocked nor blocking and sees the old note until commit.
- **Guards R-1 to R-7** (`guards`). One parse-only source guard (for example `TestContactRequestTextChangesStayInTheirHelpers` in `guards_test.go`) with positive and negative self-test fixtures, scanning production Go directories with the exclusions `TestMailGoesThroughJobs` uses. *Protected columns:* `add_childcare_ages_note` (Go field `AddChildcareAgesNote`) may be written only on the struct being built in `apiCreateAddRequest` before its `tx.Create`, and by the single UPDATE literal in the helper, whose SET list must be exactly that column; `reason` (Go field `Reason`) may be written only in the two creation literals in `apiCreateContactRequest` and `apiCreateAddRequest`, with no update site of any kind until R4-T07B is designed, reviewed and merged. Allowed sites are bound by enclosing function name, not by line; column matching is exact and word-bounded (never `end_reason`, never `EndReason`). *Bound values:* an expression counts as a `ContactRequest` when the parser can bind it, in the same function, to `models.ContactRequest` or its pointer (var declarations, `:=` from a literal, parameters and results, slices, arrays and maps of either, index expressions, range variables, dereferences, an aliased or dot-imported `models`). *Four target kinds* (`contact_request_targets`): (a) explicit `Model(x)` with `x` a bound value or a `ContactRequest` literal; (b) explicit `Table(lit)` naming `contact_requests` (optionally `public.`, quoted or aliased); (c) the implicit destination of `Create`, `CreateInBatches`, `Save`, `FirstOrCreate` or `FirstOrInit`; (d) the implicit update model of `Updates` or `UpdateColumns` with no `Model` in the chain. R-3, R-4 and R-7 apply to all four kinds. A local `*gorm.DB` variable assigned exactly once from a chain counts as that chain's prefix. *Rejected forms:* **R-1** field write to `.Reason` or `.AddChildcareAgesNote` on a bound value outside the allowed sites. **R-2** a `ContactRequest` composite literal that sets either field outside the creation functions, assigning any `ContactRequest` literal over an existing bound value outside them, or passing a literal to `Save`. **R-3** `Update`/`UpdateColumn`/`Updates`/`UpdateColumns` on any target whose key or field is either column, or whose argument is not a literal the parser can read (a loaded value, a map variable, a function result, `gorm.Expr`). **R-4a** a `Clauses` argument of type `clause.OnConflict` on any target, including the implicit destination of `Create`, `CreateInBatches` or `Save` and including inside the creation functions, is rejected when `UpdateAll` is true (whatever `DoUpdates` says, because GORM then writes every non-key column including the two), when `DoUpdates` names either column, when `DoUpdates` is present but unreadable, or when the `OnConflict` value is not a composite literal; an `OnConflict` literal with `DoNothing: true` alone is allowed. **R-4b** (accepted, with parse-only limits, by the independent review recorded in section 15.3): on `FirstOrCreate` or `FirstOrInit` against a target, an `Assign`, `Attrs`, `Where` or inline condition that names either column through a map key, struct field or string condition, or any such argument that is not a literal the parser can read, is rejected everywhere including the creation functions; `FirstOrInit` makes the same in-memory writes and counts as an R-1 write. **R-4c** `Select` naming either column, `Select('*')` or a non-literal `Select` before `Save`/`Update*`; `Omit` is allowed. **R-5** raw SQL: any string literal (constant concatenations folded, whitespace normalized, case ignored) with `UPDATE [ONLY] [public.]contact_requests ... SET` naming either column, `INSERT INTO contact_requests ... ON CONFLICT ... DO UPDATE SET` naming either, or `MERGE INTO contact_requests` with an UPDATE SET naming either, except the one ages-note literal in the helper; there is no exception marker for `reason`. **R-6 (exact allowance):** the helper may be called exactly once in production code, as exactly one direct call in the `contactMutation` callback literal in `apiAdminDeleteUser`, as an expression statement, assignment, or the init or condition of an `if` that is itself a statement of that body, after the statement containing `tx.Delete(u)`, with that callback's own `*gorm.DB` parameter as the first argument; the callback literal is the one allowed closure; nested closures (including a `tx.Transaction` body), function values, `go`, `defer`, other callers and any use of the function as a value are rejected. **R-7** (accepted, with parse-only limits, by the same review): a GORM `Delete` on any Model/Table target or whose argument is a bound `ContactRequest` value or literal, and any string literal containing `DELETE FROM [ONLY] [public.]contact_requests` or `TRUNCATE` naming it, is rejected everywhere in production code; nothing in the current tree is rejected by it. *Persisting unchanged:* the whole-row `tx.Save` calls on loaded request structs stay valid, including through GORM's internal upsert fallback, provided no R-1, R-2 or R-4b write to the value appears in the function and no `Select`, `Clauses` or `Assign` is in the chain; the guard does not pretend those Saves leave the columns out, and a stale Save that changes the ages note or a terminal add row is rejected by the triggers (23514), while a stale Save that changes `reason` on a `contact` row or pending add row would not be. *Fixtures:* a negative set (the current tree passes unchanged; DTO and audit-meta `reason` keys, `EndReason` writes, other tables' `reason`, `Count`/`Find`/`First`/`Pluck` chains, the creation sites, `DoNothing` creates, other tables' find-or-create and `OnConflict` sites, and the single R-6 call) and a positive set that must each be rejected (every form above, each with both columns, aliased imports, the four target kinds, `UpdateAll` on `Create` and `Save` and inside the creation functions, unreadable conflict clauses, the find-or-create forms, `Select`, raw SQL, each R-6 violation and each R-7 form, including `tx.Delete(&r)`, `tx.Where(...).Delete(&models.ContactRequest{})`, `Table(...).Delete(nil)` and literal SQL DELETE).
- **Guard limits and database enforcement scope as limits** (`guards.limits`, `guards.database_enforcement_scope`). The guard is parse-only, like the other repo guards. It is not a type checker, not a call graph, not dynamic-SQL coverage and not a proof about external clients. It matches selectors on identifiers it can bind to `models.ContactRequest` in the same function, receiver chains it can read syntactically, composite literals and string literals with constant concatenation. It does not see SQL built from non-literal pieces or `fmt.Sprintf` with non-constant arguments, table or column names held in variables, reflection, generics, type aliases, embedded structs, values or `*gorm.DB` chains that arrive from another function's result, a parameter, a field or an interface, cross-package helpers, `gorm.Expr` or clause values built at run time beyond the listed forms, GORM scopes or callbacks that add clauses, or SQL sent around GORM (Fold sends none). Within those limits it rejects every form listed; beyond them only the database rules below apply. What the database enforces after R4-T07A: (a) the ages note on `group_add` and `occurrence_add` rows can change only through the licensed blank, at every status, because it is in `add_request_identity`'s list (i); on `contact` rows it is fixed at `''` by `contact_requests_purpose_scope`; (b) on terminal `group_add` and `occurrence_add` rows every column change except the licensed blank is rejected with 23514, so `reason` there cannot be changed by any client; (c) a purpose change is rejected on every row. **What it does not enforce:** `reason` is not in list (i), so the baseline (unchanged) permits `reason` changes on `contact` rows at any status and on pending `group_add` and `occurrence_add` rows, including together with the status change that ends them, and an upsert or `FirstOrCreate` `Assign` that updates such a row is an UPDATE the database treats the same way. The triggers are therefore not a general backstop against a `reason` scrub: preventing a new one depends on this source guard and on review, and it is not enforced against SQL the guard cannot see or any client outside Fold.
