Row deletion is not prevented by the triggers either; R-7 and review are its only protection here, apart from the two existing foreign keys, which protect referenced rows only (T07 FK bullet above), so source review remains necessary.
- **Behavior changes** (`behavior_changes`). Once the migration runs the two functions are not behaviorally identical to the baseline; these are the only differences. Changed: `add_request_identity` accepts one UPDATE shape it used to reject (an `occurrence_add` row whose ages note goes from nonempty to `''` while every other column stays equal, where the subject's `users` row is soft-deleted and can be share-locked without waiting); that same shape can now fail with 55P03 instead of 23514 when another transaction holds a conflicting lock on the tombstone (the baseline already rejected the statement, so only the SQLSTATE of a refusal changes); `add_request_completed` no longer runs the historical recheck for the deferred event of exactly that licensed UPDATE on a granted `occurrence_add` row. Unchanged: every INSERT event, every move into granted, every no-op UPDATE and every other UPDATE, with the same logic and SQLSTATEs; trigger definitions (timing, events, deferral, enablement, function called); every CHECK, FK, unique constraint, index and column of `contact_requests`, `contact_grants` and `registrations`; `contact_grant_successful_request`, `registration_access_provenance` and every other function; and what `reason` may change into, by purpose and status.
- **Objections accepted as limits** (`objections`). **D-2**: NOWAIT turns some contention into 55P03; narrowed (impossible only on the erasure's own lookup; outside it the lookup fails closed, and only for statements the baseline already rejected; no claim that nothing waits). **D-3**: once the subject is deleted, any transaction can issue a licensed blank, not only the erasure; accepted with limits (the license is the committed or own-transaction soft delete, rechecked under a lock, with no flag a caller can set; the guard limits Go changes to the helper and its single call site; the triggers enforce the ages-note boundary; arbitrary SQL clients are outside the threat model of every repo guard). **D-4**: accounts deleted before this ships keep their notes; outside R4-T07A's scope (R4-6 applies the same helper under the gate after taking the tombstone FOR UPDATE, needs its own R-6 allowance in R4-6's PR, and the migration runs no backfill). **D-9**: within parse-only limits the `reason` guard cannot see every possible writer, and the database does not protect `reason` on `contact` rows or pending add rows; accepted and stated above, because closing it structurally would change trigger rules for `reason`, which is R4-T07B's question and is not designed here.
- **Scale and UI** (`scale`, `ui`). Scale: run `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` on statement 1 with representative nonempty data (many subjects, a few rows per subject, an unrelated-person control), record the actual plan and rows touched and show the work is bounded by the subject's own rows; a sequential scan on a tiny table is acceptable if recorded; follow R4-2d1b's measurement pattern. UI: not affected (`addRequestJSON` does not expose the ages note and the admin history reads do not select it; this is source evidence). The reviewer still gives an explicit "not affected" for the UI concern.

Implementation docs (AGENTS data-model line and rules-table row, the `account_erasure.go` comment, present-tense permission text, STATUS done) belong to the R4-T07A implementation PR.

#### R4-T07A implementation record (Nov 5, 2026, R4-T07A implementation)

This is a dated note beneath the adopted specification. It changes none of the signed or adopted bullets above it, which keep the wording they were adopted with (the sentence in the opening paragraph that nothing is implemented, and the H8 sentence, describe the specification as adopted; the entries below say what the implementation does and where it reads a signed line more narrowly). It is a record, not a review signature. The implementation PR is not merged: no PR number and no merge commit is recorded here, and nothing in this note claims either.

- **What is implemented.** Migration `00004_ages_note_erasure_scrub.sql` replaces the two function bodies (an additive expand under DEC-138, no `-- contracts:` header; its Down restores the previous bodies and never restores scrubbed text). `eraseContactRequestTextIn` in `account_erasure.go` blanks the erased subject's `occurrence_add` ages notes in batches of at most `lookupChunk` ids, and is called once, in the `contactMutation` callback of `apiAdminDeleteUser`, after `eraseConsentEvidenceIn` and before `promoteOnDisable`. The source guard is `TestContactRequestTextChangesStayInTheirHelpers`, with `TestContactRequestTextGuardCatchesBypasses` and `TestContactRequestTextGuardReadsTheRealTree`, in `guards_test.go`. `reason` is untouched by all of it: the baseline trigger still lets `reason` change on `contact` rows and pending add rows, and the guard allows only the two creation literals to write it.
- **Accepted reconciliation items.** Each item below departs from, narrows or adds to reviewed or adopted text. It was proposed in `results/t07a-implementation-plan-rev4-20261105.json` (`specification_reconciliation_record.items`) and took effect for R4-T07A when the independent review named here accepted it by id; the plan was adopted with RC-1 to RC-5 and N1 to N4 binding (`results/t07a-plan-adoption-20261105.json`). No signed or dated line is edited by any of them. Grier's DEC-141 amendment, owner decisions 1 to 5, the G-7a and G-7b rejections and the G-7c and G-7d text are not touched.
  - **RC-1, the scoped reading of H8** (accepted by `results/t07a-implementation-plan-rev1-review-20261105.json`, `rc_dispositions` RC-1, with its T07A-Q1 wording correction; carried by `results/t07a-implementation-plan-rev4-review-20261105.json`). Read literally, H8's first sentence fails on the current tree, because `account_erasure.go` reads and sets `statement_timeout` with `current_setting` and `set_config` and `internal/testdb/testdb.go` holds the `session_replication_role` literal. H8 keeps its intent, that nothing bypasses the two triggers, and is read in five parts. (a) The migration file's text contains none of DISABLE TRIGGER, ENABLE REPLICA TRIGGER, ENABLE ALWAYS TRIGGER, `session_replication_role`, `set_config`, `current_setting`, DROP TRIGGER, CREATE TRIGGER or ALTER TABLE; `TestAgesNoteMigrationFileHasNoTriggerBypass` reads the file. (b) In production Go string literals and foldable constant concatenations, case-insensitive and whitespace-normalized, the guard rejects DISABLE TRIGGER, ENABLE REPLICA TRIGGER and ENABLE ALWAYS TRIGGER on any table, DROP TRIGGER, CREATE [OR REPLACE] FUNCTION naming `add_request_identity` or `add_request_completed`, and any literal containing `session_replication_role`. The one allowance is the exact literal `SET LOCAL session_replication_role = replica;` as the argument of the `b.WriteString` call in `resetStatements` in `internal/testdb/testdb.go`; it is not an allowance for that package, that file or any other literal. (c) `set_config` and `current_setting` stay allowed when no literal in the call names `session_replication_role`, so the `statement_timeout` uses stay. (d) On the real production tree, at every guard run, no non-test Go file outside `internal/testdb` imports `fold/internal/testdb` under any alias, as a blank import or as a dot import, which keeps the allowance out of the binary. (e) ENABLE REPLICA TRIGGER and ENABLE ALWAYS TRIGGER are added to the banned list: REPLICA stops ordinary origin-mode firing and ALWAYS fires in either mode, and both are banned to keep the reviewed `tgenabled = 'O'` configuration. No test disables a trigger. The guard stays parse-only (SQL built from non-literal pieces, names held in variables, reflection and SQL sent around GORM stay outside it).
  - **RC-2, the batched statement 3** (accepted by `results/t07a-implementation-plan-rev1-review-20261105.json`, RC-2; carried by the rev4 review). One UPDATE over all ids binds `len(ids) + 1` parameters, and pgconn refuses more than 65,535 parameters, which len(ids) + 1 exceeds for 65,535 or more selected rows and would roll the whole erasure back. Statement 1 still locks every id in one ordered `FOR UPDATE` selection; statement 3 is the same literal run once per contiguous batch of at most `lookupChunk` (5,000) ids in ascending order, each batch's `RowsAffected` must equal its length, the total must equal `len(ids)`, and every batch is part of the caller's transaction. For 5,000 rows or fewer the statement sequence is the signed one. Nothing is claimed about the order PostgreSQL visits rows inside one UPDATE.
  - **RC-3, the later-column type limit** (accepted by `results/t07a-implementation-plan-rev1-review-20261105.json`, RC-3; carried by the rev4 review). The signed claim that a column added later is covered automatically holds when the column's type has a default equality operator, as every current type does. A later column of a type without one (for example `json`, `point` or `xml`) would make the whole-row comparison raise instead of pass, which fails closed but would stop erasures, so any migration that adds such a column to `contact_requests` must re-review both functions. The baseline's own `NEW IS DISTINCT FROM OLD` comparison has the same property. `TestAgesNoteTriggerCoversAddedColumns` is a witness for one `int` column in a session that compiled both functions before the ALTER TABLE, not a proof for every type.
  - **RC-4, the restored scale criterion** (accepted by `results/t07a-implementation-plan-rev4-review-20261105.json`, `rc_dispositions` RC-4). The signed criterion stands: the work is bounded by the subject's own rows. The tiny-table sequential-scan exception applies to ordinary small fixtures, never to the representative scale fixture. At the representative size every `contact_requests` scan feeding the SELECT must restrict access to the subject through an Index Cond (or a subject Recheck Cond fed by a Bitmap Index Scan), and each UPDATE batch must restrict access to exactly its captured id set or to the subject; a Filter alone does not qualify. Displayed parameters are resolved against the captured SQL and Vars of that execution, and ids are compared as sets. Proof needs supported, non-lossy scan shapes with `loops=1` and complete counters; examined rows are actual rows plus rows removed by filter and by index recheck, and bitmap-index entries are counted apart from heap candidates. The bound is S_U (all of the subject's `contact_requests` rows before erasure, across purposes and statuses) for the SELECT and subject-based UPDATE access, and the batch length for exact-id UPDATE access. Unknown nodes, loops other than one, lossy scans, incomplete counters or unresolved bindings give `bounded=false` with a not-proven reason and no invented total; a sequential scan, a nonselective index scan or a count above the bound gives `bounded=false` for its actual reason. A false verdict is an open database finding that blocks merge until it is dispositioned, and no index, query or schema change follows automatically. The measurement is visible candidate access on a fresh fixture, not every physical index or heap operation. The separate 70,001-row bind-limit test is not judged by this rule; it is opt-in (`FOLD_ERASURE_SCALE=1`) and needs local evidence, not CI.
  - **RC-5, Lane T's source-change review and migration gate additions** (accepted by `results/t07a-implementation-plan-rev4-review-20261105.json`, RC-5). These bind the R4-T07A lane's process and add review scope only. Dependency discovery D1 to D5 runs at every transition from the reviewed base to a newer master and covers added, modified, renamed, copied and deleted production Go files, with the request, grant, consent, user-lock, gate, advisory-lock and traced-call census rerun and old and new dependencies unioned before any commit is classified. An expected sensitive commit needs an independent delta recheck over that transition's exact file list before the reviewed base moves; an unexpected one stops the lane for design review. The initial post-consent recheck runs on fetched immutable git objects before a base is assigned or a worktree exists. Lead-only setup is exempt from task ranges until it completes, renumbering runs only in an explicitly manifested range, the final merge run refreshes the inherited O-15 sources as its own step with per-source coverage labels, and the final gate keeps exact-head equality and stop-on-change. None of it grants write permission outside the manifest, relaxes the existing gates, requires a repository setting or revives a base-binding requirement.
- **Gate takers (T07A-P2).** The signed design's list of exclusive contact-gate takers was incomplete; the conclusion stands because every holder of a `contact_requests` row lock takes the exclusive gate. The rule is every `contactMutation`, `contactOperation` and direct `lockContacts` caller. At `87c6934` (line numbers are that commit's): direct `lockContacts`: `mail_jobs.go` 261, 301, 515, 594; `api_teen_conversion.go` 89; `api_registrations.go` 1059, 1463, 1651; `api_series_registrations.go` 212, 486; `api_invitations.go` 319; `api_occurrence_mutations.go` 260, 389, 635; `api_guest.go` 687; `api_auth.go` 456; `account_security.go` 20, 59, 88; `api_contact.go` 616; `api_admin_household.go` 50; `api_admin.go` 539. `contactMutation`: `api_teen_guardians.go` 226; `api_registrations.go` 1339; `api_teen_consent.go` 256; `api_events.go` 924, 1164, 1252, 1356; `api_guest.go` 720; `api_event_access.go` 454, 647; `api_guardian_email.go` 141; `api_groups.go` 400, 743, 1043; `api_account_requests.go` 464; `api_departure.go` 790; `api_admin.go` 128, 738, 950, 1077. `contactOperation` (exclusive): `api_contact.go` 267, 375, 451, 526, 765, 987. Shared (`contactRead`): `api_contact.go` 333, 490, 877. Try-lock: `contact_lifecycle.go` 405. The source is `r4-t07a-recheck-completion-20261105.json` and the plan's `post_consent_recheck.lock_inventory`; it is a source reading, not a type-checked call graph, and it does not claim global deadlock freedom.
- **Why the two exceptions differ (T07A-P4).** The consent exception of `00003` checks deletion with an unlocked `EXISTS`; this one uses `FOR SHARE OF u NOWAIT`. G-7c's signed predicate includes the share lock so that a blank issued outside the erasure cannot race a later clearing of `deleted_at`; `00003`'s signed shape relies on `setConsent`'s users-row lock. Each is kept as signed, and the migration header says so.
- **Carry-forward for R4-6 (T07A-P13).** Outside the erasure, a licensed blank fails with SQLSTATE 55P03 if another transaction holds the tombstone's users row with `NO KEY UPDATE` or `FOR UPDATE`. Unscoped takers exist (`api_teen_conversion.go` 61, `api_household_conversion.go` 506, `api_guest.go` 315, at `87c6934`). Inside the erasure the user row is held `FOR UPDATE` from `deletionHostPlan` to commit, so the trigger's share lock cannot wait there. R4-6's legacy sweep must take the tombstone `FOR UPDATE` under the gate before it calls the helper, as D-4 says, and needs its own R-6 allowance.
- **G-7d condition (4) is closed (15.3 (d)(4)).** The recheck record is `results/r4-t07a-recheck-completion-20261105.json` (gpt-6.1-sol through Codex in T3 Code, Nov 5, 2026 17:33 UTC), verdict `proof holds` for exactly `e806cf3` to `87c6934`. It is a source-only review: it is not a runtime, scale, CI, global-deadlock or merge verdict. Condition (5) stays open until merge (15.3 (d)(5)).
- **What this note does not do.** Reason review is not implemented: `reason` is untouched on every row, R4-T07B stays held (owner decisions 6 and 7, section 15.4), M7.4 is not complete, and C4 is not met. Nothing here says the implementation passed tests, scale or reviews; those are recorded with the PR and at merge.

#### R4-T07B (reason): dormant receipt schema staged; review behavior pending

G-7a and G-7b stay rejected (predicate FALSE) for every automated scrub of `reason`; splitting T07 does not resolve them, and their rejection is not a final retention approval. Writers accept any trimmed text up to 1000 characters, and row facts say nothing about what the text says, so classifying it by name or substring is forbidden. Asked what to do with `reason`, Grier has since chosen (Nov 5, 2026): "Add an admin review step during deletion to decide whether to blank each reason". The review step is new product surface (DEC-145); the approved revision 8 design and its independent review are recorded in section 15.4's staging update. The review behavior remains unimplemented. The choice is recorded in this file only; no PLAN decision row records it. Until the approved R4-T07B review behavior is implemented, `reason` stays as written on every row (staged retention, not a final disposition), and R4-T07A's guard keeps the two creation literals as the only `reason` writers. Any R4-T07B implementation changes that guard and its fixtures in the same PR. R4-T07A alone does not satisfy M7.4's contact-request completion condition: `reason` is closed only by a merged R4-T07B or by an authorized PLAN change recording Grier's dated decision. Nov 5, 2026: the owner also answered how to handle the reasons of accounts deleted before the review step exists ("Create a one-time admin review list for unreviewed deleted accounts (Recommended)"); both answers are recorded in full, as owner decisions 6 and 7, in 15.4. Revision 3's returned review below is historical. T07B-1a stages only the dormant receipt table (T39); it changes no reason, erasure behavior or completion gate. T07B-2a stages migration `00007` (section 15.4): a database-only licence to blank a `reason` on a non-contact, non-pending request with a soft-deleted party, with no caller, so `reason` is still untouched on every row. T07B-1b and T07B-1c (section 15.9) are committed locally and use the T39 receipt table for lost-reply settlement of an account deletion; they read and write no `reason` and change no erasure disposition, so C4 is still not met.

### T08 `event_hosts` (baseline 703; `models.go:340`)
- **FK**: `user_id`→users no action (3363); `event_id`→events (3355); `added_by` none. **Personal**: ids only.
- **Disp.**: DEL the erased person's host rows after the replacement guard (owner decision 3). A co-host leaves silently; a **sole** host requires an admin-chosen replacement first. Rows the erased person added for others → RET (`added_by` attribution). **Implemented by R4-H**: the discovery endpoint, the guard and the picker.
- **Evidence** (before R4-H, now fixed): `apiRemoveEventHost` counted host rows without checking `users.deleted_at`; `hostIDs` returned tombstone ids; the delete handler never deleted host rows. F-2. R4-H: `policy.EventKeepsHost` ignores deleted accounts, `hostIDs` joins live users, `apiAdminDeleteUser` hands hosting off (`deletionHostPlan`, `handOffHosting` in `api_host_handoff.go`), and adding a host removes the tombstone rows of that event, each with an audit row (`reason` `deleted_host_cleanup`). Tests: `api_host_handoff_test.go`. Host rows on a deleted event are removed with the account's other rows, without a lock on that event.

- **R4-6b legacy cleanup, staged locally (Nov 6, 2026).** L12 deletes every host row of the exact tombstone and writes one system `event.host_removed` per deleted row, with `userId` and `reason: deleted_host_cleanup`, no actor or IP. It chooses no successor and changes no event. This is behavior-neutral because `hostIDs` and `policy.EventKeepsHost` already ignore tombstones. The host lock-order review is in `account_erasure_tombstones.go` and `r46b1-recheck.json`; it is not a claim of global deadlock freedom. See 12.1.

### T12 `guardian_links` (baseline 900; `models.go:797`)
- **FK**: `guardian_user_id`→users (3459), `teen_user_id`→users (3475), `person_id`→household_people (3467), `confirmed_by`→users SET NULL (3451; never fires). Exactly one of `teen_user_id`/`person_id` (baseline 911).
- **Personal**: `check_note`. Two producers write it: an admin's typed note when deciding a link (required to confirm, `api_errors.go:208`; written `api_admin.go:386-393`, mirrored in audit `checkNote`, `api_admin.go:399`), and a fixed system sentence when a teen turns 18 and pending links are rejected (`age_rollover.go:154`; "This account is now an adult account. Guardian confirmation is no longer needed."). Only the first kind is person-typed.
- **Writers (all four, traced)**: create pending `api_profile.go:963` (`createGuardianLink`, household person link) and `api_teen_guardians.go:64` (`requestTeenGuardian`, teen link); status change `api_admin.go:388` (admin confirm/reject), `api_profile.go:980-981` (`revokeGuardianLinks`, called only from `api_profile.go:1399` person kind change and `:1473` person removal) and `age_rollover.go:154` (reject pending at 18). **Readers**: `contact_lifecycle.go:230,235`, `api_admin.go:205-253`, `api_teen_consent.go:180-182`, `api_teen_conversion.go:159`, `age_rollover.go:69,205-207`, `registration_subjects.go`.
- **Disp.**: MIXED (section 5): live links of an erased guardian, teen, or converted-duplicate person → `revoked` with an audit row (`GuardianLinkRevoked`, meta `reason: "account_deleted"`); decision history and the admin-typed `check_note` are **proposed RET as essential safeguarding evidence, pending the safeguarding reviewer's signature** (O-4); the system sentence needs no scrub. No hard delete.
- **Converted-duplicate links (O-14, traced and closed)**: neither `api_household_conversion.go` nor `api_teen_conversion.go` writes `guardian_links` (their only references are reads at `api_household_conversion.go:97-104,461` and `api_teen_conversion.go:159`), and a converted person cannot be edited or removed afterwards (`errConversionUnavailable`, `api_profile.go:1344-1345,1456-1457`), so `revokeGuardianLinks` never runs on it. A duplicate's `person_id` links therefore stay `pending`/`confirmed` after conversion and R4-3 must select them.
- **Staged teen conversion (O-18)**: an approved teen conversion creates the teen account (`pending_consent`) and one **pending `teen_user_id` link per confirmed person-side guardian** (`api_household_conversion.go:724-736`, `requestTeenGuardian` with `source` `household_conversion`, the approving admin's `ClientIP`), while the person's own `person_id` links are left exactly as they were. Until completion the person is frozen against household edits and removal (`errConversionConsent`, `api_profile.go:1357,1464`; `approvedConversionForPerson`, `api_teen_conversion.go:46-50`). So for a staged erased teen **both** link kinds are live: the teen-side links (M17) and the person-side links (selector below). Completion needs the teen's account consent and at least one confirmed guardian who is also confirmed on the person (`api_teen_conversion.go:156-165`); erasure of the teen ends that path (`changeTeenConsent` refuses a soft-deleted teen, `api_teen_consent.go:87`).
- **Selector (R4-3)**: `guardian_links.person_id IN PERS` (3.1, completed **and** staged) and `teen_user_id = erased`, plus `guardian_user_id = erased`. Sharing a household alone does not select a surviving person’s link: `PERS` is never widened by household. A link to a surviving person is still selected when its `guardian_user_id` or `teen_user_id` is the erased account. Status handling: revoke `pending` and `confirmed`; leave `rejected` and `revoked` rows as the history they are; an `OnConflict DoNothing` duplicate teen link (`api_teen_guardians.go:64`) leaves one live row per pair by the unique index `guardian_links_live_teen_guardian` (baseline 2581), so there is no duplicate-live case to handle for teen links; person links have no such index, so revoke every live row the selector returns.
- **Slice**: **R4-3**.
- **Implemented by R4-3b-2** (committed locally on the integration branch, staged: not pushed, not merged; `eraseHouseholdRecordsIn` steps 4 and 5 in `account_erasure_households.go`; independent review `r43b2-recheck.json` and `r43b2-integrated-review.json`: pass, no open findings; the landing record is 15.6). The selector is the one above: `status IN ('pending','confirmed')` and (`teen_user_id = U` or `guardian_user_id = U` or `person_id IN PERS`). The matching rows are locked `FOR UPDATE` in ascending id with the household and the user row already held (for the distinct `decideGuardianLink` branch orders, see 9.6.1), then set to `revoked` with `decided_at`, one `GuardianLinkRevoked` audit row each (meta `reason: account_deleted`, `guardianUserId` and the `personId` or `teenUserId`: ids only), and the pending `HouseholdLinkPending` waiting task is resolved `withdrawn`. `check_note`, `confirmed_by`, rejected and revoked rows and sibling links outside that explicit selector are not touched (O-4). Erasing a guardian revokes their links to surviving people without anonymizing those people or their household. So **O-14 and O-18 are implemented for the link rows**, not closed as gates; tests: `TestErasureHouseholdStagedTeen`, `TestErasureHouseholdCompletedConversion`, `TestErasureHouseholdGuardianLinksOfAnErasedGuardian`.

### T13 `guest_signups` (baseline 939; `models.go:505`)
- **FK**: `occurrence_id`→event_occurrences (3403). **Personal**: `name`, `email`, `ip`, `timezone`, `token_hash`. **Writers**: `api_guest.go` (create at `:229`, mail job at `:244`); deleted only when an event is deleted (`api_events.go:1204`); **no TTL prune**, so unconfirmed and confirmed rows persist.
- **Disp.**: address-owned. Pending (unconfirmed) rows with `email` = captured address → DEL (the link must die). Confirmed rows → ANON: `name`, `email` (placeholder, O-10), `ip`, `timezone`, and `token_hash` (overwritten with a unique non-secret marker, because `idx_guest_signups_token_hash` is unique, baseline 2791); keep `occurrence_id`, `create_account`, `confirmed_at`, `expires_at`. **Slice**: R4-2a.
- **Implemented by R4-2a** (merged in PR #275; `eraseDependentRecordsIn`, `apiConfirmGuestRegistration`): a signup at the held address that was never confirmed is deleted; a confirmed one is anonymized (placeholder address `deleted-guest_signups-<id>@deleted.invalid`, marker token `erased-guest_signups-<id>`, name, `ip` and `timezone` cleared) and keeps its occurrence, `create_account`, `confirmed_at` and `expires_at`. These statements run after the user and occurrence locks, in the order guest redemption holds them. Redemption now locks the guest's own user row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` and reads it again before it locks the occurrence, so its order is guest row, then occurrence, then signup (L3), the holder-first order of the other registration writers; an erased or re-addressed guest finds the link invalid. Tests: `TestAccountErasureGuestSignupsAndOutbox`, `TestGuestRedemptionRacingErasure` (production schedules), `TestGuestRedemptionAfterErasureOfConfirmedSignup`, `TestGuestRedemptionAfterErasureOfRotatedSignup`, `TestQueuedDependentMailForErasedAccountSendsNothing` (guest verification mail). The one cycle redemption can still close, with an admin erasure, is D4 (9.5).

### T14 `household_accounts` (baseline 978; `models.go:751`)
- **FK**: `household_id` (3483), `user_id`→users no action (3491); unique `idx_household_accounts_user_id` (2805). **Disp.**: DEL the erased account's own seat row (R4-3). Other seats unchanged.
- **Implemented by R4-3b-2** (staged, see 15.6): step 1 of `eraseHouseholdRecordsIn` reads the households the account's seat names (it refuses to continue if one is not in the set `lockErasureHouseholdsIn` locked), then runs `DELETE FROM household_accounts WHERE user_id = U`. No other seat is written. The household step runs after `eraseContactRequestTextIn` (the account is already soft-deleted) and before `promoteOnDisable`.

### T15 `household_people` (baseline 1012; `models.go:765`)
- **FK**: `created_by`→users (3507), `household_id` (3515), `converted_user_id`→users SET NULL (3499; never fires).
- **Personal**: `first_name`, `last_name` (NOT NULL, `''` allowed), `birth_date`, `care_notes`, `photo_consent`. A soft-deleted row keeps all of it today (`models.go:765`).
- **Two identity states of the same individual, one set.** `PERS` (3.1) is the person row(s) that *are* the erased user:
  1. **Completed conversion**: `converted_user_id = U`. Conversion moved the person's registrations to the account (`api_household_conversion.go:626-632`), so no registration references the duplicate afterwards.
  2. **Approved but not completed teen conversion** (`converted_user_id` IS NULL). `approveHouseholdConversion` creates the teen `User` (`pending_consent`), records `account_requests.approved_user_id = U` (`api_household_conversion.go:716-721`) and returns **without** touching the person (`:738`); the person row, its care notes, its birth date and **its registrations** stay where they were and are frozen against household edit and removal (`errConversionConsent`, `api_profile.go:1357,1464`). Only `completeTeenConversionIfReady` later sets `converted_user_id` and moves registrations (`api_teen_conversion.go:128-196` → `transferHouseholdConversion`). The code itself reconstructs this link from the request, never from the user: `stagedTeenConversionIn` (`api_teen_conversion.go:21-44`: `source = household_conversion`, `status = approved`, `approved_user_id = U`, then `household_person_id`). Admin delete reaches a `pending_consent` teen (`adminTargetUser` filters nothing by status, `api_admin.go:578-585`; `apiAdminDeleteUser`, `:828-884`), so this state is reachable and is **not** covered by a `converted_user_id`-only selector.
- **Exact selector.** `household_people.id` in `PERS`, read **unscoped** (soft-deleted rows included), where `PERS` is the union in 3.1: `converted_user_id = U` **or** the `household_person_id` of the one request with `source = 'household_conversion'`, `status = 'approved'`, `approved_user_id = U`. Two partial unique indexes (baseline 2588-2596) allow at most one such request per person and per user, so the staged link names at most one person. If the union yields more than one distinct person, or a person whose `household_id` differs from the request's person, R4-3 **aborts** and reports an integrity error; it never guesses. The selector never uses household membership, `kind`, birth date, surname or a request's `status` other than `approved`: a sibling or child of the erased person is not in `PERS`.
- **Disp.** (owner decision 2, applied to both states): ANON `first_name`, `last_name` (`''` is allowed), `birth_date` (NULL), `care_notes`, `photo_consent`; soft-delete it; **keep `converted_user_id` exactly as it is** (set stays set, NULL stays NULL). Do not write `converted_user_id` on a staged person: that would record a completion that never happened. Everything else in the household is another person's record: RET untouched; `created_by` = erased → attribution.
- **Dependents of a staged person** (none exist for a completed duplicate):
  - `registrations.household_person_id IN PERS`: future `going`/`maybe`/`waitlisted` rows are cancelled through the same path as removing a household person (`reconcileHouseholdPerson(deleting=true)`, `registration_subjects.go:171-233`: audit `registration.changed`, notice to the living holder, waitlist promotion) **after** the person is anonymized, so no stored notice carries the name; then every such row is ANON like M5 (`manage_token_hash`, `childcare_ages_note`, `removal_note`). Keep `status`, counts, ids, `registered_by` (the living holder: attribution) and every `access_*` column. Do **not** set `user_id` on any of them: that is what completion would do, and it is the revival this rule prevents. A soft-deleted person is already refused for new registration (`HouseholdRegistrationProblem`, `internal/policy`: `person.Deleted || person.Converted`).
  - `guardian_links.person_id IN PERS` and the staged teen-side links: T12.
  - The approved request, its accepted invitation and the audit rows `account.approved`, `invitation.accepted` (`meta.personId`) and `guardian_link.requested` (`source = household_conversion`) keep their ids and `meta_json`; their personal fields follow T01, T17 and 6.2. `teen_consents` for the teen are immutable and retained (T36). There is no `household.person_converted` row for a staged person (it is written only at completion, `api_household_conversion.go:646`).
- **Non-revival.** After the scrub no code path may complete the staged conversion: `changeTeenConsent` refuses a soft-deleted teen (`api_teen_consent.go:87`), `completeTeenConversionIfReady` returns `errConversionPerson` for a deleted person (`api_teen_conversion.go:138`) and does nothing for a deleted user (`:143`), and `stagedTeenConversionIn` still finds the approved request but the person is soft-deleted. R4-3 asserts this with a test rather than relying on the reading.
- **Why a completed duplicate's link must stay**: converted people are hidden from every reader by `converted_user_id IS NULL`: `registration_subjects.go:83,507`, `api_profile.go:875`, `api_registrations.go:1134`, `age_rollover.go:64,194`, `api_teen_conversion.go:37,137`, `api_household_conversion.go:687`. Nulling or hard-deleting would revive the duplicate.
- **Slice**: **R4-3**. Owner decision 2. Unrelated members and shared household data are not touched. R4-3 tests: a completed adult conversion; a staged `pending_consent` teen erased before consent; a staged teen erased after consent but before a guardian confirmed; a sibling in the same household untouched; the staged teen's registrations cancelled without gaining a `user_id`; no completion possible afterwards, and the daily age rollover (which reads users and people through the soft-delete scope, `age_rollover.go:29-31,64`) neither errors nor revives the pair.
- **Implemented by R4-3b-2** (staged, see 15.6; reviews `r43b2-recheck.json` and `r43b2-integrated-review.json`, pass). *Integrity first.* `validatePersIn` runs right after `captureErasureSetsIn`, before any household is locked or any row is written, and reads both arms unscoped. It aborts the whole erasure (`errErasureIntegrity`, the existing 500, nothing written) when (1) the distinct union of the completed and staged arms holds more than one person; (2) a staged person has no row or already points at another account; (3) **any person of the set is named by an approved `household_conversion` request whose `approved_user_id` is another account** (read from the person's side, so it is caught whether or not U has a request of its own; this third rule came from review finding R43B2-REV-02 and is stricter than the sentence above, in the direction it asks for: abort, never widen); or (4) the invitation the staged request names belongs to a person of a different household. A pending, rejected or expired request by another account is not a contradiction. *Writes*, once the households are locked: the seat (T14); `UPDATE household_people SET first_name = '', last_name = '', birth_date = NULL, care_notes = '', photo_consent = false, deleted_at = COALESCE(deleted_at, now()) WHERE id IN PERS`, unscoped, and **no write to `converted_user_id`** (a completed duplicate stays set, a staged person stays NULL). *Non-revival.* `completeTeenConversionIfReady` (`api_teen_conversion.go`) now returns nil at once for a soft-deleted user, before it looks at the person, so a direct call on an erased pair no longer reaches `errConversionPerson`; the other callers already skipped or refused (`changeTeenConsent` returns `errConsentTeen`, admin Enable answers 4xx, the rollover reads live users only). It changes nothing a caller could reach. *Dependents of a staged person* are T28's. Tests: `TestErasureHouseholdCompletedConversion`, `TestErasureHouseholdStagedTeen`, `TestErasureHouseholdStagedTeenBeforeConsent`, `TestErasurePersIntegrityAborts` (the staged/staged case drops the partial unique index in the scratch database and recreates it in cleanup), `TestValidatePersAcceptsOneIndividual`, `TestValidatePersRejectsAnotherAccountsApprovedRequest`, `TestHouseholdSeveralPersPeopleStopBeforeAnyLock`. The scale fixture's PERS is 1: any U with two different people is now an integrity abort, so a PERS of 2 cannot exist in a valid fixture.

### T16 `households` (baseline 1053; `models.go:740`)
- **FK**: `created_by`→users (3523); only `household_accounts` and `household_people` reference the table (baseline 3483, 3515); no unique index or CHECK on `name`, so a fixed neutral value is legal (`name text NOT NULL`, `address` defaults to `''`, baseline 1053-1060). **Personal**: `name` (family name) and `address` (home address), both editable text (`apiUpdateMyHousehold`, `api_profile.go:1031-1064`; ≤100 and ≤500 characters).
- **Who a household belongs to (traced).** A household is made on first use **for one account holder**, named after that holder's surname ("Smith household", "My household" when nameless, `defaultHouseholdName`, `api_profile.go:710-738`) with that one seat and **no person row** (`ensureHousehold`, `:739-741`); an admin can make the same one-holder household with an admin-typed name and address (`api_admin_household.go:158-165`). Nothing requires a second person. Conversion adds the new account's seat to the **existing** household of the person's family (`api_household_conversion.go:642`), and other adults, teens and children join the same household through `household_accounts` and `household_people`. So the same row is sometimes one person's home and sometimes a family's.
- **Two cases, decided by a predicate and not by a guess.** Under the household lock (`lockHousehold`, `registration_subjects.go:89`, the `FOR UPDATE` on the household row that person add/update/remove, household edit and conversion take: `api_profile.go:1068,1205,1338,1450`, `api_household_conversion.go:273,433,513,678`; R4-3 re-greps for any seat or person writer that does not take it; the lock alone is **not** enough, because before R4-3a, self-service writers authorized before their transaction without revalidating the account under it: 9.6, F-10), after the erased account's seat is deleted (T14) and `PERS` is anonymized (T15), the household is **sole-subject** exactly when **both** hold: (S1) **no** `household_accounts` row remains for it, of any user, any status, soft-deleted or not (a seat held by an earlier tombstone or a disabled account is another account's seat); and (S2) **every** `household_people` row of it, read unscoped (soft-deleted included), is in `PERS`. Everything else is **shared or ambiguous**. A household with an empty seat list but a surviving person (a child of the erased adult), a soft-deleted other person, or a legacy tombstone seat is therefore **not** sole-subject: "no account holder" alone is not the test, and no surname or free-text comparison is ever used.
- **Disp.**
  - *Sole-subject*: ANON `name` to the fixed neutral value **`Removed household`** (required, non-empty; nobody sees it because no account or person remains) and `address` to `''`. Keep `id`, `created_by` (attribution; it may be an admin, whose id is not erased data), timestamps and the audit rows that target the id (ids only). This includes the household whose only person row is the erased individual's own anonymized duplicate, and the one-holder household with no person rows.
  - *Shared or ambiguous*: RET untouched. The name and address describe other living people's home (or an unprovable mix); the erased person's own name appears in it only as a family surname the others use. The erasure result reports a **count** of retained households (no names), and an admin can see the household still lists its other members.
  - The two selectors are exact and side-effect free; if the predicate cannot be evaluated under the lock (the lock fails), R4-3 aborts the erasure rather than falling back to retention or scrubbing.
- **Preloaded self-service writers can undo the scrub (R40-SEC-08, 9.6, F-10).** Four writers of the erased person's own
  household authorize from the request's earlier user load, then take only `lockHousehold`, and write without checking
  that the account is still active, the session still current or the seat still theirs. Erasure-first ordering: the
  request is past authorization and waits on the household lock; erasure deletes the seat, anonymizes the sole-subject
  household and commits; the request then writes its submitted `name` and `address` (or a person row) onto the household,
  which for a shared household is another family's record and for a sole-subject one restores exactly what the scrub
  removed. First use has the same gap: `ensureHousehold` creates a `<surname> household` row and a seat from the
  preloaded user, and the foreign keys only test that the user row exists. **R4-3 owns the closure; R4-3 does not start
  its household statements until 9.6's requirements and barrier tests are in its plan.**
- **Gate for the ambiguous class (O-9).** Where the only other holder is a legacy tombstone (an account erased by the old handler never lost its seat) or the only other person is soft-deleted, ownership of the name and address between those records cannot be decided from source. This draft **retains** them and counts them; the privacy reviewer must sign that retention (O-9) or name another rule. R4-6's legacy sweep re-runs the same predicate after it has cleaned legacy seats, so a household that becomes sole-subject then is scrubbed.
- **Slice**: **R4-3**. R4-3 tests: (a) a one-holder household with no people; (b) holder plus the same individual's anonymized duplicate only; (c) holder plus another adult's seat; (d) holder plus a surviving child; (e) holder plus a soft-deleted other person; (f) holder plus a legacy tombstone seat; (g) a staged teen erased in a parent's household (shared); (h) a barrier test where another request adds a person or seat to the household while the erasure waits on `lockHousehold`, proving the predicate is read after the lock; (i) the preloaded-request barriers of 9.6, erasure-first and writer-first, for the household update, the person writers and first-use creation.
- **Implemented by R4-3b-1 and R4-3b-2** (staged, see 15.6; reviews `r43b1-recheck.json`, `r43b1-integrated-review.json`, `r43b2-recheck.json`, `r43b2-integrated-review.json`: pass). *Locks (R4-3b-1).* `lockErasureHouseholdsIn` takes, after the user row lock and `captureErasureSetsIn` and before the first write, each household of the account's seat and of PERS (unscoped) with `SELECT id FROM households WHERE id = ? FOR UPDATE NOWAIT` in ascending id; a 55P03 rolls the whole erasure back and `apiAdminDeleteUser` runs it again (9.6). *Decision (R4-3b-2).* `householdErasedOwnerIn(tx, h, s, seatHouseholds)` is evaluated for every locked household, after the seat is deleted and PERS anonymized, reading everything unscoped: S1 (no `household_accounts` row of any user, linked user soft-deleted or not) else retain; then, for the subject, every person of `h` is in PERS(U), or `h` has no person and is in U's own seat set; otherwise, only for an `h` with at least one person, exactly one soft-deleted account X owns every person of `h` (both PERS arms), no person has two owners and X's PERS is at most one distinct person. A sole-subject household is set to `name = 'Removed household'`, `address = ''` (`updated_at` moves; id, `created_by` and audit untouched); one already scrubbed is counted and not rewritten; every other locked household is retained and counted (`householdsKept`). No marker is written, so every later erasure that locks `h` asks again (O-9). *Result.* `DELETE /api/v1/admin/users/:id` answers `{"erasure": {householdsKept, householdsScrubbed, peopleAnonymized, guardianLinksRevoked}}` (integers, `account_erasure_report.go`; `ErasureReport` in `web/src/types.ts`, pinned by `TestWebTypesMatchGoJSON`); no name, id or address. The delete panel does not display it yet. *Tests.* Fixtures (a) to (h): `TestErasureHouseholdSoleSubjectFixtures` (a to f), `TestErasureHouseholdFixtureGStagedTeenInAParentsHousehold`, `TestErasureHouseholdFixtureHPredicateIsReadAfterTheLock`; the two-seat and two-erased cases `TestErasureHouseholdTwoSeatsEitherOrder`, `TestErasureHouseholdTwoErasedSubjectsStayShared`, `TestErasureHouseholdAlreadyScrubbedIsNotRewritten`. Barriers (i): `TestHouseholdBarriersWriterFirstThenErasureScrubs`, `TestHouseholdBarriersErasureFirstRefusesTheWriter` and `TestHouseholdBarriersFirstUseGapRefusesTheSecondStep`, plus `TestHouseholdAdminWritersRefuseAnErasedHolderOrActor` and `TestHouseholdConversionWritersRefuseAnErasedInviterOrActor` for rows 6 and 7. **First use is two transactions** (found while writing the barriers, not in the 9.6 text): `ensureHousehold` commits the household and seat, and the route's own write takes the user lock again in a second transaction. An erasure that commits in the gap finds a sole-subject household and scrubs it, the first transaction's `household.created` audit row stays, and the second step is correctly refused with 401, writing none of the submitted text (`TestHouseholdBarriersFirstUseGapRefusesTheSecondStep`); writer-first with both steps done yields the scrubbed household the barrier text describes. Rows 3 and 4 and the first-use person route leave a surviving non-erased person in the household, so those schedules assert retention and a count, not a scrub.

### T17 `invitations` (baseline 1086; `models.go:607`)
- **FK**: `invited_by`→users CASCADE (3539; never fires), `group_id` (3411), `household_person_id` (3531).
- **Personal**: `email`, `phone`, `name` of the invitee; `token_hash`.
- **Writers**: `api_invitations.go:220`, `api_household_conversion.go:350`. **Readers**: `mail_jobs.go:359` `prepareInvitationMail`, `api_invitations.go:308+` acceptance.
- **Identity links that exist today (traced).** A plain acceptance writes `invitation.accepted` with `target_type = 'invitation'`, `target_id` = the invitation and `meta.userId` = the new account, in the acceptance transaction (`api_invitations.go:433,455-456`; for a guest row upgraded in place the user id is the guest row's, `:385`). A conversion invitation carries `household_person_id` (`api_household_conversion.go:349-350`), its acceptance creates an `account_requests` row with `invitation_id` and `household_person_id` (`:382-390`, called at `:560`) whose `approved_user_id` is set at approval (`api_account_requests.go:595`, `api_household_conversion.go:721`), and its `invitation.accepted` row carries `meta.personId` and **no** `userId` (`:572`). The invitation row itself has no user column.
- **Disp.**: MIXED. Rows in the set `INV` (3.1) → ANON: `email` (placeholder, O-10), `phone`, `name`; set `revoked_at` if open; overwrite `token_hash` with a unique non-secret marker (`idx_invitations_token_hash` is unique, baseline 2868, so never `''`). `INV` is selected by the **retained id links** (accepted-audit `meta.userId`, conversion request `invitation_id` for a request approved for the erased user, `household_person_id` in `PERS`) **and**, for rows nobody accepted, by the exact current address. The id links are what make the address history irrelevant: an invitation accepted at `A0` by an account that later changed to `A` and is then erased is still reached by `meta.userId`, even though `A0` is nowhere on the user row. Rows the erased person *sent* → RET (the invitee's data is theirs); acceptance already fails once the inviter is not an active user (`api_invitations.go:358-364`).
- **Modelled cases (R4-2a tests).** (1) *Change then delete*: accept at `A0` → change to `B` (`email.changed` audit) → erase with captured `B`. `INV` contains the `A0` invitation by the `invitation.accepted` link; its `invitation.created` email meta is scrubbed from the captured ids (6.2). (2) *Address reuse*: after (1) another account takes `A0` and is erased: the `A0` invitation accepted by the first account is **excluded** (3, rule 6) and untouched, and so is its audit row. (3) *No attributable link*: an invitation accepted by an older release with no `userId`, or an unaccepted invitation to a *past* address of the erased account, is not matched (no broad past-address matching) and is a named limit (O-17). (4) *Open invitation to the current address*: matched by address, `revoked_at` set. (5) *Conversion*: a completed and a staged teen conversion both reach their invitation through the request or `PERS`. (6) *Unrelated holder*: an invitation to a different address, or sent by the erased person, is untouched.
- **Capture order.** `INV` and its audit target ids are read **before** the invitation rows are anonymized and held in memory; the audit scrub (R4-4) selects `invitation.created` rows by `target_id IN INV`, never by the text of `meta_json`.
- **Slice**: R4-2a for the rows, R4-4 for their audit meta; the legacy sweep (R4-6) applies the same id-link selectors to accounts erased by the old handler, which is how an `A0` invitation is reached when the old tombstone kept no address. Prepared-and-in-flight mail: section 9.
- **Implemented by R4-2a** (merged in PR #275; `captureErasureSetsIn` for `INV`, `eraseDependentRecordsIn` for the rows): `INV` is the union of four captures read before the scrub: the `invitation.accepted` audit rows whose `meta.userId` is the erased account, the invitation of a conversion request approved for the account, the invitation behind one of the `PERS` people, and unaccepted invitations to the held address. Every `INV` row loses address (placeholder `deleted-invitations-<id>@deleted.invalid`), phone, name and token (marker `erased-invitations-<id>`) and keeps its inviter, group, household person and acceptance time; an open one is revoked, so its link dies. Rows the erased person sent, rows accepted by another account that once held the address, and invitations to a past address with no id link are untouched (M34, M35, O-17). The audit scrub of the matching `invitation.created` rows stays with R4-4. Tests: `TestErasureCapturesRequestInvitationAndPersonSets`, `TestAccountErasureAnonymizesInvitations` (cases 1 to 6), `TestAccountErasureInvitationOwnershipAfterReuse`, `TestAccountErasureStagedConversionInvitation`, `TestInvitationAcceptanceRacingErasure` (acceptance and erasure serialize on the contact gate in both orders), `TestQueuedDependentMailForErasedAccountSendsNothing`.

### T18 `jobs` (baseline 1131; `models.go:1091`)
- **FK**: none. **Personal**: `payload`, `unique_key`, `last_error`.
- **Payload map (producers `jobs.Enqueue` sites)**: direct mail carries ids only (`userId`, `requestId`+`case`, `invitationId`, `accountTokenId`, `guardianLinkId`, `guestSignupId`; `mail_jobs.go`, `AGENTS.md` direct mail section); `notify.deliver {notificationId}` (`notify.go:189`); `push.deliver` ids (`push_deliver.go:57`, `messages_push.go:138`); message batches carry message and recipient ids; `reminders`: occurrence id and times; `push.devices_off {userId, devices, key}` from the users trigger (baseline 127-131). **Exception**: `webhook.process` carries the plaintext **address**, provider message id and detail (`providers.Event`, `internal/providers/events.go:20-35`; enqueued `api_email_webhooks.go:69` with the provider report key as `unique_key`).
- **Retention**: `done`/`dismissed` rows are pruned after 30 days (`jobs_daily.go:23,77`, `internal/jobs/jobs.go:359`); `queued`/`running`/`failed`/`dead` stay until handled; `idx_jobs_unique_key` covers every status (baseline 2889).
- **Disp.**: RET. Direct-mail and notify payloads are ids and fine. `webhook.process` payload/`last_error` plaintext is a suppression-storage item (R4-S/R4-SC), not an erasure-time rewrite. R4-S leaves the payload `address` and `detail` and every `last_error` copy as they were; they stay plaintext until R4-SC. No R4 code writes an address, note or rendered message into a payload. Races: section 9.

### T19 `login_throttles` (baseline 1175; `models.go:549`)
- **FK**: none (text key). **Personal**: the key embeds a normalized address or a user id. Pruned daily after one day (`api_auth.go:175-179`).
- **Key builders (verified)**: `email:<addr>` (`api_auth.go:41`); `user:<id>` (`:202`); `pwreset:addr:<addr>:h|:d` (`:433`); `emailchange:user:<id>:h|:d` and `emailchange:addr:<new addr>:h|:d` (`api_profile.go:200,214`); `acctreq:addr:<addr>` (`api_account_requests.go:224`); `guestreg:hint:<addr>` (`api_guest.go:203`); `guardian:addr:<addr>` where `<addr>` is the address an adult guardian typed into the claim form to find a teen (`apiClaimTeenGuardian`: `api_teen_guardians.go:76-95`; the throttle is hit before the teen lookup at `:101`, so it keys the **searched-for address**, which may belong to a teen, an adult or nobody); `guardian-email:addr:<own addr>` (the guardian's own address, `api_guardian_email.go:173`); `people-search:user:<id>` (`api_people.go:171`); `manager-date-add:<id>` (`api_occurrence_mutations.go:451-453`); shared budgets `ip:`, `pwreset:ip:`, `guestreg:ip:`, `guestreg:site`, `acctreq:site`.
- **Disp.**: DEL the keys the erased person precisely owns: `email:<captured>`, `user:<id>`, `pwreset:addr:<captured>:*`, `emailchange:user:<id>:*`, `emailchange:addr:<captured>:*`, `acctreq:addr:<captured>`, `guestreg:hint:<captured>`, `guardian:addr:<captured>:h|:d` (the budget on the erased person's **own** address as a search target), `guardian-email:addr:<captured>`, `people-search:user:<id>`, `manager-date-add:<id>`. **Preserve** every shared budget: `ip:*`, `*:ip:*` (including `guardian:ip:<ip>`, the typing guardian's address, `api_teen_guardians.go:90`), `*:site`, and every `guardian:addr:<other address>` key (another person's, or nobody's, address). Keys built through `targetAllow` are stored with a `:h` and a `:d` suffix (`api_auth.go:406-412`), so the selector is exact equality on `<key>:h` and `<key>:d`, never a `LIKE` prefix. The earlier reading that `guardian:addr` holds a *guardian's* address was wrong: the typist is the guardian, the key is the target. Erasure never creates a throttle or lockout for a returning address. **Slice**: R4-1. R4-1 selector tests: the erased address's `guardian:addr` keys are deleted; a different address's `guardian:addr` keys and the `guardian:ip` key survive; a prefix-sharing address (`a@x.test` vs `a@x.test.example`) is untouched.
- **Implemented by R4-1.** *Builders and guard (R4-1-1).* Every key now comes from a typed builder in `throttle_keys.go` (`type throttleKey`; 11 owned and 7 shared builders, each with an `// owned:` or `// shared:` doc line; byte-identical strings, pinned by `TestThrottleKeyStringsAreUnchanged`). The throttle helpers accept only a `throttleKey` or `[]throttleKey`; `targeted()` is the one place the `:h`/`:d` suffix rule lives, and `ownedThrottleKeys(id, address)` lists the 18 stored keys the erased account owns. Guards: `TestThrottleKeysComeFromBuilders` (which source shapes may reach a key position), `TestThrottleSQLStaysInItsHelpers` (`login_throttles` SQL only in the named helpers and `eraseOwnedThrottleKeysIn`), `TestOwnedThrottleKeysCoverEveryOwnedBuilder`, with `TestThrottleGuardsCatchMistakes` feeding it fixtures and `TestOwnedThrottleKeysAreExactlyThePersonsKeys` pinning the exact list. **Stated limits:** the guard is parse-only analysis, not a type checker. Identifiers match by name, so shadowing and same-named locals are approximated. It does not see keys assembled inside SQL text within a listed helper, SQL sent around GORM (Fold sends none), reflection, a generic parameter instantiated over `string`, or code outside the root package (no other package writes `login_throttles`; re-grepped). It proves which source shapes reach a key position, not that a builder got the right argument. *Erasure.* `eraseOwnedThrottleKeysIn` is one `DELETE ... WHERE key IN ?` over `ownedThrottleKeys`, exact equality, never `LIKE`, and it is the **last statement of the erasure callback** (before `contactMutation`'s reconcile and the commit), so the erasure holds sign-in rows for the shortest time. Deleting a key lifts any lockout on the erased address, and erasure creates no throttle. The statement is detected deadlock limit **D2** (9.5): the erasure is not deadlock-free. Tests: `TestAccountErasureDeletesOnlyOwnedThrottleKeys` (every owned key goes; shared keys, other people's keys, prefix-sharing addresses and the O-21 residue survive byte-identical), `TestAccountErasureCapturesTheAddressAfterTheLock` (the address deleted is the one held when the lock was taken; a past address is never matched).
  *Changer and target.* `emailchange:user:<id>` is the **changer** budget. `emailchange:addr:<address>` is a **target** budget, deleted only for the address the account holds at erasure. This note does **not** claim the erased account was that key's sole spender: the taken check (`api_profile.go:204-212`) and the spend (`:214`) are separate unlocked statements, so another account can pass the check while the address is free and spend after the erased account took it. The deletion stands under T19's signed disposition because the key embeds the erased person's captured address and no requester id, and deleting it only restarts a per-address mail budget (3 an hour, 10 a day) for an address the tombstone frees. Keys for any other address (the account's pending new address, any past address) are not matched: see **O-21**.

The snapshot side (L1): `prepareMessageOnce` locks every user a snapshot names `FOR KEY SHARE`, ascending, 500 to a query, before it claims the message, and revalidates, so a person erased after the facts were read gets a suppressed row with no address and no row is inserted that still carries an erased address; the order is users, then the message, which is the erasure's order too. ### T28 `registrations` (baseline 1538; `models.go:439`)
- **FK**: `user_id`→users (3443), `registered_by`→users (3635), `household_person_id` (3627), `access_request_id`→contact_requests (3619). `removed_by`, `access_issuer_id`, `access_departure_membership_id`, `access_departure_actor_id`, `access_ended_by` have none. **Trigger**: `registration_access_provenance` BEFORE INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE (baseline 3260 → 144-343).
- **Personal**: `manage_token_hash` (guest cancel capability), `childcare_ages_note`, `removal_note` (typed by a leader about the registrant).
- **Disp.**: MIXED. Subject = erased (`user_id`): ANON blank `manage_token_hash`, `childcare_ages_note`, `removal_note`; keep `status`, `attendance_mode`, counts, ids, timestamps and **every `access_*` column** (past anonymous head-counts and future promotion are preserved; seat release stays with `promoteOnDisable`, `api_registrations.go:1732`, called at `api_admin.go:882`). An UPDATE that leaves the `access_*` and identity columns unchanged takes the trigger's first branch (baseline 169-178) and succeeds. `registered_by`, `removed_by` = erased → attribution RET. A `removal_note` an erased leader wrote about another registrant → other person's record, RET.
- **Staged teen (O-18)**: rows with `household_person_id IN PERS` and `user_id` NULL are the same individual's registrations made by the household holder before the account existed. They get the same ANON statement and the future ones are cancelled by R4-3 through the person-removal path (T15); the statement sets neither `user_id` nor `registered_by`, and leaves every `access_*` column alone, so it takes the trigger's unchanged-identity branch like the account rows.
- **Slice**: **R4-2a** for `user_id` rows; **R4-3** for `household_person_id IN PERS` rows (they depend on `PERS`). The `access_*` columns and the matching audit snapshots are authorization input (sections 6, 9).
- **Implemented by R4-2a** (merged in PR #275; `user_id` rows only, `eraseActivityRowsIn`): one `UPDATE` clears `manage_token_hash`, `childcare_ages_note` and `removal_note` on the erased person's registrations, and writes no status, count, identity or `access_*` column, so the access-provenance trigger takes its unchanged branch. Seat release stays with `promoteOnDisable`. A leader's removal of a registration serializes with the erasure on the contact gate in both orders (9.5). The `household_person_id IN PERS` rows stay with R4-3. Tests: `TestAccountErasureScrubsRegistrations`, `TestRegistrationRemovalSerializesWithErasure`.
- **Implemented by R4-3b-2 for the `household_person_id IN PERS` rows** (staged, see 15.6). After PERS is anonymized, for each *staged* person (`converted_user_id` NULL) `a.reconcileHouseholdPerson(tx, &person, true, actorID, ip)` cancels the future going, maybe and waitlisted rows the way removing a household person does (audit, a notice to the living holder, waitlist promotion), and then one `UPDATE registrations SET manage_token_hash = '', childcare_ages_note = '', removal_note = '' WHERE household_person_id IN PERS` clears the capability and note fields. The statement writes neither `user_id` nor `registered_by` and no `access_*` column, so the access-provenance trigger takes its unchanged-identity branch, and no row gains a `user_id` (that would record a completion that never happened). A completed duplicate has no registrations of its own to cancel. `reconcileHouseholdPerson` locks the person's occurrences ascending under the household, so the D4-class limit in 9.6 applies. Tests: `TestErasureHouseholdStagedTeen`, `TestHouseholdStagedErasureAgainstAHouseholdWriter`.

### T35 `suppressions` (baseline 1826; `models.go:1140`)
- **FK**: `cleared_by`→users SET NULL (3739; never fires). **Personal**: `address` **in plaintext** (CHECK `address = lower(btrim(address))`, baseline 1836), `provider`, `detail` (≤200). Open uniqueness `idx_suppressions_open (channel, address, reason) WHERE cleared_at IS NULL` (3190).
- **R4-S (Route B expand, migration `00002_suppression_address_hash.sql`)**: adds a nullable `address_hash` (lowercase hex SHA-256 of the stored address's UTF-8 bytes), the CHECK `suppressions_address_hash_check` (`address_hash IS NULL OR address_hash = encode(sha256(convert_to(address,'UTF8')),'hex')`, so a hash can never disagree with its address) and the unique open-hash index `idx_suppressions_open_hash (channel, address_hash, reason) WHERE cleared_at IS NULL`. The plaintext `address`, its CHECK and `idx_suppressions_open` are **kept** (Route B). `address_hash` is NULL only for a row written by the previous release or by out-of-band SQL after the upgrade; the migration fills every existing row, and a redelivered webhook report fills a NULL one. Plaintext `address` remains stored beside the hash, so storage is **not** hash-only.
- **Disp.**: RET with a **named, temporary** exception (section 7.4). Erasure never creates, clears or changes a suppression. Hashing is slice R4-S (expand, implemented) and R4-SC (contraction, not built). Full M7.4 completion needs hash-only storage or an explicit authorized PLAN change.

The retention of the prose is the reviewer-signed exception O-4. **Slice**: R4-3 asserts unchanged.

### T37 `users` (baseline 1906; `models.go:132`)
- **Unique/idx**: `uni_users_email` (2538), `idx_users_deleted_at` (3211). **Trigger**: `push_devices_end_with_sessions` (above).
- **Personal**: `email`, `name`, `display_name`, `phone`, `password_hash`, `avatar_key` (a blob key, not a URL), `avatar_focal_x/y`, `birth_date`, `timezone`, `hide_contact_from_leaders`.
- **Disp.** (accepted plan, R4-1): keep `deleted-<id>@deleted.invalid`; clear `name`, `display_name`, `phone`, `timezone`; `password_hash` NULL; `birth_date` NULL; focal points neutral (50); `hide_contact_from_leaders` false; `avatar_key` cleared with a durable cleanup intent (R4-5); `status` `disabled` and `administratively_disabled` true; `session_version` + 1; soft delete. Keep `id`, `role`/`account_type`/timestamps unless R4-1 review says otherwise (O-11). Never hard-delete.
- **Current handler** (`api_admin.go:828-893`) updates only `email` and `session_version` (863-868), so name, phone, hash, birth date, avatar and timezone survive on the soft-deleted row and any `Unscoped` read sees them (`api_admin.go:214`, `api_contact_reads.go:49`, `api_notifications.go:257`, `api_add_request_reads.go:53`).
- **Slice**: **R4-1**, with R4-5 for the blob.
- **Implemented by R4-5 (the `avatar_key` note only)**: the delete handler now sets `avatar_key` to the empty string and queues `avatar.cleanup` for the user in the same transaction as the email rewrite, under the row lock it already held (`api_admin.go`, `apiAdminDeleteUser`). The rest of the T37 scrub (name, display name, phone, timezone, password hash, birth date, focal points, `hide_contact_from_leaders`, status) is **still R4-1**, and the scrub of legacy tombstone rows (which may still hold an `avatar_key` value) is **still R4-6**. The cleanup keeps nothing for a row with `deleted_at` set, so the daily sweep also removes the files in such an account's folder; it does not clear the old column value.
- **Implemented by R4-1 (the rest of the user-row scrub)**: `tombstoneUserIn` (`account_erasure.go`) is one `UPDATE` that sets the email to `deleted-<id>@deleted.invalid`; clears `name`, `display_name`, `phone` and `timezone` to `''`; sets `password_hash` and `birth_date` to NULL, `avatar_focal_x/y` to 50, `hide_contact_from_leaders` to false, `avatar_key` to `''`, `status` to `disabled` and `administratively_disabled` to true; and bumps `session_version` by 1. The handler then soft-deletes the row. It keeps `id`, `role` (O-11), `account_type`, `created_at` and `logout_version`. The "Current handler" bullet above describes the handler before R4-1. Tests: `TestAccountErasureScrubsTheTombstone` (an active adult with every column set, an admin, a guest, an invited account and a `pending_consent` teen; the old session answers 401; the tombstone is not counted as an admin), `TestErasedPersonRendersAsMember` (every unscoped reader of rule 5 shows `Member` and no birth date), `TestEraseAdminKeepsRole`, `TestAccountErasureLeavesSuppressionsAlone` (T35: erasure never creates, clears or changes a suppression), `TestAccountErasureRollsBackWhole`. Rows of accounts erased before R4-1 are not changed: that is **R4-6**.

### T38 `email_address_optouts` (migration `00005_email_address_optouts.sql`; model `models.go:1130`)
- **Stage**: live in the local R4-2d2 implementation. R4-2d2-1 added dormant storage; R4-2d2-2a, -2b and -2c added effective-choice reads, holder turn-on and erasure export, and R4-2d2-3 added the link explanation. These changes are committed locally, not published or merged to master. Section 15.8 records prior checks and reviews; final integration verification and landing remain pending.
- **Columns and Disp.**: `address_hash` RET, `scope` RET, `opted_out_at` RET, under PLAN 10.1 and owner decision 8, with rules T38, 7.6 and O-1 in `erasure_inventory.go`. The composite primary key is `address_hash, scope`. The hash CHECK accepts exactly 64 lowercase hex characters. The scope CHECK accepts only `category:admin`, `category:groups`, `category:events` and `category:messages`. There is no user id, plaintext address, channel, creation time, source, IP, user agent, history or push data.
- **Implemented writer and deleter**: only `exportEmailOptOutsIn` at erasure exports the latest in-force email opt-outs for the address the account holds. An existing record keeps its original choice time. Only the current address holder's affirmative turn-on deletes the matching record. Defaults, account creation, address changes, webhooks and admin suppression clearing do neither. Legacy tombstones export nothing. Section 7.6 records the implementation against the approved design.
- **Privacy limit**: `mail.AddressHash` computes an unkeyed SHA-256 hash in Go. It is pseudonymous and guessable, not anonymous. `opted_out_at` equals a retained `consents.at` on the tombstone, and the record's PostgreSQL `xmin` identifies the erasure transaction. An operator with database access and a candidate address can therefore link the record to its tombstone id. Records are never shown or exported to anyone through Fold.
- **Migration**: this DEC-138 additive step changes no existing table. Down is for scratch databases and down/up verification only. It refuses when any record exists, with `email_address_optouts has retained opt-outs; restore the upgrade.sh dump instead`, so rollback cannot silently discard retained choices. O-1 is implemented locally only; C2 and M7.4 remain incomplete.

### T39 `admin_operation_attempts` (migration `00006_admin_operation_attempts.sql`; model `models.AdminOperationAttempt`)
- **Stage**: T07B-1a added dormant additive storage, its model, migration tests and inventory coverage only. T07B-1b (committed locally as `cd3ad9a`, staged, section 15.9) is now its only writer and reader: `insertAttemptReceipt` inserts one row in the deletion's own transaction right after the restore ticket (only when the request carries an attempt id), settle reads it under its fenced transaction, and the daily `admin_attempts.sweep` job deletes rows older than 24 hours. The reason review (T07B-2 and later) is still not implemented.
- **Columns and Disp.**: `attempt_id` DEL, `created_at` DEL, under rule T39 in `erasure_inventory.go`. This is a transient workflow row: random nonce and timestamp, deleted after 24 hours, no personal data. The 24-hour cleanup is the sweep named above (batched `DELETE` of 5,000 rows per batch, a 10-second context per batch, a 2-minute budget, then `jobs.Later`), staged locally with the rest of 1b. These rows have no account selector and erasure does not delete them by user id.
- **Shape**: `attempt_id` is a `bytea` primary key with a CHECK requiring exactly 16 bytes. `created_at` is `timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now()`, indexed by `admin_operation_attempts_created_at`. There is no kind, actor, subject, outcome, foreign key or restore-generation value. The stored nonce is not the full signed attempt id described by the later workflow design.
- **Migration**: DEC-138 additive storage changes no existing table. Down drops only these transient receipts, including populated ones; it does not undo a domain operation or its audit rows. The existing full-schema drift and Down/Up checks include the model through `models.All()`. Tests added for this slice have not been run by the implementation agent; Terra owns execution and evidence. No T07 or M7.4 completion gate closes here.

---

## 5. Mixed subject/actor rows

Rows where one table holds records of different ownership. Each is a separate selector in the erasure code.

| # | Table / predicate | Owner | Disposition | Reason / limit | Slice |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | `notifications` where `user_id` = erased | subject | DEL | already done, `api_admin.go:874` | R4-1 |
| M2 | `notifications` where `actor_id` = erased, recipient someone else | other person's | RET | rendered name inside another person's bell/email; DEC-45 retention prunes it; not rewritten | none |
| M3 | `notifications` waiting tasks about the erased subject (kind + `subject_id`) | church task | `notify.Resolve` | close by exact kind/subject; surviving-subject tasks stay | R4-2a, R4-3 |
| M4 | `notifications` where `resolved_by_id` = erased | attribution | RET | id only | none |
