**Implemented by R4-2a** (merged in PR #275), for the mixed rows below; each follows the table's own disposition and adds nothing to it:

- **M11, M12, M13, M39** (account requests and their `ip`): `eraseAccountRequestsIn`, see T01. M11 anonymizes the `REQ` rows and withdraws the pending ones; M12 changes nothing but the `ip` of a `member_invite` row the erased person requested; M13 clears a `guardian_email` equal to the held address; M39 keeps a `member_invite` `ip` on the friend's own erased row and blanks the other sources' `ip`. Tests: `TestAccountErasureAnonymizesAccountRequests`, `TestAccountErasureRequestAuditDisposition`.
- **M14, M32, M33, M34** (invitations): `captureErasureSetsIn` builds `INV` from the acceptance audit link (M32), the conversion request and `PERS` links (M33) and, for rows nobody accepted, the held address (M14); a row accepted by another account that once held the address is excluded (M34). Tests: `TestAccountErasureAnonymizesInvitations`, `TestAccountErasureInvitationOwnershipAfterReuse`, `TestAccountErasureStagedConversionInvitation`, `TestErasureCapturesRequestInvitationAndPersonSets`.
- **M22, M23** (announcement recipients): the erased person's rows are scrubbed and a queued one is suppressed (M22); a guardian's copy row about the erased person is kept (M23). Tests: `TestAccountErasureScrubsMessageRecipients`, `TestMessageSnapshotRevalidatesRecipientsUnderLock`.
- **M26**: R4-2a (merged) wrote no `contact_requests` statement. R4-T07A (ages note) is adopted and implemented in its implementation PR, which is not merged and waits on its merge gates; R4-T07B requires an admin review step during deletion; its design is approved and behavior remains pending (see 15.4). Local migration `00007` adds the dormant database licence only (non-contact, non-pending rows; no caller).

---

## 6. Audit log census (by action, target, key and owner)

Actor equality alone **never** permits removing another subject's metadata: `audit_logs` has no foreign keys, so
the only joins are `target_type`/`target_id` and a meta key, and the actor, the target and the person a note or
address is *about* can be three different people. Everything below is *traced* unless marked *Proposed*.

### 6.1 How complete the census is

- **One insert path.** `audit.Write` (`internal/audit/audit.go:380-396`, a single `db.Create(&models.AuditLog{...})`)
  is the only code that creates an `AuditLog`. No raw `INSERT INTO audit_logs`, no trigger and no job writes one:
  the baseline only reads `audit_logs` (triggers listed at `00001_baseline.sql:3225-3267`; none is on `audit_logs`).
  `audit_logs` is not append-only by trigger, so a later scrub UPDATE is physically possible; the baseline column
  list is `id, actor_id, action, target_type, target_id, meta_json jsonb, ip text null, at` (`:473-482`).
- **113 `audit.Write(` call expressions** in 41 non-test root-package files (`grep -rn "audit\.Write(" --include=*.go`, none under
  `internal/` except the definition). Every one was opened. Wrapper functions that put a variable `Entry` or
  `meta` into one of them, and their callers, were followed: `auditAndNotifyLeadership` (`api_groups.go:1031`; four
  callers: `api_groups.go:860`, `api_handoff.go:167`, `api_handoff.go:231`, `api_departure.go:738`),
  `notifyLeadership` (`api_notifications.go:509`; reads `Meta["userId"]`, `oldRole`, `newRole` from the same `Entry` only to word a notice),
  `auditRegistration` (`api_registrations.go:823`; callers `:643,816,1838,1856,1991` and
  `registration_subjects.go:214`) with `registrationMeta` (`:837`), `writeAccessSnapshot`
  (`registration_access.go:167`) with `accessSnapshotMeta` (`:136`), `contactAudit` (`contact_lifecycle.go:207`;
  21 callers in `api_contact.go`, `contact_lifecycle.go`, `api_occurrence_mutations.go`), `saveSetting`
  (`api_message_settings.go:22`; callers `api_email_settings.go:204`, `api_message_settings.go:49,75`, `api_push.go:124`)
  and the audited refusal helpers `refuseContact`/`refuseAdd`/`writeInvitationRefusal`. Sites whose
  `Action:` is a variable (`auditAction`, `action`) were resolved to the constants assigned to it.
- **119 action strings**: the 118 constants in `internal/audit/audit.go:16-366` plus `contact.privacy_changed`, which
  is defined outside the package (`api_contact.go:21`, written at `api_profile.go:150`). A reference search for
  `audit.<Name>` over non-test Go found **no unreferenced constant**. That proves every action has a producer, not
  that every producer path runs.
- **Not covered, by construction**: rows written by earlier releases with meta shapes or actions that no longer
  exist in the tree (the comments at `audit.go:90-101` name two: `oldSource/newSource` before DEC-48 and
  `membership.return_approved/return_declined` from DEC-39). Their keys cannot be enumerated from source.
  R4-4's coverage test must fail on any action or meta key it has no row for, and the unknown-key residue of
  legacy rows is a named limit (O-3), not a silent gap.

### 6.2 Actions whose meta (or text) names a person or an address

| Action(s) | Producer sites | Personal key(s) | Whose data | Proposed treatment (pending review) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `user.email_changed` | `api_admin.go:733-735` | `oldEmail`, `newEmail` | the **target** user (an admin changed it) | scrub both keys when target = erased; when only the *actor* is erased keep, the addresses are the target's |
| `email.change_requested`, `email.changed` | `api_profile.go:244-246`, `:345-347` | `oldEmail`, `newEmail` (the requested address, which may never have been confirmed) | target = actor = the person | scrub both keys when target = erased |
| `invitation.created` | `api_invitations.go:222-228` | `email` (invitee's address); `groupId`, `overLimit` are not personal. The conversion variant (`api_household_conversion.go:360`) writes `personId`, `householdId` and **no** address | the **invitee's address**; actor is the inviter | select by `target_type='invitation'` and `target_id ∈ INV` (3.1), the set captured **before** the invitation rows are anonymized (T17), then clear `email`. `INV` is built from retained id links (`invitation.accepted` `meta.userId`, conversion request, `PERS`) and, for rows nobody accepted, the exact current address; so an invitation accepted at `A0` by an account now at `B` is reached, and an `A0` invitation accepted by a *different* account is not. A string search of `meta_json` is not used. Keep when only the inviter is erased |
| `invitation.accepted` | `api_invitations.go:455-456`; `api_household_conversion.go:572` | `userId` (plain path), `personId` (conversion path), `groupId`: ids only | the accepting account | **keep `meta_json` byte-identical**: `meta.userId` is the retained link the `INV` selector (and the legacy sweep) reads. Its `ip` is 6.5 |
| `membership.removed` | `api_departure.go:735-745` | `note` (written only when non-empty) | typed by a leader/admin **about** the subject `meta.userId` | scrub `note` where `meta.userId` = erased; keep when only the actor is erased. A note may record a safeguarding reason, so the scrub is subject to the safeguarding reviewer (O-4) |
| `registration.removed` | `api_registrations.go:1345-1349` | `note` (written only when the remover held no occurrence grant, `!hadGrant`) | typed by a leader/host **about** the registrant (`meta.userId` or `meta.personId`) | same rule and same reviewer as `membership.removed` |
| `guardian_link.confirmed`, `guardian_link.rejected` | `api_admin.go:399-421` (`checkNote`, `guardianUserId`, `personId`/`teenUserId`, `selfConfirm`) | `checkNote` | safeguarding record typed by an admin; mirror of `guardian_links.check_note` | **proposed RET** (O-4). The age-rollover variant `age_rollover.go:160` carries `reason`, `teenUserId` and no note |
| `teen_consent.granted`, `teen_consent.revoked` | `api_teen_consent.go:162-163` | `evidenceText` | safeguarding evidence typed by an admin or guardian; mirror of immutable `teen_consents.evidence_text` | **proposed RET** (O-4) |
| `push.device_on` | `api_push.go:252-253` (refused), `:278-279` | `label` (device label) | the target user's device | scrub `label` when target = erased (the devices themselves are deleted by R4-1) |
| `group.approved`, `group.rejected` | `api_admin.go:149-153` (`message` = the deciding admin's note); `api_groups.go:482` writes no meta | `message` | the **admin's** words about a group, not about a person | RET (church record); not erased because the actor is erased |

`user.deleted` is written today with **no** meta (`api_admin.go:869`); R4-1 adds counts and a mode only
(`credentialsRevoked`, `rowsScrubbed`, brief section 6), never an address or a name.

### 6.3 Actions with no personal key (grouped, with the evidence)

Every key below is a numeric id, an enum, a count, a boolean, a fixed code or a timestamp. Numeric ids that name the
erased person (`userId`, `subjectUserId`, `teenUserId`, `guardianUserId`, `holderUserId`, `requesterId`, `hostIds`,
`departingUserId`, `registeredBy`, `issuerId`, `endedBy`, `subjectId`) are **attribution** and stay: the tombstone
renders as "Member" (`models.go:177-185`). The rendering of a name inside an audit row never happens; names are
looked up at display time (`nameIn`, `api_notifications.go:286`).

| Group | Exact actions | Producer sites | Keys (all non-personal) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Account and credential lifecycle | `user.created`, `user.role_changed`, `user.disabled`, `user.enabled`, `user.deleted`, `user.turned_18`, `password.reset_requested`, `password.reset_completed`, `password.changed`, `email.change_cancelled`, `account.requested`, `account.approved`, `account.rejected`, `account.expired`, `account.setup_completed`, `contact.privacy_changed` | `api_admin.go:554,727,754,774,789,869,927`; `age_rollover.go:184`; `api_auth.go:261,470,509`; `api_email_change_cancel.go:36`; `api_account_requests.go:280,629,692,765`; `api_guest.go:527`; `api_household_conversion.go:393`; `api_profile.go:150` | `role`, `oldRole`, `newRole`, `byAdmin`, `previousStatus`, `status`, `churchDate`, `source`, `accountType`, `hasWarnings`, `personId`, `userId`, `created`, `upgradedGuest`, `hideContactFromLeaders`. `account.approved/rejected` keep the admin's `decisionNote` on the request row, **not** in the log (`audit.go:215-219`); `account.requested` never carries email or phone (`:210-213`) |
| Tombstone maintenance | `user.erasure_swept` | `eraseTombstoneOnce` (`account_erasure_tombstones.go`), L15 | Target `user`, numeric `version`, `householdsKept`, `activityEntriesKept` only; no actor or IP, classified `auditIPSystem`, which maps to NONE. The marker commits with the scrub and records only that version's work, not complete erasure. Query and count limits: 12.2 |
| Invitations (no address) | `invitation.resent`, `invitation.refused`, `invitation.revoked`, `invitation.accepted`, `invitation.rate_limited` (and `invitation.created` from the conversion path) | `api_invitations.go:33,179,455`; `api_household_conversion.go:360,408,572` | `reason`, `limit`, `userId`, `groupId`, `personId`, `householdId`. `audit.go:176-177,186-188` state that refusals carry no address |

| Registrations | `registration.changed`, `registration.restored`, `registration.not_restored`, `registration.cancelled`, `registration.added`, `registration.added_over_capacity`, `registration.promoted` (and `registration.removed`, minus its `note`, 6.2) | `api_registrations.go:643,816,1469,1838,1856,1991,2145`; `registration_subjects.go:214`; `api_group_visibility.go:347`; `api_guest.go:737`; `api_household_conversion.go:635` | `registrationId`, `subjectType`, `subjectId`, `userId`, `personId`, `registeredBy`, `occurrenceId`, `attendanceMode`, `status`, `oldStatus`/`newStatus`, guest/childcare counts and flags, `seats`, `capacity`, `newVisibility`, `reason` (`event_more_private`), `formerRegisteredBy`, `clearedChildcareRequested`, `invitationId`. `registrationMeta` (`api_registrations.go:837-843`) defines the base set; **no** child-care ages note is copied into the log. The conversion transfer's `registration.changed` (`api_household_conversion.go:635`, `target_type = registration`) stores **only** `personId`, `userId`, `formerRegisteredBy`, `clearedChildcareRequested`, `invitationId`: no `accountType`, no `transferCount` (6.5.1) |

| Contact and add requests | `contact.requested`, `.decision`, `.withdrawn`, `.expired`, `.granted`, `.reveal`, `.refused`, `.revoked`, `.ended`; `add.requested`, `.decision`, `.withdrawn`, `.expired`, `.completed`, `.refused`, `.ended`; `occurrence.refused`; `contact_history.exported` | `contact_lifecycle.go:216,226`; `api_contact.go:626`; `api_occurrence_mutations.go:266,392,635`; `api_registrations.go:1633`; `api_contact_history.go:475` | `requesterId`, `subjectUserId`, `contextType`, `contextId`, `occurrenceId`, `purpose`, `needsAdmin`, `outcome`, `fields` (re-validated by `policy.ValidContactFields` before writing, `contact_lifecycle.go:219-222`), `reason` (a fixed vocabulary or `party:decision`; callers listed by `grep contactAudit(`), `method`, `route`, `operation`, and export filters (`count`, `from`, `until`, `action`). A rejected request's typed reason text is **not** logged (`api_contact.go:263-265`) |
| Messages | `message.scheduled`, `.cancelled`, `.edited`, `.test_sent`, `.refused`, `.sent`, `.retried` | `api_messages.go:824,889,921`; `api_message_preview.go:217`; `api_message_history.go:585`; `messages_send.go:373,421` | `audience` (type only), `recipients`, `at`, `status`, `byOther`, `people`, `why` (fixed codes), `sent`/`skipped`/`failed` counts |
| Rooms | `room.created`, `room.updated`, `room_booking.booked`, `.released`, `.overlap`, `.series`, `room_request.confirmed`, `.rejected`, `room.location_matched`, `room_feed.created`, `room_feed.revoked` | `api_rooms.go:235,284`; `api_room_bookings.go:395,459`; `api_room_series.go:428`; `api_room_requests.go:317,389`; `api_room_locations.go:155`; `api_room_feeds.go:121,144` | room and event ids, dates, counts, `replaced`; church-owned text in 6.4 |
| Settings and system | `setting.self_signup_changed`, `setting.rooms_enabled_changed`, `setting.room_approval_changed`, `setting.church_address_changed`, `setting.guardian_copies_changed`, `setting.reply_to_changed`, `setting.push_enabled_changed`, `job.retried`, `job.dismissed`, `email.suppression_cleared`, `push.keys_rotated`, `push.keys_reset`, `push.test_sent`, `calendar_token.regenerated`, `.revoked`, `.scope_changed` | `api_account_requests.go:732`; `api_rooms.go:319`; `api_room_requests.go:60`; `api_message_settings.go:28` (via `saveSetting`); `api_admin_jobs.go:150`; `api_admin_suppressions.go:69`; `push_keys.go:175,223`; `api_push.go:385`; `api_feeds.go:435,474,526` | `mode`, `enabled`, `on`, `set` (a boolean; the new address is **not** logged, `api_email_settings.go:204`, `api_message_settings.go:75`), `kind`, `reasons` (suppression reason codes), `devicesRemoved`, `deviceId`, `scope`, `oldScope`, `newScope` |
| Authorization snapshots | `occurrence_access.added`, `occurrence_access.ended`, `occurrence.blocked` | `registration_access.go:234,260,168`; `api_occurrence_mutations.go:330` | see 6.7 |

### 6.4 Church-owned text that is not the erased person's data

`event.deleted.title`, `group.deleted.name`, `room.created.name/building`, `room.location_matched.location`, the
`reason` an admin typed for a room overlap or rejection (`room_booking.overlap`, `room_request.*`,
`room_booking.series`: `api_room_bookings.go:336,459`, `api_room_requests.go:251,356`, `api_room_series.go:252`), and the
leader's visibility `reason` (`api_event_access.go:403-415,494`, `api_events.go:822-827,854`) describe a church
object. They stay. Such a field may happen to contain a person's name; deciding that would need a name match
across free text, which this inventory forbids (3, rule 1). That residue is a bounded limit (O-16).

### 6.5 The `ip` column: whose address, call site by call site

*Traced.* No authority check consumes `audit_logs.ip`: `api_contact_history.go` omits it;
`registration_access.go` and the provenance trigger read `action`, `target_type`, `target_id`
and `meta_json`. R4-4a now filters it for erasure and retention counts (6.9).
No endpoint lists the audit log. The IP is separate evidence, not an input to authorization.

Ownership of the value is **not** the same as `actor_id`, and it is not the same as the person a row is *about*.
The value is the `ClientIP` of the request that wrote the row (3.2). The table lists each site by the class of that
request, then says what the draft proposes. `U` is the erased user id.

| Site(s) | `actor_id` | Whose address `ip` holds (class) | Proposed disposition (pending review) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `invitation.accepted` `api_invitations.go:455-456`; `membership.added_over_limit` `:443-447` | the inviter (`inv.InvitedBy`) | the browser that created or activated the new account in the same request (**A**, for the account in `meta.userId`) | blank where `meta_json->>'userId' = U`; keep when only the inviter is erased |
| `invitation.accepted` conversion `api_household_conversion.go:572`; `account.requested` conversion `:393` (ip passed at `:560`) | the inviter / nil | whoever redeemed the emailed link (**B**; the mailbox holder, who may be a relative) | blank where `target_id` (or the request) is in `INV`/`REQ` of `U` (**Proposed**, limitation O-19); keep when only the inviter is erased |
| **C-approval** (6.5.1). `account.approved` (`api_account_requests.go:629`); teen `guardian_link.requested` with `source = household_conversion` (`api_household_conversion.go:733`, the **only** caller with that source, teen branch only); adult-path transfer rows, which are **two different shapes** written by `transferHouseholdConversion` (`api_household_conversion.go:597-658`, through `moveHouseholdConversion` `:594` ← `approveHouseholdConversion` `:741` ← `decideAccountRequest` `:540`): `N` × `registration.changed` `:635` (`target_type = registration`; meta `personId`, `userId`, `formerRegisteredBy`, `clearedChildcareRequested`, `invitationId`; **no** `accountType`, **no** `transferCount`) and one `household.person_converted` `:646` (`target_type = household`; meta `personId`, `householdId`, `invitationId`, `userId`, `accountType`, `transferCount`) | `account.approved`: the admin `D`; the other rows: the inviter (`inv.InvitedBy`) | **C-approval**: the request of the approving admin `D`, the one `account_requests` row with `source = household_conversion`, `status = approved`, `approved_user_id = S` and **retained `decided_by = D`**, stored by the `UPDATE` at `api_account_requests.go:526-533` before the helper ran, in the same transaction, with the same `c.ClientIP()`. The `household.person_converted` row is classified as an approval **by exclusion** (`accountType = adult`, no age-rollover marker for the person) and only under the **history precondition**; each `registration.changed` row is not classified by its own metadata (it has none that can) but is joined to that row by the exact tuple `(personId, invitationId, userId)` derived from `D`'s request, after the count `N = transferCount` is verified | **blank `ip` where `D` is the erased user and the row is selected by the 6.5.1 selectors** (Proposed, owner decision 1); **keep** when only the inviter, the converted person `S` or a guardian is erased (they did not make the request). **Retain, and report with actual counts, the whole discovered group of transfer rows when the history precondition is unestablished (the default), any discovered row is malformed, the `household.person_converted` row is not exactly one, or the `registration.changed` count differs from `transferCount`** (6.5.1; O-19; the residue gates M7.4 completion, 6.5.2) |
| **C-completion** (6.5.1). Teen conversion completing later: `registration.changed` `:635` and `household.person_converted` `:646` reached through `completeTeenConversionIfReady` (`api_teen_conversion.go:195`), whose callers are an admin deciding a link (`api_admin.go:414`), an admin changing the account's status, which includes Enable and is the only completer while the account is suspended (`api_admin.go:797`), an admin or a confirmed guardian recording consent (`api_teen_consent.go:154`), and the nightly age job with an **empty** address (`age_rollover.go:181`) | the inviter | **C-completion**: one of those callers; the stored row does not say which (`actor_id` is the inviter, and no `meta_json` key names the caller). Only the `household.person_converted` row says it is a completion: `accountType = 'teen'`, or `'adult'` with the age-rollover marker (`household.person_updated`, `target_type = 'household_person'`). The `registration.changed` rows say nothing of the kind and are joined to it by `(personId, invitationId, userId)` | **retain, as an attribution limitation (O-19), not as accountability retention**: no exact key reaches the erased caller, so no selector can aim at it, and an erased `D` is not assumed to be the caller even if `D` also approved. Never blanked because the converted person, the inviter or `D` is erased. An address that is empty (the job) is already clear |
| `guardian_link.requested`, `source = signup` `api_teen_guardians.go:60` (`nominateTeenGuardian`, called at `api_account_requests.go:590` for every non-conversion approval of a teen with a guardian address, whatever the request's `source`) | nil | `req.IP`, the approved request's own `ip` (class by that request's `source`, T01 table: **B** for `self_signup` and `guest_upgrade`, the inviting member for `member_invite`) | follow that request's row (M39), **only when exactly one non-conversion request has `approved_user_id` = `meta.teenUserId`**. There is no unique index for non-conversion sources, and an approval of an already existing account sets `approved_user_id` too, so a second request can share the same user; with two or more, retain and count (the row carries no request id) |
| `guardian_link.requested`, `source = claim` `api_teen_guardians.go:112` | the claiming guardian | their own session (**A**) | blank only if the guardian is the erased actor, subject to O-4 (safeguarding action) |
| `account.requested` `api_account_requests.go:280` | nil for self-signup; the inviting member for invite-a-friend | self-signup: the form submitter (**B**); member invite: the inviting member, **not** the requested friend (**A**, actor) | self-signup: with the request's row (M39); member invite: blank when the **actor** is the erased member, keep when only the friend is erased |
| `account.requested` guest upgrade `api_guest.go:527-531` | nil | `signup.IP`, the guest form's submitter (**B**) | with the request's row (M39) |
| `registration.cancelled` `api_guest.go:737` | nil | the guest cancelling (**B**) | blank where `meta` ties the registration to `U` |
| `password.reset_requested` `api_auth.go:470` | the account found by the typed address | whoever submitted the public form (**B**), not necessarily the account holder | blank where `target_id = U`, **Proposed** (O-19): the row exists only because someone typed `U`'s address |
| Ordinary signed-in writes (profile, settings, push, groups, registrations) | the signed-in user | that user's session (**A**) | blank where `actor_id = U` (admin accountability classes: O-6) |
| System writers (`age_rollover.go`, `messages_send.go:373,421`, `push_keys.go`, `api_account_requests.go:765`, `finishContactRequest`) | nil | empty | nothing to do |

*Proposed (pending review).* Because `ip` is never read, blanking an address that is **attributable** to the erased
person is technically safe and follows owner decision 1's "scrub personal details where technically safe" and
DEC-144's "only an anonymous tombstone needed for audit and safeguarding integrity" (the row's `actor_id`, `action`,
target, time and `meta_json` stay and are that integrity; a person's network address is not part of it). It applies to
Class A rows by actor or by the retained `meta.userId`; to Class B rows that hang off a row already tied to `U` by an
exact id link; and to **C-approval** rows when `U` is the approving admin `D`, selected by the exact join in 6.5.1 (the transfer rows only when every discovered row is well formed, the join is unique, counts out and the history precondition is established). Class B
is "probably the person, unprovably": blanking it removes at most an unrelated submitter's address from the erased
person's own record, and keeping it keeps at most the erased person's; the draft proposes blanking and **labels the
limitation (O-19)** for the reviewer to confirm or reverse.

What is **retained**, and the specific reason each time:

- An address whose owner is a **living** party (the approving admin when only the inviter or the converted person is
  erased; a redeemer or guardian when someone else is erased). Blanking it would remove a living person's data for no
  erasure. This is the owner-attribution reason, not an accountability reason.
- **C-completion** rows (6.5.1): the owner is one of four possible callers and no stored key says which. This is an
  attribution limit (O-19), and it is **residue, not an accepted retention**: if the erased person was that caller, their address stays on those
  rows, and the draft says so rather than guess. **The draft therefore does not claim that every address of an erased
  administrator or guardian is scrubbed.** An erased approving admin who also confirmed a link, enabled the account or
  recorded consent keeps that address on the completion rows of the conversion (normally `1 + transferCount` of them; the report gives the actual count), and so does any other erased
  completer. The owner's "scrub personal details where technically safe" is applied to every row whose owner is
  exactly attributable, and only to those. The rest is gated: M7.4 is not complete while it can remain (G-9, 6.5.2).
- **Transfer rows whose class or join cannot be proven** (6.5.1): the audit history is not shown to be current (the
  default), a discovered row is malformed, the `household.person_converted` row is not exactly one, or the `registration.changed` rows do not number
  `transferCount`. The owner is probably the approving admin and **cannot be shown to be**, so the draft retains the
  whole discovered group of the conversion (normally `1 + transferCount` rows; 6.5.1 reports the actual counts) with the reason. An erased approving admin
  therefore keeps those addresses in that case, and **the erasure is not complete for them**; the limit and its gates
  (G-8 for the history, G-9 for milestone completion) are named in 6.5.1, 6.5.2 and O-19, O-20 rather than hidden behind a "complete" report.
- **No accountability exception is proposed for an erased administrator.** The accountability record is the audit row
  without its address. If the safeguarding reviewer believes an administrator's address is *essential safeguarding
  evidence* on a particular action, that would be a retention exception to owner decision 1 and DEC-144; it needs a
  stated reason and an owner/PLAN decision, and a reviewer signature alone does not create it. None is made here. O-6
  records this. The same holds for the residue above: a reviewer's acceptance, and this file's naming of it, cannot
  keep a personal IP address past M7.4's completion; 6.5.2 states the gate, the owner's bounded P2 scope (owner decision 5) and
  the unapproved ways it could be closed.

Safeguarding actions (`guardian_link.*`, `teen_consent.*`) keep their ids, status, meta and the admin-typed prose under
O-4; their `ip` follows the same ownership rule, and the safeguarding reviewer confirms the address is not part of the
evidence. R4-4 must preserve `meta_json` of all three authorization snapshots and of every row used as a selector
(`invitation.accepted`, the age-rollover marker `household.person_updated` with `target_type = 'household_person'`, and the
6.5.1 selector inputs). R4-4 tests are in 6.5.1. This is a proposal, not an exemption or
an approval.

#### 6.5.1 Conversion steps: which rows have a provable owner and which do not

*Second-round correction (R40-PRIV-03).* The previous candidate chose between "adult" and "teen" rows with
`household_people.kind` and called it frozen after approval. **That was wrong and is removed.** `kind` is not frozen:
the nightly age job changes a staged teen person to `adult` before completion (`age_rollover.go:118-130`,
`stage.person.Kind == HouseholdTeen` then `Update("kind", HouseholdAdult)`), and when the account is administratively
disabled the completion waits for a later admin Enable (`api_teen_conversion.go:143`; `api_admin.go:797`; covered by
`TestAgeRolloverSuspendedStageKeepsHouseholdUntilEnable`, `age_rollover_test.go:355-396`). The material-edit
refusal (`errConversionConsent`, `api_profile.go:1353-1357`) stops a *person's* edit, not the job. **No selector below reads
`household_people.kind`, and none may.** Every discriminator used is a retained audit or request key whose source was traced.

*Fourth-round correction (R40-PRIV-03).* The third-round selectors required `meta.accountType` on both transfer actions and
said the two share every key. The `registration.changed` writer never stores `accountType` or `transferCount`
(`api_household_conversion.go:635` writes `personId`, `userId`, `formerRegisteredBy`, `clearedChildcareRequested` and
`invitationId`); only the `household.person_converted` row does (`:645-646`). As written, even an ordinary adult approval's
`registration.changed` rows could not have been selected, and the claim that two rows were blanked for it was false. Below, the two
shapes are separated; the `registration.changed` rows are reached through the exact tuple `(personId, invitationId, userId)`
from the erased admin's own request to a `household.person_converted` row that is verified to be unique; and the audit history that
classification relies on is an explicit, gated precondition (G-8) rather than an assumption. Where uniqueness or the history cannot be
shown, both transfer classes are **retained and reported** as residue (G-9, 6.5.2); the draft does not claim a scrub it cannot prove.
*Fifth-round correction (R40-CORR-10).* The fourth-round predicates dropped rows with the wrong shape before V1 and V2 counted them,
so a malformed duplicate could keep its address while the rest of the group was scrubbed. Discovery is now separate from shape
validation (V0), and any failure retains the entire discovered group (below).

*Traced, in the order the code runs* (`decideAccountRequest`, `api_account_requests.go:445-640`, one `contactMutation`
transaction; `me` is the signed-in admin of this HTTP request, locked and re-checked as an active admin with the same
session version, `:480-484`):

1. `UPDATE account_requests SET status, decided_by = me.ID, decided_at, ... WHERE id = ? AND status = 'pending'` (`:526-533`).
   `RequestApproved` is assigned only at `:524`, so every approved request has `decided_by` set, to the admin whose
   HTTP request approved it. That admin is `D`.
2. `approveHouseholdConversion(tx, c, req, ...)` (`:540`), the same request and the same `c.ClientIP()`. It branches on
   `person.Kind` **as read inside this transaction under the household lock** (`api_household_conversion.go:716`). That is a
   fact about the moment of approval only; it is not stored anywhere, and nothing below reads it back.
   - *Adult branch* (`:741` → `moveHouseholdConversion` `:588-595` → `transferHouseholdConversion` with `c.ClientIP()`):
     creates the account `S` with `AccountType = person.Kind` (adult), writes `N` `registration.changed` (`:635`) and one
     `household.person_converted` (`:646`, `meta.accountType = user.AccountType`), sets `converted_user_id`. Done; nothing
     runs it again.
   - *Teen branch* (`:716-736`): creates `S` as `pending_consent` with `AccountType = teen` (`:717`), sets
     `approved_user_id` (`:721`) and, for each confirmed active adult guardian of the person,
     `requestTeenGuardian(..., "household_conversion", c.ClientIP())` (`:733`). It transfers nothing. An admin who supplies
     consent evidence at approval reaches `completeTeenConversionIfReady` in the same transaction (`:599-608`), but readiness
     needs a **confirmed** teen-side link (`api_teen_conversion.go:156-165`) and the links just created are `pending`, so no
     transfer row is written at approval for a teen.
3. `approved_user_id = S` is written (`:595`) and `account.approved` is written with actor `D`, target the request and the
   same address (`:629`).
4. **Later, teen only**: `completeTeenConversionIfReady` (`api_teen_conversion.go:131-196`) runs the **same**
   `transferHouseholdConversion` with the caller's address. Its callers, all traced: `api_admin.go:414` (an admin
   confirms a guardian link), `api_admin.go:797` (an admin changes the account's status, including Enable),
   `api_teen_consent.go:154` (consent recorded by an admin or a confirmed guardian) and `age_rollover.go:181` (the nightly
   job, address `""`). It returns without completing while the account is deleted or administratively disabled (`:143`). The flag is cleared in exactly one place,
   `apiAdminUpdateUser` (`api_admin.go:709`), which then calls completion itself (`:797`). So **while an account is
   suspended, the first completer is the admin who enables it**, and every other caller returns early.

*What the two transfer actions share, and what they do not (corrected, fourth round).* The adult approval and a later teen
completion run the same function, `transferHouseholdConversion` (`api_household_conversion.go:597-658`), so they write the
same two audit actions with the same actor (the inviter, `inv.InvitedBy`) and the same ids. **They do not store the same
keys.** The previous candidate said they did and required `meta.accountType` on both. Traced at `:635` and `:645-646`:

| Action | `target_type`, `target_id` | `meta_json` keys, all of them |
|---|---|---|
| `registration.changed`, one per transferred registration | `registration`, the registration id | `personId`, `userId`, `formerRegisteredBy`, `clearedChildcareRequested`, `invitationId` |
| `household.person_converted`, exactly one per run | `household`, the household id | `personId`, `householdId`, `invitationId`, `userId`, `accountType`, `transferCount` |

So `accountType` and `transferCount` exist **only** on the `household.person_converted` row. A transferred registration's
`registration.changed` row carries no account type, no count and no marker, and **no selector may require `meta.accountType`
on it**. That was the defect: as written the previous selector could not have chosen even an ordinary adult approval's
`registration.changed` rows, so an erased approving admin's address would have stayed on them. Those rows are told from the
event-registration `registration.changed` rows by shape: the other writers go through `auditRegistration`
(`api_registrations.go:823-835`, called at `api_registrations.go:643,814` and `registration_subjects.go:214`) with
`target_type = 'event'` and `registrationMeta` (`api_registrations.go:837-843`), which has no `invitationId`,
`formerRegisteredBy` or `clearedChildcareRequested`; the last two keys have exactly one production writer (`:635`, by
search).

*Keys retained by erasure* (T01, T15 keep them): `account_requests.id/source/status/decided_by/approved_user_id/household_person_id/invitation_id`,
every audit `action`, `target_type`, `target_id`, `actor_id` and `meta_json` key named here. **Not a key**: `household_people.kind`
(mutable, above), `audit_logs.at` and `audit_logs.id` order, the address value, `actor_id` (the inviter), the neighbouring `user.enabled`,
`teen_consent.granted` or `guardian_link.confirmed` rows (they identify the actor of *those* rows; picking the one that
belongs to a completion needs time order or adjacency), `account_requests.account_type` and `account.approved`'s `meta.accountType`
(copied from the person at *request* time, `api_household_conversion.go:386`, and the person can change before approval),
`users.account_type` (the job changes it), and the **absence** of a marker in a history not shown to be current (below).

*Cardinality of the request.* Two partial unique indexes on `account_requests` (baseline 2588 and 2595, `source = household_conversion`,
`status = approved`, `approved_user_id IS NOT NULL`) allow **at most one** such request per person `P` and per account `S`.
Approved requests never change status afterwards (the decision at `:526-533` and the expiry at `api_account_requests.go:755` both require `pending`, and a new conversion for `P` is refused while an approved one exists, `api_household_conversion.go:287`), so there is one request
`R` per person for the life of the database, and `S` is created inside the approval transaction (ids are never reused).
Let `D = R.decided_by`, `S = R.approved_user_id`, `P = R.household_person_id`, `I = R.invitation_id`.

**The age-rollover marker.** `rolloverTeen` writes, in the **same transaction** as the `users.account_type` change and
**before** it calls completion, one row `household.person_updated` with `target_type = 'household_person'`, `target_id = P`,
`meta.reason = 'age_rollover'`, `meta.previousKind = 'teen'`, `meta.kind = 'adult'`, no actor and no address
(`age_rollover.go:122-131`). It is written whenever the job changes a staged teen's account type, and it is distinguishable
from every other `household.person_updated` row, which has `target_type = 'household'` and a `fields` list
(`api_profile.go:1417`; those are the only two production writers). `audit_logs` is never pruned (no code or trigger deletes it), and the marker holds no personal key, so
R4-4 leaves it untouched. It is the only way a teen-created account becomes `accountType: adult`:
`users.account_type` is changed on an existing user only by `age_rollover.go:115` and the two guest upgrades
(request approval `api_account_requests.go:559` and invitation acceptance `api_invitations.go:385-398`; both apply only to a user whose status is `guest`, and `S` is created as `pending_consent` and never becomes a guest), no trigger or raw SQL writes it, and
`household_people.kind` is changed on an existing person only by the material-edit path (refused for a staged person) and by
`age_rollover.go:126`. This is a closed-world argument over the writers, not a stored positive key, and the exclusion rule below says so.

**The join from a `registration.changed` row to its `household.person_converted` row.** It starts from the request `R` of
the erased `D` and reads `P`, `S` and `I` from `R`, never from an audit row, so no other person's row is reachable by
construction. Is the join exact? *Traced:*

1. **One writer each.** `household.person_converted` has one production writer (`:646`); a `registration.changed` row with
   `target_type = 'registration'` and the key `invitationId` has one (`:635`). Both sit in `transferHouseholdConversion`,
   which writes every audit row through `audit.Write(tx, …)` in the caller's transaction, so one run's rows commit or roll
   back together.
2. **At most one committed run per person.** `converted_user_id` is written only at `:639`, in the same function. The
   approval path refuses a converted person under the household lock (`:687`); the completion path takes its stage only
   while `converted_user_id IS NULL` (`stagedTeenConversionIn`, `api_teen_conversion.go:37`) and refuses a converted person
   (`:137`). A retry (`errConversionRetry`, a rerun of the age job) is a rolled-back transaction and wrote no audit row.
3. **The tuple names one run.** `S` is created in the approval transaction and ids are never reused; `P` has one approved
   request (above); `I` is that request's invitation. In a history written by the current source, exactly one
   `household.person_converted` row and exactly `transferCount` `registration.changed` rows (distinct `target_id`) carry
   `(P, I, S)`. Registrations added to `S` later are written by `auditRegistration` with `target_type = 'event'` and cannot match.

**What the schema does not enforce.** `audit_logs` has a primary key on `id` and indexes on `action`, `actor_id`, `at` and
`(target_type, target_id)` (baseline 2655-2679); it has no unique or exclusion constraint on any key above, no index on
`meta_json`, and no trigger that rejects an update or delete. So the uniqueness above is a property of the writers, not of
the table, and a restored backup, a manual repair or an older binary could break it. **R4-4 therefore verifies it at
erasure time and never assumes it.** Performance, read not measured: one pass over the `registration.changed` rows by the
`action` index with a `meta_json` filter per erasure (a tuple list for all of `D`'s requests, not one scan per request);
no new index is proposed, and a `meta_json` index would be a schema change for the database/performance review.

**Discovery first, shape second (R40-CORR-10).** The previous candidate counted rows only after a shape filter: `C(R)` left out a
row whose household target or `meta.householdId` disagreed, and `T(R)` left out a row missing one of the two writer keys, and V1 and
V2 then counted the survivors. A malformed duplicate was never counted and never compared. Starting from valid L1 history, add a
second `household.person_converted` row with the same numeric `(P, I, S)` and a mismatched `householdId`: `C(R)` still counted one,
`T(R)` still equalled `transferCount`, V1-V4 could pass, the valid rows were scrubbed and the malformed duplicate kept its address
with no reason code, which broke the all-or-none rule below. An extra transfer row missing a shape key did the same. The two jobs are
now separate: **discovery** finds candidates through the retained keys and the household fallback described below;
**validation** (V0) then judges every discovered row; and any failure retains the **entire discovered group**, valid rows
included. Discovery is bounded by the available keys and action/target filters; it cannot find every possible malformed row.

**Discovery (broad on purpose; a row is a candidate when any listed arm matches).** `H` is the household of `P`, read unscoped from
`household_people` when that row can be read (R4-3 only soft-deletes the person); when it cannot, arm (d) is skipped and the report
says so. Identity comparison is by **text** (`meta_json->>'key' = <id>::text`), so a JSON number or a JSON string
holding the same digits is found; the type is judged later, in V0. Other wrong-type values need a different matching
identity key or the household fallback.

- `CC(R)` (C-candidates): `action = 'household.person_converted'` and one of (a) `meta.personId` text-equals `P`; (b) `meta.userId`
  text-equals `S`; (c) `meta.invitationId` text-equals `I`; (d) `target_type = 'household'` and `target_id = H` and at least one of
  the three identity keys is absent, JSON `null`, or a JSON type other than `number`. For each key this includes
  `jsonb_typeof(meta_json->'key') IS DISTINCT FROM 'number'`, which also covers a missing key. Arm (d) finds a row with
  missing or wrong-type identity data through its household. A valid row of another conversion in `H` carries three
  numeric identity keys and is not drawn in by arm (d); a *malformed* row of another conversion in `H` can be, which
  only makes this conversion retain its discovered group and report it (the retaining direction).
- `CT(R)` (T-candidates): `action = 'registration.changed'` and `target_type = 'registration'` and one of arms (a), (b), (c).
  Event-registration rows (`target_type = 'event'`, written through `auditRegistration`) are never candidates.
- Because `P`, `S` and `I` are each unique to one conversion (the cardinality argument below), any one matching arm ties a row to
  `R` in a history written by the current source. A candidate that then fails V0 is retained with the group; it is never dropped
  from the count.

**What discovery cannot do (stated limits, not a completeness claim).** Identity arms (a)–(c) need at least one
text-equal retained identity key. For a same-household `household.person_converted` row, arm (d) also reaches missing
or non-number identity keys. A `registration.changed` transfer row whose three keys are absent, wrong-typed or
otherwise do not match, and a `household.person_converted` row with no matching key and no qualifying arm (d)
(for example, the household target differs or is unknown, or all three keys are numbers holding other values), can
remain unreachable. The action filters also exclude rows with a different action; `CT(R)` requires
`target_type = 'registration'`. An otherwise malformed transfer row with a different target type is therefore
unreachable residue, not something V0 has checked. In particular, ordinary `registration.changed` rows whose
target type is `event` are excluded even when they share person or user ids; admitting them would mix real event
registrations with conversion transfers. Unreachable rows are not candidates, are never counted and are never touched.
A registration's later owner is not a key (it changes), and neither is `at` or `id` order. The report may say only
"N candidates were found by the stated identity and household rules, M were retained, K were blanked", never "the group
is complete". The text comparison can also find a row for the wrong reason when ids collide across a restored backup;
that lands in the retaining direction. Unreachable personal IPs remain part of IR-4 and block completion under G-9.

**Shape predicates (V0; exact; every condition must hold, applied to every candidate).** A JSON value counts only when it is a
JSON number equal to the id; a missing, string, null or differing value fails.

- `ShapeC` (for each row of `CC(R)`): `target_type = 'household'`; `meta.personId`, `meta.userId`, `meta.invitationId` and
  `meta.householdId` are JSON numbers; they equal `P`, `S`, `I` and (for `householdId`) the row's `target_id`, and `target_id`
  equals `H` when `H` is known. **`accountType` and `transferCount` are not shape conditions here**: a missing `accountType` stays
  `unclassified` in V4 and a bad `transferCount` stays `count_mismatch` in V2, as before.
- `ShapeT` (for each row of `CT(R)`): `target_type = 'registration'`; `meta.personId`, `meta.userId`, `meta.invitationId` are JSON
  numbers equal to `P`, `S`, `I`; and the keys `formerRegisteredBy` and `clearedChildcareRequested` are present (the writer's shape).
  **No condition on `accountType`, `transferCount`, `actor_id`, `ip`, `at` or the registration's current owner.**
- `M(P)`: `action = 'household.person_updated'`, `target_type = 'household_person'`, `target_id = P`, **whatever the meta**
  (wider than the writer's `reason = age_rollover` on purpose: a malformed marker then counts as present, which takes
  the retaining direction).

**Verification, in this order, before any transfer row is blanked.** If a step fails, **every** row of `CC(R) ∪ CT(R)` is
retained (the whole discovered group or none, never some, whether or not an individual row was well formed), and the report names the
reason code of the first step that failed:

| Step | Must hold | Otherwise (reason code) |
|---|---|---|
| V0 | every row of `CC(R)` meets `ShapeC` and every row of `CT(R)` meets `ShapeT` | `malformed_candidate` (the wrong-household C duplicate, the T duplicate missing a shape key, a string or missing identity value). Precedes V1, so a malformed duplicate is never hidden by a passing count |
| V1 | `count(CC(R)) = 1` | `0`: `no_converted_row` (the conversion never completed, or the history lacks it; any `CT(R)` rows found are retained and counted); `2` or more: `duplicate_converted_row` |
| V2 | `C.meta.transferCount` is a non-negative JSON integer, `count(CT(R)) = transferCount`, and the `target_id` of `CT(R)` are pairwise distinct (0 and 0 passes: a person with no registrations) | `count_mismatch` |
| V3 | the history precondition below is established for this database | `history_unestablished` (the default) |
| V4 | `C.meta.accountType` is the JSON string `adult` and `count(M(P)) = 0` | `accountType = teen`, or `adult` with `count(M(P)) ≥ 1`: `completion`; the key missing or any other value: `unclassified` |

Only when V0-V4 all hold are `CC(R)` and `CT(R)` an approval transfer, and then their `ip` is blanked **by the list of
`audit_logs.id` just verified**, never by a second query that could drift. `CT(R)` inherits its class from `CC(R)`; it has
none of its own to read. The verification reads no `ip`, so a second erasure run reaches the same set.

**Counts the report must give, per request `R` of the erased `D`: the actual numbers, never `1 + transferCount` assumed.** `|CC(R)|`
and `|CT(R)|` as discovered; `C.meta.transferCount` as stored (or "not an integer"); the retained row counts, which equal the
discovered counts on any failure and are `0` on success; the blanked count, which is `1` and `transferCount` on success because V1
and V2 require it; the reason code; and whether arm (d) was skipped because `H` could not be read. A duplicated or missing `C`
therefore reports `2` or `0` retained `C` rows, not an assumed `1`.

**The sibling selectors follow the same rule (Consistency).** The teen `guardian_link.requested` rows of `R` are discovered by `action
= 'guardian_link.requested'`, `meta.source` text `household_conversion` and `meta.teenUserId` text-equal to `S`, then validated
(`target_type = 'guardian_link'`, `teenUserId` a JSON number equal to `S`). They have no count relation to each other (0 or one per
distinct guardian), so a malformed one is retained and reported on its own (`malformed_candidate`) and does not hold back its valid
siblings. A row with `source = household_conversion` and no `teenUserId` cannot be reached by `S` and is the same stated limit as
above. `account.approved` is selected by the exact `target_id = R.id`, which has no metadata shape to get wrong.

**The history precondition (V3; gate G-8).** V4 states a fact about audit rows only for a history written by binaries that
already had **both** the staged teen conversion (commit `93fdcb1`, Oct 22, 2026; before it a teen person's conversion was
refused, `errConversionTeen`, so older history holds adult approvals only) and the age-rollover marker (first added by
commit `0bfafdc`, the same evening, by `git log -S`; `age_rollover.go:118-131`). A history written by a binary that had the
first but not the second can hold a post-birthday completion with `accountType = adult` and no marker, which V4 would call
an adult approval, **blanking an address that belongs to the completer** (the harmful direction). Whether a binary in
that window ever served a database that still exists is not known. No release tag exists at this commit, so the
older-binary population is probably only developer databases, but this file does not know that. No stored row records which binary wrote a row, and the order of
`audit_logs.at` or `id` cannot establish it without using time as a key, which is forbidden here. So:

- **Default: not established.** Every transfer row is retained and reported `history_unestablished`. This is the
  documented limit and **not complete erasure**: the erased approving admin's address stays on the discovered group (normally `1 +
  transferCount` rows; the report gives the counts). It must appear in the erasure report and the dialog copy (R4-6), not be hidden.
- It could become established only by one of two routes, and **neither is approved or verified**. (a) An operator's recorded
  statement that the audit history of this database was written entirely by binaries at or after `0bfafdc`: the recording
  mechanism is an R4-4 design question for review, none is designed here, and no one has said who may make the statement or how
  it would be kept. (b) A database-level provenance fact that R4-4 proves against the real migration runner and the baseline and a
  reviewer accepts. The candidate for (b), "the database was created by the generated baseline", is **not established here**:
  the version table alone cannot tell, because a later numbered file may reuse a number the frozen chain used
  (`TestBaselineFileShape` allows numbered files after the baseline), and no one has checked it against the runner. Neither route
  has been tested against an actual database or any operational history; no database was inspected. Each needs its own evidence and
  review before it can switch the transfer rule on, and a reviewer's signature on this file does not approve either.
- **A missing marker never implies an adult approval by itself.** In a history not shown to be current it means unknown.
- R4-4 ships with the transfer-row rule disabled (retention) until G-8 is dispositioned. The rest of R4-4 does not wait on it.
  **That is staged slice progress, not completion of M7.4**: even with G-8 established, the completion rows remain
  (6.5.2, gate G-9).

**The audit rows of a conversion, by their own retained keys.**

| Row | Selector (every condition) | Written when / by | Caller identified? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `account.approved` | `action`, `target_type = 'account_request'`, `target_id = R.id` (also `actor_id = D`) | the approval transaction, always, exactly 1 | **Yes, `D`**, through `R.decided_by` |
| teen `guardian_link.requested` | discovered by `action`, `meta.source` text `household_conversion`, `meta.teenUserId` text-equal to `S`; valid when `target_type = 'guardian_link'` and `teenUserId` is a JSON number (a malformed row is retained and reported on its own) | the approval transaction only | **Yes, `D`** |
| `household.person_converted` (one per completed conversion) | `CC(R)`, then V0-V4 | the approval transaction (adult person), or a later completion (teen person) | **Yes, `D`, by exclusion** when V0-V4 hold; otherwise no |
| `registration.changed` transfer rows (`N` = `C.meta.transferCount`) | `CT(R)` joined to `CC(R)`, then V0-V4 | the same transaction as its `household.person_converted` row | inherits the class of `CC(R)`; none of its own |

Classes of the `household.person_converted` row (the `registration.changed` rows follow it):

| Class | Condition | Written when / by | Caller identified? |
|---|---|---|---|
| **approval transfer** (adult person) | V0-V4: unique, `adult`, no `M(P)` row, history established | the approval transaction | **Yes, `D`, by exclusion** |
| **completion, teen account type** | `accountType = teen` | a later completion while `S` is still a teen (13-17) | **No.** Link-deciding admin, enabling admin, consenting admin or guardian |
| **completion, after the birthday** | `accountType = adult` and `count(M(P)) ≥ 1` | the job, or the enabling admin when the account was suspended | **Partly.** Empty address if the job; otherwise the enabling admin, whose identity no key stores |
| **unclassified** | key missing or other, history unestablished, a discovered row malformed, `C` not unique, or count mismatch | unknown | **No** |

*`guardian_link.requested` with `source = household_conversion`* needs no kind and no history: `requestTeenGuardian` has three call sites
(`api_teen_guardians.go:60` `signup`, `:112` `claim`, `api_household_conversion.go:733` `household_conversion`), the third
runs only in the teen branch of `approveHouseholdConversion`, which runs once per `R` (the `pending → approved` update at
`:526-533` is a compare-and-set), `S` is created in that transaction so no link for it can exist earlier, and a repeated
(`guardian`, `teen`) pair conflict-skips and writes no audit row (`:64-65`). So the row, `meta.source` plus `meta.teenUserId = S`
with `S = R.approved_user_id`, is approval-only, 0 or more per `R` (one per distinct qualifying guardian), and its address is `D`'s.
This join is independent of any mutable kind or account type. It also has `actor_id = inviter`, and so do the transfer rows of
both kinds, so **R4-4 must exclude all of these rows from the ordinary `actor_id` rule** (rows whose address is another
party's): otherwise an erased inviter would blank `D`'s address, or a completer's.

*Why the approval transfer is attributed to `D` only by exclusion.* There is no stored key that *positively* says "this
transfer ran at approval". What is stored is enough to rule completion out, given V3. A completion happens only for a teen-created
`S`. Such an account is `accountType: teen` until the job changes it, so a completion before the birthday records
`teen`; and the job writes the marker whenever it changes it, so a completion after the birthday records `adult` **and has
a marker**. A `household.person_converted` row with `accountType: adult` and **no** marker therefore cannot be a completion, so it is the approval of
an adult person, and the address is `D`'s. The converse never misfires either: an adult approval has no marker, because the marker
requires a staged teen request for `P` and `P` has only one request. This conclusion depends on (a) the writer lists above, (b)
the marker not being removed, (c) the history precondition (V3), and (d) V0, V1 and V2: the join's uniqueness and counts.
R4-4 also carries a source-guard test (below) that fails the build when any new writer of the guarded columns or either
transfer shape appears, so the argument cannot silently decay.

*What stays unattributed, and exactly why.* The completion kinds and the unclassified rows in the tables are **retained, as the O-19 attribution
limit**. The evidence:
- Before the birthday the completer is one of four: an admin confirming a link, an admin enabling the account, an admin
  recording consent, or a confirmed guardian recording consent. The row stores none of them, and the inviter is
  `actor_id`. Any of them may be the same person as `D` (an admin approves, then enables), as the inviter (an admin who
  also holds a seat), or as a guardian. Therefore the row's address can be `D`'s, the inviter's, a guardian's or another
  admin's, and **none of that can be told from stored keys**.
- After the birthday the job's rows carry an empty address (nothing to scrub, nothing lost). If the account was suspended,
  only an admin Enable can complete (above), so the owner is "the admin who enabled `S`". That admin is not
  `D` unless the same person did both, and the row does not say. The `user.enabled` rows for `S` carry the actors, but there
  can be several, one per earlier suspension, and choosing the right one needs time order, which is not a key.
- In a database whose history is not shown to be current (V3), or where a discovered row is malformed or the join is not unique or does not count out (V0,
  V1, V2), the approving admin is probably the owner of the transfer rows and **cannot be shown to be**. The rows are retained
  with their reason code. They are not claimed to be scrubbed and not claimed to belong to anyone.
- The rows therefore keep their address **whoever is erased**: an erased `D` (when `D` also completed, or when the rows
  could not be proven), an erased enabling admin, an erased consenting guardian, an erased inviter. This is a documented
  loss of attribution, not an accountability retention exception and not a claim about the rows' sensitivity. Their counts
  are reported per erasure and per reason code, as the actual discovered and retained numbers (normally one `household.person_converted` row and `transferCount` `registration.changed` rows per conversion).
- **This is residue, and it gates M7.4's completion (G-9, 6.5.2).** A forward-only audit key naming the caller, written by new
  code, would let a later selector aim at the right person, but it cannot reach rows that exist, it stores one more identifier, and
  it is **not shown to be the only remedy**: 6.5.2 lists the other routes found (including limited minimization of the
  non-authorizing `ip` field that needs no attribution), with what each would change and what each lacks. None is proposed
  or approved here.

*Lifecycles, and what each leaves behind* (all traced; each is a required R4-4 fixture). `T` rows never carry `accountType`; the column below is the `household.person_converted` row:

| Path | Rows written | `C.meta.accountType` | Marker | Address on transfer rows | Selected for erased `D` (history established) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| L1 adult person approved | `account.approved`, transfer rows | `adult` | none | `D` | `account.approved`, `C`, `T` |
| L2 teen approved, completes before 18 (last of consent, link confirmation, Enable) | `account.approved`, `guardian_link.requested` ×G at approval; transfer rows later | `teen` | none | the completer's | `account.approved` and the `guardian_link.requested` rows only |
| L3 teen turns 18, not suspended | as L2, plus marker, transfer rows in the job | `adult` | yes | `""` | `account.approved` and the `guardian_link.requested` rows (transfer rows hold nothing) |
| L4 teen turns 18 while suspended, later Enable by `E` | as L2, plus marker at the birthday, transfer rows at Enable | `adult` | yes | `E`'s | `account.approved` and the `guardian_link.requested` rows only. **Not** `E`'s transfer rows |
| L5 teen suspended before 18, enabled by `E` at 13-17 | as L2 | `teen` | none | `E`'s | as L2 |
| L6 any path, history **not** established (the default), or a discovered row malformed, or `C` not unique, or count mismatch | as the path | any | any | `D`, the completer or empty: unknown | `account.approved` and the `guardian_link.requested` rows only (a malformed link row is retained alone); **the whole discovered group of transfer rows is retained and reported** with the reason code and the actual counts |

At the time of erasure `household_people.kind` of `P` is `teen` in L2 and L5 and `adult` in L3 and L4, and in L1. The selector
must give the same answer in every case, so `P.kind` is irrelevant by construction.

R4-4 builds `DR(D)`, the requests with `source = household_conversion`, `status = approved`, `decided_by = D`, and applies the
tables to each. `meta.guardianUserId` is not a condition (the address is `D`'s, not the guardian's). The counts are test
assertions and reported numbers; a conversion that fails V0-V4 is retained whole, not aborted. In the matrix below "keep (limit)"
means the G-9 residue (6.5.2): staged and reported, not accepted.

| Erased user | `account.approved` and teen `guardian_link.requested` of `R` | approval transfer rows (L1; only when V0-V4 hold) | completion or unproven transfer rows (L2-L6) | `account_requests.ip` of `R` (T01, Class B) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `D`, the approving admin (distinct from `inviter`, `S`) | **blank** | **blank** | **keep (limit)** | keep (the redeemer's) |
| `inviter` | keep (`D`'s) | keep (`D`'s) | keep (limit) | keep (the inviter did not make it) |
| `S`, the converted person | keep (`D`'s) | keep (`D`'s) | keep (limit) | blank (Proposed, O-19) |
| a guardian `G` of a teen `S` | keep | n/a | keep (limit) | not affected |
| `E`, an enabling or consenting admin who is not `D` | keep | keep | **keep (limit)** | not affected |
| `D` who is also the inviter | **blank** | **blank** | keep (limit) | keep |
| `D` who is also the enabler (`D = E`) | **blank** | **blank** | **keep (limit)**: the address may be theirs and no key says | keep |

*R4-4 tests.* One scratch database; the **primary** fixtures are **built through the real handlers** (the redeem, admin approve, link
confirm, consent, admin Enable, and `rolloverAges` calls), never by hand-inserting audit rows, so no impossible combination of keys can
be seeded (for example an adult approval with a marker). Seven distinct users with distinct documentation-range addresses, so a
wrong owner is visible: inviter `192.0.2.1`; approving admin `D` `192.0.2.2`; the redeemer (who is not a user at that time) `192.0.2.3`;
guardian `G` `192.0.2.4`; enabling admin `E` `192.0.2.5`; link-deciding admin `L` `192.0.2.6`; consenting admin `K` `192.0.2.7`.
The only permitted hand edits are in the labelled **fallback fixtures** (tests 10 and 11): they start from a real-writer fixture and
change stored rows after the fact, each marked as a simulated anomaly, because the real writers cannot produce the shapes
being tested. Every test that erases `D` runs once with the history precondition established and once with it left at the default.

1. **Writer shape (L1, the defect).** An adult person with two registrations, `D`, inviter and subject all distinct,
   history established. First assert the stored shapes: every `registration.changed` transfer row has `meta` keys exactly
   `personId`, `userId`, `formerRegisteredBy`, `clearedChildcareRequested`, `invitationId` and **no** `accountType` or
   `transferCount`; the one `household.person_converted` row has `accountType = adult`, `transferCount = 2`, and no marker exists. Then
   erasing `D` blanks exactly `account.approved`, that `household.person_converted` row and the two `registration.changed`
   rows (by id), each on `192.0.2.2`; erasing the inviter, `S` and `G` each blank none of them.
2. **L2.** Teen, two confirmed guardians, completes at 17 by link decision `L`: the `registration.changed` rows lack `accountType`, the
   `household.person_converted` row says `teen`; erasing `D` blanks `account.approved` and the two `guardian_link.requested` rows and leaves
   all transfer rows byte-identical, and so does erasing `L`, `G`, the inviter and `S`. The report counts the retained rows with
   reason `completion`. Repeat with completion by consent `K` and by guardian `G`.
3. **L3 direct birthday conversion.** Staged teen, not suspended, `rolloverAges` at the birthday. Assert the marker exists, the `household.person_converted` row has
   `accountType: adult`, the transfer rows' address is empty, `P.kind` is `adult`, and erasing `D` blanks `account.approved` and the two link rows
   (with `P.kind` adult, the failure mode of the third-round candidate), selects no transfer row, and reports them `completion`.
4. **L4 suspended, then Enable by a different admin.** `D` approves, an admin suspends, the birthday passes, a **distinct** `E` enables. Assert the transfer rows carry `192.0.2.5`, `P.kind` is `adult`, the marker exists, the
   `registration.changed` rows lack `accountType`. Erasing `D` blanks `192.0.2.2` on `account.approved` and the link rows and **leaves `192.0.2.5`**; erasing `E` leaves `192.0.2.5`; the report counts the rows as `completion`.
5. **L5.** Suspended at 16, enabled by `E` at 16: same assertions as 2, with no marker.
6. **Distinct inviter, approver and subject with a prior registration.** The person already has a cancelled and a past registration
   before conversion: every registration the writer moved is counted by `transferCount`, `CT(R)` matches it exactly, and a registration
   `S` makes after conversion (target `event`) is never selected.
7. **Coincident identities.** `D` is also the inviter; `D` is also the enabler (`D = E`, L4 and L5): erasing `D` blanks the approval rows and **keeps** the completion rows.
8. **Two conversions in one household** approved by `D1` and `D2`: erasing `D1` touches only `R1`'s rows, by `audit_logs.id`. An unrelated
   row of **another actor, action or conversion that happens to carry `192.0.2.2`** is untouched (the selection never compares the address).
9. **Actor rule exclusion.** Erasing the inviter, whose `actor_id` is on every conversion row, leaves every conversion row's address unchanged.
10. **History not established (fallback, default).** Run L1 and L3 with the precondition left unset: erasing `D` blanks `account.approved` and the
    link rows, **retains `C` and `T` of L1** with reason `history_unestablished`, and the report states that the erasure is not complete for them.
    Then simulate a database an older binary served: delete the marker from an L3 fixture. With the default the L3 transfer rows are still retained (this is
    the markerless adult row that the exclusion would misread as an approval). With the precondition set, the same row *is* treated as an approval: that is
    the operator's assertion and it is the only thing the test lets change the outcome, so the mechanism that sets it is the reviewed surface (G-8).
11. **Ambiguous join (simulated).** From L1: (a) copy the `household.person_converted` row (two matches, both well formed) → the whole discovered group retained (2 `C` rows and the `T` rows, not an assumed `1 + N`), `duplicate_converted_row`;
    (b) delete it → retained, `no_converted_row`; (c) delete or copy one `registration.changed` row → retained, `count_mismatch`;
    (d) change `transferCount` to a string or remove it → retained, `count_mismatch`; (e) remove `accountType` → `unclassified`; (f) add a second
    `household.person_updated` row on the person with a different shape → `completion`. In every case the report is exact and the rows are byte-identical.
12. **Idempotence and retry.** Erase `D` twice, and interrupt the first run before commit and rerun: same selected id list, same final rows, same counts.
    Erase `D`, then the inviter, then `S`, in each order: the same final state.
13. **Selector inputs and metadata unchanged** after the scrub: `decided_by`, `approved_user_id`, `invitation_id`, `household_person_id`, `source`, `status`; the marker's `meta_json`; in `household.person_converted`
    `personId`, `householdId`, `invitationId`, `userId`, `accountType`, `transferCount`; in `registration.changed` transfer rows `personId`, `userId`, `invitationId`, `formerRegisteredBy`, `clearedChildcareRequested`;
    in link rows `source`, `teenUserId`. The `meta_json`, `action`, `target_*` and `actor_id` of every audit row and the three authorization snapshots in full are byte-identical, and the registration access trigger path still passes.
14. **Source guard.** A parse-only test lists the only production writers of `users.account_type` and `household_people.kind` on an existing row (`age_rollover.go`, the two guest upgrades, the profile material edit), of
    `household.person_converted` and of `registration.changed` with `target_type = 'registration'`, and pins the key set of both transfer shapes; it fails when another writer appears or a key is added or removed, and the message tells the author to revisit 6.5.1.
15. **Candidate discovery versus shape (R40-CORR-10, labelled fallback fixtures).** Each starts from a real-writer L1 fixture (adult
    person, two registrations, history established, `D` erased) and applies one marked anomaly by hand, because the real writers cannot produce
    it. Each case states its expected result; when a malformed candidate is discovered, **no row of `CC(R) ∪ CT(R)` is blanked**, valid or not.
    Reports give the reason and actual discovered and retained counts, describe the bounded discovery rules, and never claim completeness.
    Every retained row is byte-identical, including `ip`.
    - **15a Bad-household C duplicate.** Add a second `household.person_converted` row with the same numeric `(P, I, S)`, `meta.householdId`
      different from its `target_id` (and, in a second run, a `target_id` of another household). Expected `malformed_candidate`, `|CC| = 2`, retained `C` rows `2`
      (not `1`), retained `T` rows `2`. V1 alone would have said `duplicate_converted_row`; V0 comes first and the test pins that order.
    - **15b Missing-shape-key T duplicate.** Add a third `registration.changed` row (`target_type = 'registration'`, tuple `(P, I, S)`) with
      `formerRegisteredBy` removed, and a second run with `clearedChildcareRequested` removed. Expected `malformed_candidate`, `|CT| = 3`, retained `T` rows `3`. A
      variant with both keys present and a repeated `target_id` is valid in shape and expected `count_mismatch`.
    - **15c Missing or invalid identity metadata.** (i) A second C row with `invitationId` removed (found by arms (a) and (b)), and a third with
      all three identity keys removed and `target_id = H` (found by arm (d) alone): each `malformed_candidate`.
      (ii) A T row whose `userId` is the string `"<S>"`: found by text comparison, `malformed_candidate`. (iii) A T row whose `personId` is `null`
      but `invitationId` and `userId` match: found, `malformed_candidate`. (iv) A C row with `transferCount` as a string but valid
      identity: not malformed in V0, `count_mismatch` in V2 (the prior behavior is preserved).
    - **15d Undiscoverable row (the stated limit).** A `registration.changed` row (`target_type = 'registration'`) with all three identity keys removed: not a
      candidate, not counted, not touched, **and the valid group is blanked as in L1** because nothing found contradicts it. The test asserts
      the report's wording claims only "found by the stated identity and household rules", never completeness, and asserts the row's `ip` is unchanged. This
      fixture documents what discovery cannot do; it is not a pass for the property, and the row is part of the unmeasurable residue named in 6.5.2.
      A second variant gives a malformed transfer row `target_type = 'event'`: it is also unreachable, while a normal event-registration
      row sharing person or user ids stays untouched. Neither variant is counted as checked by V0.
    - **15e Unrelated rows.** Rows that must be neither discovered nor changed, each carrying `192.0.2.2`: an event-registration
      `registration.changed` (`target_type = 'event'`) with `personId = P`; a complete, valid conversion (`CC`, `CT`) of another request in the same household; the
      other `D`'s rows; an `account.approved` of another request. Expected: unchanged, uncounted, and the group of `R` unaffected by them.
      A *malformed* row of another conversion in `H` that arm (d) draws in is the one accepted exception: it makes `R` retain its group and is reported.
    - **15f Link-row sibling.** A teen fixture with two guardians where one `guardian_link.requested` row has `teenUserId` as a string: that row
      is retained and reported alone, the valid sibling is blanked, and no transfer row changes.
    - **15g Exact pre/post comparison.** For 15a-15f and 15i, a snapshot taken before and after the erasure must show byte-identical `meta_json`, `action`, `target_type`,
      `target_id`, `actor_id`, `at` of every audit row, the selector inputs of test 13 (`decided_by`, `approved_user_id`, `invitation_id`,
      `household_person_id`, `source`, `status`), the three R9 authorization snapshots (`occurrence_access.added`, `occurrence_access.ended`,
      `occurrence.blocked`) in full, and `ip` changed on exactly the audit ids the report lists as blanked, with the registration access trigger path run after the scrub.
    - **15h Retry and order.** Erasing `D` twice, after an interrupted run, and in the order `D`, inviter, `S`, gives the same discovered, retained and blanked
      id lists for each anomaly; the second run reaches the same set because discovery reads no `ip`.
    - **15i All identity keys wrong-typed at the household.** Add a second `household.person_converted` row with
      `target_type = 'household'`, `target_id = H`, numeric `householdId = H`, and `personId = {}`,
      `userId = []`, `invitationId = false`. Arm (d) finds it even though none of arms (a)–(c) matches.
      V0 reports `malformed_candidate`; both C rows and both T rows are retained, with their actual counts.
      A complete valid conversion in `H` remains outside the candidate group and unchanged.

#### 6.5.2 The personal IP residue is a completion gate for M7.4 (G-9; R40-PRIV-09)

*Status of everything in this subsection: a gate and a list of alternatives found in source, plus one bounded owner authorization
(owner decision 5, below). No coder and no reviewer has chosen among the alternatives, approved any implementation or design, or waived
the gate, and none can: the choice changes how long personal data stays and whose records are touched, which PLAN DEC-144, the
owner's instruction and the safeguarding reviewer's evidence judgment decide. The owner has authorized the **concept** of P2
within a stated scope; nothing is approved beyond that, and **G-9 is OPEN**.*

**Slice progress is not milestone completion.** R4-4 may merge with the transfer rule disabled, and R4-1 to R4-3 and the other
slices may proceed, each described in its PR, STATUS and PLAN as *staged*, once R4-0 is signed. What is blocked is declaring
**M7.4 complete** (STATUS "done", PLAN milestone closed, the brief's acceptance boxes ticked). The same shape as 7.7's
suppression staging, which already says M7.4 is not complete until hashed suppression exists or an authorized PLAN change says otherwise.

**The residue the gate covers** (personal IP addresses that R4-4 would leave on `audit_logs.ip`, each reported by count and reason):

| # | Residue | Why it stays |
|---|---|---|
| IR-1 | **C-completion rows**: the `household.person_converted` row and its `registration.changed` rows written when a staged teen conversion completes later (6.5.1) | no stored key names the caller: link-deciding admin, enabling admin, consenting admin or guardian, or the job (empty address, nothing to scrub) |
| IR-2 | **Transfer rows of an unestablished history**: the same two shapes when G-8 is not established (the default) | the rule cannot tell an adult approval from a markerless old completion; neither G-8 route is verified or approved (6.5.1) |
| IR-3 | **Groups retained whole** by V0, V1 or V2: a malformed, duplicated, missing or count-mismatched candidate | fail-closed (6.5.1); also a malformed teen `guardian_link.requested` row, retained alone |
| IR-4 | **Rows no retained key reaches** (the stated limit of discovery) | not countable; the report cannot give a number and claims none |

**The gate (G-9).** M7.4 is **not complete** while any of IR-1 to IR-4 can remain an erased person's personal address in
`audit_logs`, **unless the owner authorizes an exact, named PLAN disposition** that says which rows and which data remain, whose
addresses they may be, why, how it is reported to the admin, and, if that is what it is, that it is an exception to DEC-144's
"anonymous tombstone" wording. A reviewer's acceptance of O-19 or of this file, the existence of the report, and the count of rows
do not satisfy it. A disposition that merely says the inventory names the residue does not satisfy it either: DEC-144 asks the
inventory to name what stays **and** limits what may stay to anonymous material needed for audit and safeguarding integrity, and 6.5 shows an
IP address on these rows is read by nothing and authorizes nothing.

**What is traced about the field** (so the alternatives below can be judged; all read from source, none run):

- *No consumer.* No Go query selects or filters `audit_logs.ip` (6.5); the admin contact history lists named columns without it
  (`api_contact_history.go:225-236`); the full-row loader `registration_subjects.go:572-600` reads `MetaJSON` and `TargetID` only; no
  trigger or constraint is on `audit_logs` (6.1); the column is nullable text and the writer stores `''` for no address (`internal/audit/audit.go:380-396`).
  Authority reads `meta_json` of the access snapshots (6.7), not `ip`. So blanking `ip` is **technically** possible without touching
  a decision, an actor, a target, a time or a snapshot.
- *Every live completion caller already writes its own copy.* The completion call and a named-actor step row are written in one
  transaction with the same `c.ClientIP()`: a guardian-link decision (`api_admin.go:414` then `:421-422`, actor `me`), an Enable
  (`api_admin.go:789-790` then `:797`; the `user.enabled` row exists only when the account was disabled) and a consent record
  (`api_teen_consent.go:154` then `:162-163`, actor the granter). So for those callers the address on the conversion rows repeats
  an address already stored on a row attributed by `actor_id`, which the Class A rule (6.5) blanks when that actor is erased
