  (subject to O-4 for safeguarding actions). The job passes `""` (`age_rollover.go:181`). **Not shown:** that every completion
  reached through `api_admin.go:797` is an Enable. That call runs after any admin update of the account, and an update other than an
  Enable writes no `user.enabled` row; whether such an update can complete a conversion was not traced.
- *The candidate callers are derivable from retained keys, without time order.* `guardian_links.confirmed_by` for the teen's links
  (T12), `teen_consents.granted_by` for the teen (T36, kept byte for byte), `user.enabled` audit rows with `target_id = S` (their
  `actor_id`), `D` (`decided_by`) and the inviter. That is a set of people who **could** have been the caller, not the caller, and
  it is not proven complete because of the `api_admin.go:797` gap above.
- *What is not on the `ip` column.* The decision, the purpose, the subjects, the actors, the time and the provenance of a conversion
  (its identity tuple, `decided_by`, the step rows, `teen_consents`, `guardian_links`) are in other fields that stay. The log is not
  tamper-evident (DEC-33's durable chain is cut, DEC-140, `PLAN.md:2138`), so no hash or signature depends on `ip` bytes.
- *Not settled by source:* whether an address is "essential safeguarding evidence" on a conversion of a minor. PLAN says rows
  about contact access, minors, consent and roles "are never purged" (`PLAN.md:367`, `:553`); that sentence is about rows, and
  whether it also forbids blanking one column of such a row is a reading the owner must confirm (6.5 already proposes blanking `ip`
  on other rows of those kinds, under the same question). The safeguarding reviewer, not this file, says whether any action's `ip`
  is essential evidence; if so, that is an owner/PLAN retention exception (6.5, O-6).

**Owner decision 5: the one scope the owner has authorized (Nov 3, 2026; question and answer verbatim in section 1).** The owner
answered **"Yes, clear those IPs (Recommended)"** to the question whether, when an erased person may have caused a household
conversion, Fold may clear the IP from those conversion audit entries even if it might belong to another living participant,
provided it is not essential safeguarding evidence, with decisions, actors, dates and provenance remaining. What this settles and what it does not:

| Settled by the owner (authority for P2's concept, in this scope only) | Not settled, not authorized, still gated |
|---|---|
| Fold **may** clear **only the `ip` field** on identified household-conversion audit entries (6.5.1's `household.person_converted` and `registration.changed` rows) when the erased person may have caused the conversion, **even where the address may be a different living participant's** | any approved implementation, R4-4 design, selector, migration or test (review of the exact design stays required, DG-9 item 3) |
| the proviso: **not** if it is **essential safeguarding evidence**; decisions, actors, dates and provenance (identity tuple, `decided_by`, `meta_json`, the authorization snapshots) **remain** | the safeguarding reviewer's **per-action** judgment that the `ip` on each such action is not essential evidence (DG-9 item 2); no reviewer has made it |
| the owner accepted that some hard-to-identify records still need a separate solution | **blanket** blanking of every person's audit `ip`, or population-wide minimization (that is P3, still undecided), or coarsening (P4) |
| | any change to the **separate step rows of living people** (`user.enabled`, `guardian_link.confirmed`, `teen_consent.granted`, a consenting or link-deciding actor's own row); those keep their addresses under the Class A rule (6.5) |
| | a claim that the candidate-set, history or action-filter approach is **complete**: the candidate set is not proven complete (`api_admin.go:797`, above), rows outside discovery are unreachable (IR-4), and a malformed or ambiguous discovered group (IR-3) is not dispositioned by the answer |
| | G-8 (the history precondition stays **unestablished**; the answer is not a G-8 route) and **G-9, which stays OPEN** |

What this does to the residue, stated without overclaiming: **if** a reviewed, implemented R4-4 design applied this scope, IR-1 and
IR-2 would be reduced for groups where the erased person is a possible caller, and the erased person's own address on them would be
cleared; IR-3 and IR-4 would not be reached as such. Until then nothing changes in the default: the whole discovered group is
retained and reported (M43 and the R4-4 slice row describe the current proposal and are unchanged). The answer does **not**
satisfy G-9 on its own: DG-9 still needs the safeguarding judgment and the independent review of the design, and the gate also
still covers any residue the scope does not reach unless the owner authorizes an exact, named PLAN disposition for it.

**Alternatives found.** Technical feasibility (above) is not authorization. Each of the first four
changes at least one person's stored data other than the erased person's; **this draft does not widen erasure to other persons'
records beyond owner decision 5's scope for P2**, and each other alternative would need an explicit scope and approval first. Of
P1 to P5, **only P2's concept has an owner scope** (above); **no alternative is an approved implementation or design**, and none is
recommended by this file.

| Option | What it would do | Whose data changes | Limits found | Needs, before it is more than an idea |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **P1** Named retention disposition | Keep IR-1 to IR-4 and say so in the PLAN, in the admin report and in the delete dialog | none beyond today (the erased person's address stays) | contradicts DEC-144's tombstone-only wording unless the owner amends or excepts it; the residue size depends on history nobody has counted; not a privacy outcome, a disclosure | the owner's exact, named PLAN text (the gate above) |
| **P2** Erasure-triggered minimization over the *candidate set* (**concept owner-authorized, in the scope above only; design unapproved**) | When the erased person is any possible caller of a conversion (the keys above), blank `ip` on the whole discovered group (`CC`, `CT`) **without deciding who was the caller**. It needs no attribution and, because it does not depend on the class of the row, no G-8 for the erased person's own groups | the erased person's address, and the copy of any *other* possible caller's address on those rows (which, for the three live callers, also stays on that caller's own step row) | (1) it touches living people's records: the owner has now authorized the scope (clear only `ip`, on identified conversion entries, only if not essential safeguarding evidence), but a reviewed design is still required; (2) over-inclusive by construction: a living caller loses a duplicate; (3) the candidate set is not proven complete (`api_admin.go:797`), so an erased admin outside it keeps their address and IR-1 is reduced, not closed; (4) IR-3's malformed groups need the same discovery rule (6.5.1) to be reachable at all; (5) the safeguarding reviewer must say the `ip` on `household.person_converted` and its transfer rows is not essential evidence, and R4-4's exact tests (15, 13) must prove the decisions and snapshots stay byte-identical | ~~owner scope decision~~ **given (owner decision 5)**; safeguarding-reviewer judgment per action (not given); a reviewed R4-4 design (not given) |
| **P3** Population-wide minimization, independent of any erasure | Stop recording the caller's address on the completion-call rows (forward-only, a writer change) and/or blank that field on existing proven-completion rows, for everyone, whenever a retention policy says so | every living caller of every completion | an audit-retention policy for all callers, not an erasure act; touches the writer and PLAN 5.3's field list; historical rows that cannot be proven completion (IR-2) are not reached; the forward part changes nothing already stored | owner decision on policy and scope; PLAN edit; the safeguarding judgment above; a reviewed writer change |
| **P4** Coarsening instead of blanking (for example IPv4 to /24 and IPv6 to /64, which login throttling already does for IPv6, `PLAN.md:1033`) | Keep a network-level hint of provenance, drop the host | the same people as P2 or P3, partially | a truncated address may still be personal data; it is not erasure and nothing here shows it satisfies DEC-144 | owner/privacy decision that coarsened is acceptable; otherwise it does not close the gate |
| **P5** Forward-only caller key | New code stores which person ran a completion step, so a later selector can aim at the right person | stores one more identifier per new completion row | cannot reach any row that already exists (so IR-1 and IR-2 remain for old rows unless combined with P1-P4); adds personal-data surface of its own; it is the route this draft previously called the only remedy, and **that claim is withdrawn: P2 to P4 need no attribution** | owner/privacy decision, a schema or `meta_json` shape change, and review |

The options combine (for example P5 for new rows with P2 or P1 for old ones). This draft does not prefer one. It records what the
source supports and what it does not. Owner decision 5 chose P2's concept for a bounded scope; it did not choose among the others,
and P3 (population-wide), P4 (coarsening) and P5 (forward key) remain undecided.

**Decision gate DG-9 (concrete, so earlier work does not wait).** Before M7.4 is declared complete, all of the following are
recorded in the PR or the PLAN by the named decider:

1. *Owner:* either an exact named retention disposition (P1) or the scope of any minimization the owner approves (which persons'
   records may change, which actions, forward-only or historical, blank or coarsen), written into the PLAN. **Partly recorded:** the
   owner's P2 scope (owner decision 5) is recorded here and in the M7.4 entry of `PLAN.md`: clear only `ip`, on identified
   household-conversion entries where the erased person may have caused the conversion, only if not essential safeguarding evidence,
   preserving decisions, actors, dates and provenance. It covers no other action, no living person's separate step rows, and no
   population-wide policy. It leaves any residue it does not reach (IR-3, IR-4, rows outside the candidate set) without an owner
   disposition, so item 1 is **not complete**.
2. *Safeguarding reviewer:* for each action named in IR-1 to IR-3, whether its `ip` is essential safeguarding evidence, with the
   reason; a yes on any action is itself an owner/PLAN retention decision (6.5, O-6).
3. *Independent reviewers (privacy, security, correctness):* review of the chosen option's exact R4-4 design and tests, including 15
   and 13, and, only if the option needs a class, a G-8 route with its evidence (6.5.1).

Items 2 and 3 are **not done**, and item 1 is partial, so **G-9 is OPEN** and still blocks declaring M7.4 complete. Until then the report's counts are the residue statement, the delete dialog (R4-6) says plainly that some network addresses can
remain in the activity log, and STATUS and PLAN say "staged". Whether and when to put the rest of DG-9 to the owner is not decided in this
file; the gate stands whether or not it is asked.

### 6.6 Everything that reads audit rows

| Reader | What it reads | Class |
|---|---|---|
| Trigger `registration_access_provenance` (baseline `144-343`) | `action IN ('occurrence_access.added','occurrence_access.ended')`, `target_type='registration'`, `target_id`, and meta `subjectUserId`, `occurrenceId`, `grantedAtMicro`, `origin`, `issuerId`, `requestId`, `departureMembershipId`, `departureActorId`, `endedAtMicro`, `endReason`, `endedBy` | **authority** |
| `loadAccessHistory` `registration_access.go:324-370`, shared corruption predicate `accessSnapshotValidSQL` | the same two actions and keys | **authority** |
| `api_registrations.go:1885`, `registration_subjects.go:572-600` | `registration.not_restored` by event target, meta `personId`, `registeredBy`, `occurrenceId`, `registrationId`, `userId`, `attendanceMode` | de-duplication / recovery list |
| `age_rollover.go:66,198` | `household.child_turned_13` by `target_id` = person | de-duplication of a once-only notice |
| `api_push.go:222,378`, `api_message_preview.go:210`, `api_household_conversion.go:223,230,240` | `actor_id`, `action`, `at`; one query joins `audit_logs.target_id` to `invitations.email` (24-hour per-address invitation limit); one counts `user.disabled` by target | anti-abuse counters |
| `api_contact_history.go:98,205-236,297-304,475` | list and CSV of contact/add/access/membership/registration actions, using ids and the enumerated `reason` | admin history, read-only |

Consequences: scrubbing `ip`, `email`/`oldEmail`/`newEmail`, `note`, `label` (6.2) cannot change any reader above.
Anonymizing an invitation row's `email` (T17) *does* change the 24-hour per-address count for that address, which
matches the brief's rule that erasure must not throttle a returning address (R2-R3-R4-people.md:1535-1539).

### 6.7 Authorization snapshots: what is preserved and why

`occurrence_access.added`, `occurrence_access.ended` and `occurrence.blocked` are the three action-scoped registration
snapshots (R9 S4), all written through `writeAccessSnapshot` with `accessSnapshotMeta` (`registration_access.go:136-168`).
Keys: `subjectUserId`, `occurrenceId`, `eventId`, `organizerType`, `organizerId`, `registrationId`, `origin`, `grantedAtMicro`,
`issuerId`, `requestId`, `departureMembershipId`, `departureActorId`, and for `ended` also `endedAtMicro`, `endReason`,
`endedBy`, `outcome`. Ids and numbers only.

- **`added` and `ended` are consumed by authority checks.** The trigger refuses to retire or replace a grant
  unless both snapshots exist with matching keys (baseline `198-233`), fails every grant closed if any such snapshot
  anywhere is corrupt (`251-256`), and uses them to detect repeat or stale manager adds (`284-305`); the Go twin is
  `registration_access.go:324-370`. Their `meta_json` must stay byte-identical (plan rule; the DEC-141 safeguarding
  gate also relies on it).
- **`blocked` is not read by the trigger or by `loadAccessHistory`** (their scope is the first two actions only,
  `registration_access.go:324`). It is read only by the admin contact history (`api_contact_history.go:98,301-302`),
  as a history entry. It is an R9 snapshot all the same, so its `meta_json` is preserved too (plan: "preserve every
  audit row and complete `occurrence.blocked` metadata"), but nothing enforces authority from it.
- **`ip` on these rows is not authority input** (6.5); only `meta_json` is. The proposal in 6.5 therefore touches the
  column only, never `meta_json`, `action`, `target_type`, `target_id` or `actor_id`. R4-4 must prove it with a test that
  runs the trigger path after the scrub; this draft does not claim that proof exists.

### 6.8 Selector rule and what is left

R4-4 selects by action + target + key from this table, in the erasure transaction, using the captured sets of 3.1
(`INV`, `REQ`, `PERS`), captured ids and the captured address; it never matches a name, never matches an IP *value*
(the `ip` column is blanked by the class table in 6.5, never searched), and never uses the actor column alone. A scrub
must not remove a key another selector reads (`invitation.accepted` `meta.userId`, 3.1). Rows from earlier
releases whose action or key is not in section 6.2 are untouched legacy residue (O-3). The R4-6b sweep does not enumerate or count unknown historical keys per subject (12.2). Whether a
scrub is allowed to touch audit rows at all is itself part of the signature: brief 11 Q1 recommends yes for the
erased person's own addresses and typed notes, and an "untouchable log" answer turns R4-4 into a retention row.

---

### 6.9 R4-4a implementation record (staged, Nov 6, 2026)

R4-4a-1/2 supplied `account_erasure_audit.go` and its tests. The independent
`r44a-review.json` findings F1 to F3 were resolved and `r44a-recheck.json` passed
for those staged selectors. R4-4a-V now wires `eraseAuditDetailsIn` into
`apiAdminDeleteUser`, inside the same `contactMutationCtx` attempt, after
`eraseDependentRecordsIn` and before `eraseOwnedThrottleKeysIn`. The throttle
step stays last (D2). Each attempt replaces both count structs; a rollback's
counts are never returned as a successful deletion. No schema, UI or policy
changes are part of this wiring.

The candidate was tested on base `21b5c67` with the uncommitted wiring and
rollback fixture: `r44av-check`, `r44av-expanded` and `r44av-full` passed on
disposable PostgreSQL 16; `r44av-lint`, `r44av-deadcode`, `r44av-tsc2` and
`r44av-web2` passed. An independent Sonnet review found no wiring defect and
requested the expanded checks and rollback fixture; `r44av-recheck` passed; local landing remains pending. No push or PR is claimed.
No real-device or live-service hand check was found for this database/API slice.
M7.4 remains staged and incomplete.

**Attribution used by the implementation.** Conditions are shape-specific and
mutually exclusive in `auditIPAttribution`. Unknown or unmatched shapes retain
their IP; there is no general actor fallback across actions. Numeric identity
keys are compared as JSON numbers for updates, never by free-text search.

| Action and shape | Class and erasure selector |
|---|---|
| Ordinary signed-in writers, including safeguarding actions and `account.approved` | ACTOR, `actor_id = U`. This includes R4-3b's `guardian_link.revoked` writer: the deleting admin supplies its actor and IP. |
| `invitation.accepted`, numeric `userId`, no `personId` | META_USER, `userId = U`; the inviter's erasure alone never blanks it. |
| `invitation.accepted`, numeric `personId`, no `userId` | LINKED, target in captured INV(U); the acceptance metadata stays intact. |
| `membership.added_over_limit` with numeric `invitationId` and `userId` | META_USER, `userId = U`. |
| `membership.added_over_limit` without `invitationId`, numeric `userId`, actor present | ACTOR. This corrects the earlier table's invitation-only reading: a manager's direct add carries the manager's IP, not the added person's. |
| `guardian_link.requested`, `source = claim` | ACTOR, the claiming guardian. |
| `guardian_link.requested`, `source = signup`, exactly one linked non-conversion request, `source = member_invite`, requester present | REQUESTER, that request's `requested_by = U`. Erasing only the teen keeps a living inviter's IP. |
| The same signup shape, request source `self_signup` or `guest_upgrade` | LINKED, request's `approved_user_id = U`. Today's guest upgrade is adult-only; the latter branch covers future writers without changing policy. |
| `guardian_link.requested`, `source = household_conversion`, exactly one approved conversion request linked to numeric `teenUserId`, decider present | C_APPROVAL, request's `decided_by = U`; never the inviter's actor attribution. |
| `account.requested`, `source = member_invite` | ACTOR. |
| `account.requested`, source `self_signup`, `guest_upgrade` or `household_conversion`, target account request | LINKED, target in captured REQ(U). |
| `password.reset_requested`, target user | TARGET_USER, target U; never selected by actor alone. |
| `registration.cancelled` with actor | ACTOR. |
| Actor-null `registration.cancelled` with the exact user or household-person subject shape | LINKED, numeric user U or person in PERS(U). Mixed or inconsistent subjects retain their IP. |
| `registration.changed` targeting an event without a conversion discriminator | ACTOR. |
| `household.person_converted`; `registration.changed` targeting registration or carrying `invitationId` or `formerRegisteredBy` | CONVERSION. Discovery and V0-V4 diagnostics only; no IP update while G-8 is disabled. |
| Empty-IP system writers | NONE; nothing to erase. |
| Unmatched, malformed or unknown shapes with a nonempty IP | RETAIN_UNATTRIBUTED, counted only when subject-linked as described below. |

**Report semantics.** `activityEntriesKept` is the sum of the subject-linked
RETAIN_ESSENTIAL and RETAIN_UNATTRIBUTED counts plus `Conversion.Retained`.
The former require a nonempty IP and a subject mention in actor, user target,
`userId`, `teenUserId` or a captured PERS `personId`, or the malformed-conversion
nomination link through the erased decider. Numeric and string mentions count
for reporting; mention is never proof of IP ownership and never authorizes an
update. Unrelated people's rows are excluded. The malformed-nomination count
is a subset of RETAIN_UNATTRIBUTED and is not added again.

Conversion discovery is restricted to the erased subject's approved conversion
requests through `decided_by`. `Conversion.Retained` counts distinct discovered
rows, including those whose IP was already empty. Groups and reason counts may
overlap, so neither is added to the total. These CONVERSION rows cannot overlap
RETAIN_* rows. `activityEntriesKept` counts entries, not personal addresses or
all rows mentioning the subject. It can remain nonzero on a repeated scrub and
it can be zero while unreachable residue remains. No total claims complete
erasure. The response contains none of the internal diagnostic keys or values.

**What remains, by action.** R4-GR-IP (`gr-ip.json`, GPT-6.1 Sol, Nov 5 America/Boise,
Nov 6 UTC) judged the IP not essential on each requested safeguarding action:
`guardian_link.requested` signup and claim, `guardian_link.confirmed`,
`guardian_link.rejected`, `guardian_link.revoked`, `teen_consent.granted`,
`teen_consent.revoked`, `occurrence_access.added`, `occurrence_access.ended` and
`occurrence.blocked`. It also judged the conversion actions' IP not essential
for DG-9 item 2. It confirmed Class B blanking and the requester split above.
This creates **no RETAIN_ESSENTIAL action and no Q4 exception**. The class stays
in the count contract; any future essential-IP exception requires its own
named, dated owner/PLAN disposition. The review makes no owner decision and
approves no R4-4b design.

The following RETAIN_UNATTRIBUTED cases remain **Q3 residue**, not approved
retention. The list includes anomalous historical shapes, even when today's
writer cannot produce them:

- `invitation.accepted`: missing, mixed, null or nonnumeric identity keys, or
  an invalid target shape.
- `membership.added_over_limit`: missing or malformed numeric identity keys,
  or a manager shape without an actor.
- `guardian_link.requested`: unknown or missing source; claim without actor;
  signup with zero or several matching requests, invalid teen identity,
  unknown request source or missing member inviter; conversion with an invalid
  target or identity, missing or ambiguous approved request, or missing decider.
- `account.requested`: unknown source or a missing required actor/target.
- `registration.changed`: non-conversion shape without a valid event target
  or actor. Conversion shapes themselves stay in CONVERSION.
- `registration.cancelled`: inconsistent or malformed actor-null subject
  identities or target.
- `password.reset_requested`: a non-user target.
- Ordinary ACTOR actions with an unexpected missing actor and nonempty IP,
  and system-only actions with an unexpected nonempty IP, listed below.
- Any unregistered historical action with a nonempty IP. Its actual action
  code is reported internally, without treating it as a known writer. New
  production writers must pass the source census.

RETAIN_UNATTRIBUTED can therefore occur on every registered action below.
`household.person_converted` is always CONVERSION and is listed separately
with its conversion-shaped `registration.changed` siblings under IR-1 to IR-4.

- `account.approved`, `account.expired`, `account.rejected`, `account.requested`, `account.setup_completed`.
- `add.completed`, `add.decision`, `add.ended`, `add.expired`, `add.refused`, `add.requested`, `add.withdrawn`.
- `calendar_token.regenerated`, `calendar_token.revoked`, `calendar_token.scope_changed`.
- `contact.decision`, `contact.ended`, `contact.expired`, `contact.granted`, `contact.privacy_changed`, `contact.refused`, `contact.requested`, `contact.reveal`, `contact.revoked`, `contact.withdrawn`.
- `contact_history.exported`.
- `email.change_cancelled`, `email.change_requested`, `email.changed`, `email.suppression_cleared`.
- `event.created`, `event.dates_restored`, `event.deleted`, `event.host_added`, `event.host_removed`, `event.visibility_changed`, `event.visibility_override`.
- `group.approved`, `group.deleted`, `group.rejected`.
- `guardian_link.confirmed`, `guardian_link.rejected`, `guardian_link.requested`, `guardian_link.revoked`.
- `household.account_turned_adult`, `household.child_turned_13`, `household.created`, `household.person_added`, `household.person_removed`, `household.person_updated`, `household.updated`.
- `invitation.accepted`, `invitation.created`, `invitation.rate_limited`, `invitation.refused`, `invitation.resent`, `invitation.revoked`.
- `job.dismissed`, `job.retried`.
- `membership.added`, `membership.added_over_limit`, `membership.not_restored`, `membership.removed`, `membership.restored`, `membership.role_changed`.
- `message.cancelled`, `message.edited`, `message.refused`, `message.retried`, `message.scheduled`, `message.sent`, `message.test_sent`.
- `occurrence.blocked`, `occurrence.refused`.
- `occurrence_access.added`, `occurrence_access.ended`.
- `password.changed`, `password.reset_completed`, `password.reset_requested`.
- `push.device_on`, `push.keys_reset`, `push.keys_rotated`, `push.test_sent`.
- `registration.added`, `registration.added_over_capacity`, `registration.cancelled`, `registration.changed`, `registration.not_restored`, `registration.promoted`, `registration.removed`, `registration.restored`.
- `room.created`, `room.location_matched`, `room.updated`.
- `room_booking.booked`, `room_booking.overlap`, `room_booking.released`, `room_booking.series`.
- `room_feed.created`, `room_feed.revoked`.
- `room_request.confirmed`, `room_request.rejected`.
- `setting.church_address_changed`, `setting.guardian_copies_changed`, `setting.push_enabled_changed`, `setting.reply_to_changed`, `setting.room_approval_changed`, `setting.rooms_enabled_changed`, `setting.self_signup_changed`.
- `teen_consent.granted`, `teen_consent.revoked`.
- `user.created`, `user.deleted`, `user.disabled`, `user.email_changed`, `user.enabled`, `user.role_changed`, `user.turned_18`.

**Conversion residue and gates.** IR-1 is unattributed completion IPs on
`household.person_converted` and its `registration.changed` rows. IR-2 is the
same actions' transfer history when G-8 is unestablished. IR-3 is a malformed,
missing, duplicated or count-mismatched discovered group; it also includes
malformed `guardian_link.requested` conversion nominations retained separately.
IR-4 is rows no retained key reaches, which cannot be counted. Conversions
where an erased caller is outside the discovered request set remain residue.
Discovery intentionally uses text equality, so numeric identities such as
`5.0` can be missed when no other arm matches. The source comment and
`TestAuditErasureConversionTextDiscoveryLimit` pin that limit. It must be
resolved before G-8 enables transfer scrubbing.

O-5's IP-only judgment is confirmed; the authorization snapshot metadata is
unchanged, and `TestAuditErasurePreservesAccessProvenanceTrigger` covers the
post-scrub trigger path. O-6's attributable-IP rule is confirmed with no
essential-IP retention exception. O-19's Class B rule is confirmed, while its
conversion residue remains gated. Tests still await Terra on this wiring.
O-4's `checkNote`, `evidenceText` and their audit mirrors, ids, decisions,
timestamps, invitation acceptance metadata and the age-rollover marker stay
unchanged. Only the exact subject-owned keys in 6.2 are removed.

G-8 remains **unestablished**, the transfer-IP rule remains disabled and G-9
remains **OPEN**. IR-1 to IR-4, every Q3 action above and any future
RETAIN_ESSENTIAL exception still require the accepted plan's disposition.
Owner decision 5 authorizes only its bounded concept; this implementation
neither widens it nor accepts the residue. R4-4b remains gated on its adopted
design and DG-9 requirements. M7.4 is not complete.

## 7. Consent, opt-out and suppression

This section describes behavior and records. **It makes no legal conclusion**; wording such as "consent" or
"erasure" below names product records and PLAN promises, not a statement about any statute or regulator.

### 7.1 Three domains that must not be mixed

1. **Ordinary communication consent** (`consents`, channels `email` and `push`): per-person, per-category choices
   that gate *optional* mail and push. Account-keyed (`user_id`); no address column. Email also honors the
   destination address's retained choices in `email_address_optouts` through `readEmailOff` (7.6).
2. **Safeguarding evidence** (`teen_consents`, `guardian_links`, and the audit mirrors): immutable or
   decision-history records about minors. Retained (O-4).
3. **System/account consent states** (`users.status` pending_consent, `administratively_disabled`): lifecycle, not
   a communication choice.
   Provider **suppressions** (`suppressions`) are a fourth, separate thing: the provider or an admin saying an
   address cannot or must not receive mail. A webhook is never a person's affirmative consent.

### 7.2 Ordinary `consents` (owner decision 1)

Blank `ip`, `user_agent` and `evidence_text`, keep the decision columns, after a new migration allows exactly that
update on a row whose owner is soft-deleted (R4-2d). Delete is still refused and the whole remaining row is
compared so a future column stays protected. TeenConsent protection is untouched. Traced: `evidence_text` is written
only by the push "turn on" (`api_push.go:273-275`, which embeds the device label) and "turn off everywhere"
(`api_push.go:332-334`) paths; category toggles (`api_push.go:481-482`) and every email choice
(`email_unsubscribe.go:125-126`) write none. `ip` and `user_agent` are written by all of them. Only the sources
`web_form` and `unsubscribe_link` are produced by code today (the CHECK also allows `sms_keyword`, `sms_reply`,
`admin_import` and `admin`, baseline 556, none written). **Before R4-2d1a**, the post-tombstone write was reachable (F-9, 9.3); the diagnosis and writer table below are historical. This draft's earlier claim that no ordinary path can append after
the tombstone was wrong. `setConsent` (`email_unsubscribe.go:133-151`) locks the owner's `users` row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`
by id, with a raw statement that does not read `deleted_at`, and then appends; the FK is satisfied by a soft-deleted
row. The lock mode conflicts with the erasure's `FOR UPDATE`, so a write and an erasure *serialize*, but a request that
**loaded the user before the erasure committed** simply waits on that lock and proceeds afterwards. The interleaving, for
the one-click link (`oneClickUnsubscribe`, `email_unsubscribe.go:103-114`): (1) `unsubscribeUser` loads the user with a
plain scoped `First` and finds a live row (`:87-96`); (2) the erasure transaction soft-deletes it and commits; (3) the
handler's transaction calls `setEmailConsent` → `setConsent`, takes the lock on the retained row, sees the latest
state is the default (opted in), and **appends an `opted_out` row carrying this request's `ip` and `user_agent`**.
Neither the token (it binds user id and scope, not the session version) nor the session bump (it affects only later
lookups) stops a request already past step (1). The same preloaded-user shape exists in every other writer of
`setConsent`, all of which reach it through one function:

| Writer before R4-2d1a | Where the user was loaded | Revalidated under the user lock then? |
|---|---|---|
| one-click unsubscribe | `unsubscribeUser`, `email_unsubscribe.go:104` | no |
| signed email-settings link (change) | `unsubscribeUser`, `api_email_settings.go:152` → `applyEmailSettings` `:106-111` | no |
| signed-in email settings and push category toggles | session middleware (`authenticatedSessionUser`, `api.go:353-376`) → `apiUpdateNotificationSettings` `api_notifications.go:987`, `applyPushSettings` `api_push.go:461-482` | no |
| push "turn off everywhere" | session `me`, `apiPushTurnOff` `api_push.go:329-335` | no |
| push "turn on" | session `me`; **does** re-read `id, status, session_version` under `FOR UPDATE` and refuses on mismatch (`api_push.go:213-222`), which erasure defeats through its session bump; it never tests `deleted_at` | yes, by `session_version` only |

*Closure, assigned to R4-2d* (needed for the ordinary-consent scrub to mean anything, because a late row would arrive
after the scrub and carry fresh evidence). **One guard in the single writer.** After taking the user lock,
`setConsent` re-reads the row's `deleted_at` (and, for signed-in callers, `status` and `session_version` against the
caller's loaded user, as `apiPushSubscribe` already does) and, if the user is soft-deleted, writes nothing and returns
a sentinel. Every caller maps the sentinel to the answer it already gives for an unknown subject: the unsubscribe
endpoint and the signed-link pages answer as for an invalid link (`404`, "This unsubscribe link isn't valid."),
signed-in routes answer `401`, with no new status and no rate-limit text (DEC-52). Putting the check in `setConsent`
covers the five writers above and any later caller (a search of non-test Go finds exactly one insert of a `models.Consent`, `email_unsubscribe.go:151`, inside `setConsent`). The lock mode stays; the erasure transaction still takes the user row
`FOR UPDATE`. Do **not** rely on a post-delete seed alone.

*Required test (not satisfied by seeding a row after the delete).* A deterministic barrier built from a **real
preloaded request**, with no production test hook: in the test, open a transaction that takes the user row `FOR UPDATE`
and soft-deletes it **without committing**; start the real HTTP request (one-click `POST`, then each sibling in turn).
Its plain scoped load sees the pre-erasure row (MVCC), it enters its own transaction and blocks on the user lock; commit
the test transaction; the request resumes. Assert it answers as an invalid link (or `401`), appends **no** `consents` row,
and leaves the retained scrubbed rows byte-identical. The same test must fail on the unguarded `setConsent`.

*Status.* **Closed by R4-2d1a (PR #274, cc1f991)**: the guard is in `setConsent`, and the barrier tests are
`TestConsentWritersRefuseAfterTheOwnerIsSoftDeleted`, `TestConsentWritersAppendOneRowWhenTheOwnerIsLive`,
`TestConsentWritersRefuseAStaleSession` (`email_unsubscribe_test.go`) and `TestPushSubscribeRefusesAGoneCaller`
(`api_push_test.go`). The scrub itself is **implemented by R4-2d1b** (merged as PR #278, `4bb3e1e`;
see T05 and 9.5). It does not touch the signed opt-out question (O-1, R4-2d2).

### 7.3 Opt-out producers and readers (implemented locally)

| Where | What |
|---|---|
| Account choices, email | One-click unsubscribe, signed email-settings links and signed-in settings use `setEmailConsent` → `setConsent`. The user row is locked `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`; deletion and session checks precede writes. One-click POST only turns off. An authorized turn-on also clears the held address's matching record and appends affirmation evidence when a record was deleted, even if the account already said opted in. |
| Retained address choices | Only `exportEmailOptOutsIn` in `email_optouts.go`, called by `eraseConsentEvidenceIn` under the erasure's user lock, inserts records. Only the current holder's authorized turn-on deletes them. No tombstone address is reconstructed. |
| Email readers | `readEmailOff` combines account and destination-address choices. Settings, notification delivery, audience preview, recipient snapshots, guardian copies, reminders, per-row batch delivery, retries and the composer test use it. Send boundaries and limits are in 7.6. |
| Push writers and readers | `api_push.go` writes device, everywhere-off and category choices through `setConsent`; `push_deliver.go`, `messages_push.go` and `api_push.go` read push choices. R4-2d2 adds no address-level push behavior. |
| Required mail | Required notification kinds pass `needChoice=false` to `readEmailRecipient` and skip the choice read. Direct account jobs and the two synchronous account-mail exceptions also bypass it. Suppression rules still apply. |
| Optional mail | Non-required notification kinds, announcements and reminders use the categories `admin`, `groups`, `events` and `messages` from `notify.OptionalEmailCategories`. The composer test also honors the effective choice and refuses before its hourly count or audit write. |
| Not implemented | SMS channel consent; `group:<id>` scopes; `admin_import`. |

### 7.4 Suppression producers and readers (verified)

Producers: the authenticated Postmark webhook (`api_email_webhooks.go:41-71` → job `webhook.process` →
`applyEmailEvent`, insert at `:158-160`, lowercased address `postmark_webhook.go:59`). **No production writer of
reason `manual`** exists besides the constant (`models.go:1164`); only clearing is implemented
(`api_admin_suppressions.go:41-77`, keyed by the *current* user's email). Readers: `Suppressing.SendEmail`
(`internal/mail/suppress.go:52-63`), `batchSuppressions` (`messages_send.go:591-602`, from the recipient snapshot
address), `readEmailRecipients` (`api_notifications.go:824`), admin list `openSuppressions` (`api_admin_suppressions.go:22`).
`mail.Blocked` (`suppress.go:84-91`): hard bounce or manual refuses everything; a complaint refuses optional and
broadcast mail only. Because the admin clear is keyed to a user's current address, a retained suppression for an
erased person's old address has **no admin screen** until some account holds that address again.

Normalization today: `strings.ToLower(strings.TrimSpace())` in Go (`api_auth.go:95`, `suppress.go:55`, webhook parser
`:59`) versus SQL `lower(btrim())` (`api_notifications.go:824`, baseline CHECK 1836). `users.email` is citext
(case-insensitive) while `suppressions.address` compares as text. Go `TrimSpace` and SQL `btrim` do not trim the
same characters; Unicode case folding in SQL depends on the database collation. The accepted design uses one Go
function and SHA-256 of its UTF-8 bytes (R4-S).

**As built by R4-S (Route B expand; staged, not hash-only).** One lookup, `mail.OpenSuppressionReasons`
(`internal/mail/suppress.go`), normalizes each input in Go (`mail.NormalizeAddress`), hashes it (`mail.AddressHash`) and
matches open rows by the stored hash, or by the stored `address` on a row whose hash is still NULL. Reasons come back in
ascending suppression id. The readers and writers converted to it:

- `Suppressing.SendEmail` (every mail send), `batchSuppressions` (send-time recipient snapshot) and `recipientFacts`
  (announcement audience) call the lookup directly.
- `readEmailRecipients` loads accounts and the footer, reads effective choices through `readEmailOff` only when
  `needChoice` is true, and reads suppressions through the lookup. R4-2d2 separates the choice statement from the
  account read so it can combine account and address choices at one statement snapshot.
- The admin list (`openSuppressions`, keyed by each row's hash, or by the hash of its stored address while the hash is
  NULL) and the admin clear (`apiAdminClearSuppression`, which matches the hash, or the address of a NULL-hash row).
- The webhook writer (`applyEmailEvent`) stores the address with its hash. A redelivery against a legacy NULL-hash open
  row now updates that row's hash only; before R4-S it did nothing. Every other column of the row stays.
- A guard, `TestSuppressionSQLStaysInItsHelpers`, keeps suppression SQL and `models.Suppression` in four places (the
  lookup and its SQL constant, the admin helpers, the webhook writer and the model). It is parse-only: it does not see
  table names built from non-literal pieces, reflection, SQL sent around GORM, dot-imports of `fold/models` or
  cross-package helpers.

*Matching basis (disclosed, pinned by `TestSuppressionMatchingBasis`).* Every reader now matches by Go byte equality of
the normalized input with the stored address (the hash arm, or the address arm for a NULL hash). That is not a change of
meaning on a database with this basis: a UTF8 database whose default collation is libc, which is deterministic, so
PostgreSQL `=` on `suppressions.address` was already byte equality. The Postgres 16 images Fold ships and tests on create
such a database, and the test fails unless the database is UTF8 with a libc default, both columns use the default
collation and SQL and Go hash a non-ASCII literal identically. Outside that basis (a hand-made non-UTF8 or ICU database,
or a nondeterministic column collation added later) only ASCII addresses are claimed equal, and a collation-equal but
byte-different hashed row could stop matching. A webhook insert whose Go hash disagreed with SQL would fail the CHECK and
be retried by the job runner. `docs/FIRST-CHURCH-CHECKS.md` asks the church to confirm `SHOW server_encoding`.

*Behavior changes, all disclosed:* `readEmailRecipients` normalizes the account address in Go instead of SQL
`lower(btrim(users.email))`, identical for ASCII addresses; the order of reasons the sender's `Blocked` check sees is now
ascending id, where it was unspecified; the separate suppression lookup above; the webhook redelivery update above. A row that only a
non-Fold writer could store (an address Go's normalization would change, for example around a Unicode space that SQL
`btrim` keeps) is matched by no Fold input before or after R4-S; the migration hashes such a row's own bytes so it
cannot fail.

### 7.5 The original conflict and its local disposition

PLAN 10.1 requires hashed addresses for both opt-out and suppression records. The brief's temporary
plaintext-suppression stage does not amend that requirement. Suppression contraction remains R4-SC.
The brief also says erasure must not reserve, suppress or throttle a returning address.

Account-keyed `consents` alone left an address-reuse gap: a new holder had no account opt-out and defaulted
to opted in. Owner decision 8 resolves how the address's optional categories persist without blocking
account creation or required mail. R4-2d2 implements that choice locally as described below. It does not
close C2 before publication and merge, or resolve the separate suppression-storage requirement.

### 7.6 Address-level opt-outs as implemented locally

Owner decision 8 was recorded at 2026-11-06T00:34:36Z, evening of Nov 5, 2026, America/Boise.
The question was: "For optional-email opt-outs after account deletion, should a reused email address keep
its opted-out categories off until its current holder explicitly turns them on? The opt-out would stay
with the address when someone changes addresses, and settings would show only the current address's
effective choices, with no prior-account history." Grier answered **"Yes, use those rules (Recommended)"**.
The host records are `state.json` → `optOutDecision`, `r42d2-design-rev2.json` and the approving
`r42d2-review-rev2.json`. The review approves the design, not an implementation or milestone completion.
These supersede the earlier candidate's unsettled choices. Prior implementation evidence is in 15.8.

The implementation follows D1 to D13 of that design:

- T38 retains only `address_hash`, `scope` and `opted_out_at`. The four scopes are `category:admin`,
  `category:groups`, `category:events` and `category:messages`. Hashes use `mail.AddressHash`, SHA-256 of
  `mail.NormalizeAddress` in Go. SQL receives the hash, never the address. Matching uses normalized Go
  byte equality; no wider collation equivalence is claimed beyond the basis in 7.4.
- `exportEmailOptOutsIn` selects the latest email consent per scope by `at DESC, id DESC`, keeps only
  in-force `opted_out` rows in those four scopes, and copies the choice's `at`, not erasure time.
  `ON CONFLICT (address_hash, scope) DO NOTHING` preserves an existing record's time. The export commits
  with erasure, only when `erasureSubject.Address` holds a nonempty normalized address. Legacy tombstone
  sweeps export nothing and reconstruct no address. Account creation, imports, invitations, defaults,
  address changes, webhooks and suppression clearing never create these records.
- `readEmailOff(db, []emailDest{UserID, Address})` is the single effective-choice reader.
  `emailOff.Off` is true when either the account's latest choice is off or the destination hash has a
  record for that scope. There is no timestamp contest: an existing account opt-in cannot override a
  retained record. Each chunk of at most 500 destinations reads both arms in one SQL statement using
  `idx_consents_latest` and T38's primary key. Settings and selection use `users.email`; batch delivery
  uses the queued `message_recipients.address`, including guardian copies and retries.
- Only the current holder's affirmative turn-on clears the record for the locked current address and
  selected scope. `setConsent` locks the user `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` and checks deletion and the session
  before `clearOptOutOnTurnOn` runs. The delete and any appended `opted_in` evidence commit together.
  Clearing a record forces evidence even when the account already says opted in. Turning all optional
  email on clears all four scopes; turning off never changes address records. The erasure export,
  user-address uniqueness and the holder's user lock serialize competing holders. Readers take no locks.
- Version 2 unsubscribe links carry separate 16-byte identity and address MACs. The address MAC uses
  the separate `fold:unsubscribe-address:v1:` key derivation and binds the body, a zero separator and the
  destination hash. Identity alone authorizes viewing and turning off. Turn-on requires a current
  signed-in session or a link bound to the locked account's current address. Version 1 and moved-address
  links cannot turn on. Their uniform 403 `email_link_turn_on` is decided before any record lookup,
  whether a record exists or not. One-click POST remains turn-off-only. Erased-account links answer 404
  on GET, PUT and one-click POST; they never resolve through an address hash.
- Moving from A to B leaves A's records on A and applies B's at the next read. Account consents follow
  the account. Verification is not a turn-on. Both address-change paths advance `session_version`;
  a signed-in turn-on authenticated before a change that commits first gets 401 and writes nothing.
  If turn-on takes the lock first, it clears only the old address's selected record before the move.
- Settings expose only current effective choices. Their email-settings JSON is identical for account
  and address sources. There is no hash, history, prior identity, arbitrary-address lookup or admin
  record screen. The token page adds `canTurnOn`, determined by address binding independently of any
  retained record. When false, the page explains turn-off-only access. A refused turn-on restores the
  switch and uses the existing `FormError`, with focus retained.
- Required notifications skip the choice read; direct account mail and the two synchronous account
  exceptions also bypass it. Provider suppressions remain separate. Push, SMS and group scopes are
  unchanged. A guest whose optional event mail is off has no turn-on channel until account upgrade.

**Delivery boundary and permitted in-flight window, D11 and D12.** At READ COMMITTED, an account opt-out
or address record committed before the effective-choice statement starts is honored. A change committed
after that statement starts may not stop that one send while it is rendered or dispatched. Every later
row, retry, notification delivery and test request rechecks.

`deliverMessageBatchOnce` reads the choice inside each row's claim transaction, after `FOR UPDATE SKIP
LOCKED` and the reader-status check, before rendering, suppression checks and provider dispatch.
Announcements, reminders, guardian copies and both Try again paths share that loop. Optional notification
delivery checks inside its claim transaction before rendering and dispatch, against the current account
address. Batch reader status and provider suppressions still use their existing per-batch reads; R4-2d2
does not change those boundaries.

The composer test checks the locked author's deletion, status, session versions and email, then reads the
effective choice while holding that row. An earlier security change returns 401; an off choice returns
400 `message_test_opted_out` before the hourly count and audit write. The transaction commits and releases
the lock before the synchronous provider call, which has a 30-second context. An opt-out, erasure or
address change that commits after that transaction commits, before or during the provider call, may not
stop that one test send to the previously checked address. No user lock spans that call. The next request
rechecks. Holding the lock across provider latency would delay erasure and settings writes.

The hash is pseudonymous and guessable. An operator with a candidate address and database access can
link it to a tombstone through retained `consents.at` and the record's transaction `xmin` (T38).
No Fold response exposes those records. Old version 1 links and links to another address lose turn-on
authority; an address-bound link works again if the same account returns to its bound address, since it
binds the address rather than an epoch.

The approved design refines plan revision 8: records are deleted rather than closed, so the primary key
replaces a unique-open-row shape; there is no `created_at` or redundant email-channel column; shared logic
lives in `email_optouts.go`; and the core work is split into readers, turn-on and export before UI.
The composer test now checks both effective choices and stale sessions. These are approved design choices,
not new product decisions made by this documentation task. O-1 is implemented locally only. Final
integration review, exact-head checks, local landing and PUB-1's merge remain pending; C2 is not met.

### 7.7 Suppression staging (accepted plan, restated)

- **Route A** (hash-only baseline edit): only if a separately reviewed, freshly refreshed check shows the DEC-138
  window still open (section 10). **Route B** (additive migration, dual-write, backfill, plaintext kept
  temporarily, contraction R4-SC after a tagged expand release): the default, including when the window is closed
  or cannot be established. Neither route resets an owner database or creates a tag or release.
- Route B's retained plaintext (`suppressions.address`, `webhook.process` payload `address`/`detail`, and the
  `last_error` copies) is a disclosed temporary gap. M7.4 is not complete until hash-only suppression storage (Route
  A or R4-SC) and the signed opt-out behavior exist, or an explicit, authorized PLAN change says otherwise.
- **R4-S status.** The Route B expand is implemented in `00002_suppression_address_hash.sql` (T35, 7.4). The remaining
  gap is the plaintext column, the `webhook.process` payload `address` and `detail`, and the `last_error` copies, until
  R4-SC. R4-SC is not built.
- **R4-SC hand-off notes (not built).** Fill every NULL `address_hash` in SQL before `SET NOT NULL`; switch the webhook
  `ON CONFLICT` to `idx_suppressions_open_hash`; convert or scrub `webhook.process` payloads and `last_error` under the
  job-state locks; a rollback to the R4-S release after the contraction needs the dump that `upgrade.sh` takes (the
  plaintext is gone from the contracted database); the contraction's lock span needs the same measurement as 00002.
- **R4-S upgrade and rollback facts.** 00002 runs in one goose transaction behind `SET LOCAL lock_timeout = '5s'`. That
  bounds the wait for the `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` lock on `suppressions`, not the hold, which grows with the table and has no
  size bound claimed (cleared rows are kept indefinitely). A migration that cannot get the lock in 5 seconds fails,
  applies nothing and the app does not boot. The previous release's goose runner treats a version-2 database as current:
  `TestSuppressionExpandKeepsThePreviousBinaryWorking` records that `Up` returned nil and applied nothing against it, so
  the previous image boots on an upgraded database without `fold migrate down-one`; rows it writes keep a NULL hash
  and the new release matches them by address. The documented `upgrade.sh --rollback` restores the dump the upgrade took
  instead (`docs/DEPLOY-DIGITALOCEAN.md` section 6).

## 8. Stores that are not tables

| Store | Fact (verified) | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| Avatar blobs | Keys `avatars/<decimal user id>/<random>.<ext>` (`api_profile.go:497`, `storage/storage.go:36`); user row holds the key, not a URL | **Implemented by R4-5** (`avatar_cleanup.go`; `storage` `CleanOwner`, `ScanOwners`, `Open`): erasure clears `avatar_key` and queues `avatar.cleanup` in its own transaction, under the user row lock; the job then removes the namespace after commit |
| Orphan avatars | `saveAvatar` and `apiDeleteAvatar` discard `Delete` errors (`api_profile.go:503-507,521-524`); upload does `Put` then a plain `Update` with no lock; an update against a soft-deleted row matches zero rows and returns no error, leaving a blob nothing references | F-5, **implemented by R4-5**: upload and removal change the key and queue `avatar.cleanup` in one transaction under the user row lock, and the job clears the rest of `avatars/<id>/`; the daily `avatar.sweep` queues the same job for folders a crash left behind |
| `.tmp-*` leftovers | `LocalStore.Put` writes `.tmp-*` beside the target, removed by `defer os.Remove` except after a crash (`storage/storage.go:69-100`) | **Implemented by R4-5**: `CleanOwner` removes every entry in the owner's folder except the current key, `.tmp-*` remnants included |
| Group covers | `covers/<group id>/<random>.<ext>` (`api_profile.go:565`); the group's record, even if a person uploaded it | untouched; no new namespaces invented |
| Public URLs and caches | `/uploads/<key>`, `Cache-Control: public, max-age=86400`, `nosniff` (`api_profile.go:637`) | a browser or shared cache may keep the image for up to a day; not recallable |
| Browser state | API responses are `no-store`; no service-worker `fetch` handler; `localStorage["fold.pushOwner"]` stores a user id, cleared at logout (`web/src/lib/pushState.ts:122-137`) | session invalid on next request (`authenticatedSessionUser`, `api.go:353-376`, compares `session_version` and `logout_version`); open pages in other browsers keep what they already rendered until reload |
| Logs | access log redacts capability paths (`access_log.go`); failure logs use `jobs.CleanError`; container logs hold client IPs and paths | outside the database; retention is the operator's |
| Providers | Postmark/SMTP hold sent mail and delivery events; push services hold subscriptions' endpoints | outside Fold; Fold cannot recall mail already accepted. Operator note only (O-12) |
| Exports | No people or own-data export exists yet (M7.8 not started). An admin can export contact history as CSV (`api_contact_history.go:475`, audited as `contact_history.exported`) | files an admin downloaded are outside the database |
| Database backups | `backup.sh`: daily dumps 14 days, dumps from the 1st of a month 180 days; optional age-encrypted offsite copy; weekly provider droplet backups; snapshots until deleted (`docs/DEPLOY-DIGITALOCEAN.md:291-335`, `deploy/digitalocean/backup.sh:40-46`) | section 11 |
| Upload backups | `uploads-*.tgz` from the same job | section 11 |
| Email opt-outs, suppressions, legacy | sections 7 and T35 | staged; O-1 implemented locally only (7.6, 15.8), closure on master pending; O-2 gated |
| `goose` metadata | versions and timestamps only | excluded |
| Ownership boundaries | the church owns its droplet, DNS, mail account and backups (`AGENTS.md` deployment) | M7.4 changes the app database and the blob store only |

### 8.1 Avatar cleanup as built (slice R4-5)

R4-5 adds no migration, no model change, no UI, no error code, no notification kind and no policy decision, and it
changes none of the five owner decisions or any gate. It does not do the R4-1 identity scrub or the R4-6 legacy row scrub.

- **What it does.** An upload, a removal and an erasure each change `avatar_key` and queue one `avatar.cleanup` job
  (payload `{userId}` only) in the same transaction, under the user row lock. The job takes the same lock, reloads the
  current key and removes everything else in `avatars/<id>/`, at most 256 entries per locked run (a longer folder
  finishes over more runs). A row keeps its current key unless `deleted_at` is set; a tombstone keeps nothing. Nothing on
  a request path deletes an avatar file any more. The daily `avatar.sweep` queues the same job, keyed by church date, for
  folders a crash left behind.
- **Bounded holds.** Every transaction that takes the user row runs under its own context deadline, and `database/sql`
  rolls a transaction back when its context ends, which frees the row even if a filesystem call is blocked. The wait to
  get the row is bounded separately by `lock_timeout = 5s`. The deadlines are 20s for an upload or a removal, counted
  from when the body was read, and never later than 50s after the request reached the router (the router stamp is taken
  before the CSRF check reads an upload body, so a slow upload eats its own time and not the server's 60s write timeout),
  and 15s for a cleanup run. An upload ordinarily holds the row for the 10s `Put` bound plus three statements.
- **The erasure wait.** While erasure holds the contact gate, it waits on the user row for each avatar transaction that
  holds the row before erasure gets it, up to that transaction's deadline: 20s for an upload or removal, 15s for a
  cleanup run of at most 256 unlinks. `database/sql`'s rollback at the deadline enforces this even when a filesystem
  call blocks. Postgres does not promise the order in which it serves row-lock waiters, so a later avatar request can
  take the row first. In that case erasure clears whatever key it then finds and queues cleanup. A request still waiting
  after its 5s `lock_timeout` answers 500 with nothing stored, and one that gets the row after erasure commits answers
  401. Only that account's own signed-in requests and its cleanup jobs take the row on these paths, which is why the
  wait is short in practice; the number of holders is not fixed. With one holder, erasure answers inside the server's
  60s write timeout. Several holders, or existing contention on the contact gate, can push it past 60s. Then the admin
  sees the network message while the erasure still commits or rolls back on its own, and a repeated delete of a
  committed erasure answers 404.
- **Alternatives not taken.** (1) A `lock_timeout` on erasure's own row lock: it would make an admin's delete fail
  because of the account's own photo activity, and it would change erasure's statements and HTTP mapping, which this
  slice leaves alone. (2) Releasing the row during the unlinks: the keep decision is only safe while the row is locked,
  because an upload that committed in the gap could have its new file removed as "old".
- **Limits, stated as limits.**
  - A cached public `/uploads` copy may persist up to a day (unchanged). Blobs in `uploads-*.tgz` backups age out under
    section 11.
  - Owner directories with no `users` row, and non-canonical names under `avatars/`, are skipped. They appear only as
    counts in the sweep's log line (accounts with no row, unsafe folders with no row, skipped names), never as names,
    and otherwise only by inspecting the filesystem directly. They are not listed by name and not shown on Admin, System.
  - An unsafe or nested owner folder for an existing account (including one Fold cannot open or read) makes that
    account's cleanup job fail; once it is dead it shows on Admin, System under `Removing old profile photos`, and it is
    not queued again until an admin retries or dismisses it.
  - A failed cleanup or sweep job is retried by the runner over the five attempts of `jobs.DefaultMaxAttempts`.
    `jobs.Backoff` waits 30s, 60s, 120s and 240s between them, each jittered by up to 20%, so the job is dead about
    7.5 minutes (6 to 9) after its first failure, plus run time (a lock-timeout attempt adds up to 5s, a deadline attempt
    up to 15s). A dead sweep is queued again by the next church day's key.
  - Erasure's wait has no fixed count of holders, because Postgres does not promise arrival order for row-lock waiters.
    An owner who keeps sending avatar requests during their own erasure can lengthen it, each by at most one deadline.
  - A filesystem call that never returns can pin a request or job goroutine, though its transaction and row lock are
    released at the deadline.
  - Hard links and bind mounts inside the upload volume are not detected. `UPLOAD_DIR` itself is trusted operator
    configuration, resolved per operation, so containment starts below it. Only Fold writes the volume.

## 9. Locks, writers, races and in-flight limits

### 9.1 `users.role/status/deleted_at` writers

Production writers found (grep for `Updates`, `Update`, `Save`, `Create`, `Delete` against `models.User`):
creation at `setup.go:91`, `api_admin.go:551`, `api_account_requests.go:580`, `api_household_conversion.go:590,718`,
`api_invitations.go:403`, `api_guest.go:388`; whole-row `Save` at `api_invitations.go:397`; column updates at
`api_admin.go:751,765,794` (PATCH role/status), `:863` and `:877` (DELETE), `age_rollover.go:115,177`,
`account_security.go:130`, `api_teen_consent.go:137`, `api_teen_conversion.go:171,191`, `api_profile.go:146,336,502,521`,
`api_guest.go:371,377`, `api_email_change_cancel.go:30`, `api_auth.go:330` (`logout_version`). Only
**admin PATCH role (non-admin), admin PATCH status = disabled, and admin DELETE** can reduce the count of active
admins; teens are never admins (`api_admin.go:656-662`); the other paths create or activate accounts. R4-1 must
re-grep and classify any newly found reducing or deleting path before it merges.

**R4-1 re-grep result (independent writer census at the R4-1-3 review).** No additional path reduces the active-admin count or deletes a user outside the contact gate. The reducing edits (role to non-admin, status to non-active) remain inside `apiAdminUpdateUser`'s `contactMutation`; `tombstoneUserIn` and the soft delete exist only in `apiAdminDeleteUser`'s gated callback. The invitation whole-row `Save` is gated and only upgrades a guest, keeping its role; account-request upgrades target guests only and are gated; password replacement only activates an invited account; the consent, age-rollover and staged-conversion status writers follow their lifecycle gate and account constraints; creation, profile, avatar, email-change and logout writes add no reducing path. `api_admin.go`'s other-admin count during guardian-link confirmation reads the count and writes no user role, status or deletion. Result: unchanged from the table above, and still a source reading, not a proof against code added later.

### 9.2 Notification writers, recipient locks and the 33 `notify.Send` callers

*Method.* Every `notify.Send(` expression in non-test Go (`grep -rn "notify\.Send("`) was listed, each enclosing
function's callers were followed upward until a function that opens a transaction, and the first lock that
transaction takes was read in the source. The walk was done with a throwaway script kept outside the repository
(it follows `name(` references, so it can miss a wrapper reached only through a function value; that was not
searched for beyond `grep "notify\.Send\b"`, which finds no alias or method value of `Send` itself). The walk's heuristic mis-sorted a few entries that
take the gate through a helper; each was corrected by reading the helper (`lockTeenLifecycleUsers`,
`lockAdminHouseholdParticipants`, `lockConsentUsers`, `sweepContacts`). No project test, build or program was run; the script and greps only read files.

**The gate.** "Gated" below means the global contact advisory lock `lockContacts` (`contact_lifecycle.go:29-30`) is
taken *before* the notice is written, by one of: `contactMutation` (`contact_lifecycle.go:49-52`);
`contactOperation` (`api_contact.go:147-167`); `lockTeenLifecycleUsers` (`api_teen_conversion.go:89`, used by
`lockConsentUsers` `api_teen_consent.go:57`, `decideGuardianLink` `api_admin.go:339` and `rolloverAges`
`age_rollover.go:38`); `lockAdminHouseholdParticipants` (`api_admin_household.go:50`); a direct `lockContacts` at the top
of the transaction (`api_occurrence_mutations.go:260`, `api_registrations.go:1424,1612`,
`api_series_registrations.go:212,486`); or the try-lock on the same id in `sweepContacts`
(`contact_lifecycle.go:403-408`, which skips the sweep when busy). "Plain" means no gate. The gate does not lock any
user row; later row locks differ by entry and are named where the entry is distinctive.

**Count and reconciliation.** 33 call expressions: `contact_lifecycle.go` 1, `jobs_daily.go` 1,
`api_household_conversion.go` 1, `age_rollover.go` 2, `push_deliver.go` 1, `push_keys.go` 1, `api_notifications.go` 11,
`api_teen_consent.go` 2, `api_room_bookings.go` 1, `api_room_requests.go` 2, `api_departure.go` 2,
`api_registrations.go` 5, `api_occurrence_mutations.go` 2, `registration_access.go` 1. They sit in 31 functions
(`changeTeenConsent` and `notifyDeparture` each hold two). The first draft's count of 33 stands. Its file-level
"files with no gate reference" list was wrong as a classification (for example `age_rollover.go` reaches the gate through
`lockTeenLifecycleUsers`) and is withdrawn. R19's future gate removal is a separate redesign; this table adds no gate
site and asks for none.

#### 9.2.1 The writers underneath `Send`

| Operation | Source | What it writes and locks (traced) | Recipient-user lock |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Insert** | `notify.go:149` (`tx.Create`), `ON CONFLICT (user_id, dedupe_key) DO NOTHING` | one `notifications` row per recipient; recipients de-duplicated, zero ids and the actor removed (`:103-106`) | **FK `notifications.user_id` → `users` (baseline 3603).** PostgreSQL's foreign-key check takes `FOR KEY SHARE` on the referenced row; the FK ignores `deleted_at`, so a soft-deleted recipient still accepts the row (F-3). Not measured here; R4-N must prove it |
| **Merge** | `notify.go:214-246` | locks the open notification `FOR UPDATE`, then its email delivery row `FOR UPDATE`, then `UPDATE`s title/body/count columns of that row. `user_id` is not in the `SET` list | **none new.** An UPDATE that does not change the FK column does not re-run the FK check, so merge takes no key-share lock on the user and cannot see a soft deletion |
| **QueueDelivery** | `notify.go:170-206` | `INSERT INTO notification_deliveries (notification_id, channel, user_id, ...) ... ON CONFLICT (notification_id, channel) DO NOTHING`, then `jobs.EnqueueMany` (`jobs` has no foreign keys, baseline 1131-1149) | each **new** delivery row checks FK `notification_id` → `notifications` (3584-3587, takes key-share on that notification) and FK `user_id` → `users` (3593-3595, key-share on the user). Rows that conflict are skipped and fire no FK check. From `Send` these rows were inserted a moment earlier in the same transaction, so the same user is already key-share locked. From repair they were selected earlier |
| **Delivery-intent repair** | `reconcileNotifications` `notify_deliver.go:67-124` and `queueReconciledNotifications` `:133-187`; hourly/daily job `reminders.go:87` | pass 1 selects delivery rows with no job in autocommit and `EnqueueMany`s (no user lock). Pass 2 selects `notifications` rows in autocommit (`db.Raw`, `:105-116`), then in a **later** transaction calls `QueueDelivery` twice (deliver and bell-only) | none held between selection and insert. A notification deleted in between (erasure `api_admin.go:874`, or retention prune) makes the delivery insert fail on `notification_id` (23503), the job returns the error and retries; a soft-deleted user still passes the user FK and gets a delivery row and job. `sendHeldNotification` then reads the recipient through `readEmailRecipients` (soft-delete scoped) and records "recipient not active" (`notify_deliver.go:293-302`), so no email goes to a tombstone; the push path is not re-read line by line |
| **Push aggregate row** | `recordPushIn` `push_deliver.go:536-553` | `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (notification_id, channel) DO UPDATE` in autocommit | same two FKs as QueueDelivery for a new row |
| **Resolve** | `notify.go:251-256` | `UPDATE notifications ... WHERE kind = ? AND subject_id = ? AND waiting AND resolved_at IS NULL` | none (no user key in the statement) |
| **Read state** | `notify.go:379-424` | `UpdateColumn` scoped by `user_id` | none |
| **Prune** | `notify.go:428-434` | `DELETE` by retention predicate (daily job) | none |
| **Erasure delete (existing)** | `api_admin.go:874` | `DELETE FROM notifications WHERE user_id = ?` inside the delete transaction, after the target row `FOR UPDATE` (`:853`); cascades `notification_deliveries` | holds `FOR UPDATE` on the user, which conflicts with another transaction's key-share |
| **Email/webhook status** | `updateEmailDelivery` `notify_deliver.go:374-377`; `api_email_webhooks.go:105,136` | status updates on delivery rows | none |

Ordering facts that bear on R4-N, all traced: `merge` locks notification then delivery and its comment explains why
the handler's opposite order cannot deadlock (`notify.go:226-229`); erasure takes the user `FOR UPDATE` and then deletes
notifications; a concurrent `Send` insert therefore either waits for erasure (and then, finding the row still
present because the user is only soft-deleted, inserts a notice for the tombstone, F-3) or holds key-share first
and makes erasure wait. KEY SHARE conflicts with `FOR UPDATE` and with an update to `users.email`, but not with
`FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, which is what the teen/household/conversion locks use (`api_teen_conversion.go:61,111`,
`api_admin_household.go:55`, `api_registrations.go:1898`).

**Recipient selection is unlocked everywhere.** `adminIDs`/`activeAdminIDs` (`api_notifications.go:217-228`) read
`role = admin AND status = active` through the soft-delete-scoped model; `leaderIDs` (`:230-235`) reads `memberships`
only; `contactNotice` (`contact_lifecycle.go:239-257`) reads `users` active with a password; registrant notices pass the
registrant id directly. None locks the user rows it returns, so every caller has a window between selecting a recipient
and inserting the notice. Whether that window matters depends only on whether the erasure transaction can interleave,
which the gate prevents for gated entries and does not for plain ones.

#### 9.2.2 Every `notify.Send` site (N-01 … N-33)

"Entries" are the transaction-opening functions that can reach the site. Gated entries hold the global gate before the
send. Plain entries hold no gate; the first lock they take is named. **Unresolved** means the walk reached a function
whose callers it could not enumerate or that runs outside any transaction; the reason is given.
