### 9.4 Active, dead and in-flight work after commit

- **Active job for the erased subject.** Direct-mail jobs prepare from stored facts: password reset, email
  verification, guardian-consent and existing-account-hint preparation `First()` the user and
  treat `ErrRecordNotFound` as nothing to send (`mail_jobs.go:259-330,514-523`); of the account-request cases only
  `exists` and `approved` load the user (through `approved_user_id`); `received`, `rejected` and `conversion_approved`
  mail `account_requests.email` (`mail_jobs.go:626,658-668`). Invitation and guest
  preparation lock only their own row (`:163-232,359-440`), so a pending invitation or guest row addressed to the
  erased address, and an account request in one of those three cases, must be revoked or cleared by R4-2a before the job runs.
- **Prepared and in flight.** A provider call already prepared and sent cannot be recalled. Delivery is at least
  once; a retry may replace a link and only the newest capability works (`AGENTS.md` direct mail). A capability
  already emailed to a now-erased account must not redeem: R4-1 deletes the account's tokens, so a redemption finds none;
  a claim recheck of hash, unused state and deadline is the existing rule.
  **After R4-1**, a queued password-reset, existing-account-hint, email-verification or account-request `exists`/`approved`
  job whose preparation starts after the erasure commits sends nothing (password reset and the hint read the user through the
  soft-delete scope, email verification finds its token deleted, `exists` and `approved` load `approved_user_id`):
  `TestQueuedAccountMailForErasedAccountSendsNothing`, which proves only that timing. A preparation that **committed
  before** the erasure (or, for the ungated hint and `exists` mail, read the user before the erasure committed) may still
  send to the captured address, or for email verification to the pending new address. Erasure cannot stop that provider
  call. It deletes the token, so any capability in that mail finds nothing; the hint and `exists` mail carry no
  capability; and a retried send re-prepares and sends nothing. **After R4-2a** (merged in PR #275), a queued invitation,
  guest-verification or account-request `received`/`rejected`/`conversion_approved` job for a source row actually revoked, deleted
  or anonymized by the erasure sends nothing when preparation starts after the erasure commits: the invitation is revoked and holds a placeholder address, an unconfirmed guest signup is
  deleted and a confirmed one anonymized, and `prepareAccountRequestMail` sends nothing to a placeholder address
  (`TestQueuedDependentMailForErasedAccountSendsNothing`, which proves only that timing). A preparation that committed
  before the erasure may still have rotated a capability and sent to the captured address, and a provider call already made
  cannot be recalled. The erasure captures request and invitation IDs once and performs address-based scrubs at specific points.
  An ungated submission can create a row outside the captured sets or after an address-based scrub, including a self-signup
  request at a guest's address after REQ capture; such a row can retain personal details and queue mail that prepares after
  the erasure commits. Guardian-consent mail is R4-3's. A throttle hit can race in after the erasure. A calendar or push request whose
  session went stale after it was loaded (erased, disabled, credential-changed or logged out) saves nothing
  (`TestCalendarLinkChangesFromAStaleSessionSaveNothing`, `TestPushSubscribeFromAStaleSessionSavesNothing`).
  An announcement being sent holds `message_recipients ... FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` inside the provider call
  (`messages_send.go:506,529`); an erasure scrub of that row waits for it, and the mail may still go.
  **After R4-2a** (merged in PR #275) the one `message_recipients` UPDATE of the erasure waits for such a row
  under a statement timeout, and a row the erasure has already updated is skipped by the worker's `SKIP LOCKED`, so it is
  never sent. A snapshot built from facts read before the erasure locks and revalidates its users before it claims the
  message (L1), so it inserts no row that still holds the erased address. Three budgets are involved, and none is the
  outer bound of another:
  - **Provider network limits** of the shipped adapters, per request: Postmark's `http.Client` timeout is 15 s
    (`internal/mail/postmark.go`); SMTP dials within 10 s and caps the exchange at 30 s (`internal/mail/mail.go`).
    `batchDeadline` (10 minutes) is only a context deadline handed to `Send`: the batch's database work uses
    `context.WithoutCancel`, and the `mail.Sender` interface cannot force an implementation to honor `ctx`. So none of
    these figures bounds the erasure, and `deliverSendTimeout` (60 s) is not on this path.
  - **Database statement.** `erasureRecipientWait` (20 s) is a transaction-local `statement_timeout` on the
    `message_recipients` UPDATE only, covering the whole statement. Workers (`FOLD_WORKERS`, default 2, at most 64) each
    hold one recipient row `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` across one provider call; while the UPDATE waits on one row, workers
    can lock later matching rows, so successive waits plus execution can exceed 20 s even when every provider call ends
    inside its own limit. Any provider, Postmark included, can therefore hit the timeout. When it does, the whole
    erasure rolls back and answers a generic 500 server error, and the admin can retry (the handler logs `could not delete user`). The other erasure
    waits (the gate, the target row, the occurrences `promoteOnDisable` locks, the D2 throttle rows) are unbounded as
    before. Tests: `TestAccountErasureRecipientWaitIsBounded`, `TestErasureStatementTimeoutContract`.
  - **HTTP.** The server's 60 s `WriteTimeout` bounds only the response, not a transaction: `contactMutation` runs on
    `a.DB` without the request context, so an erasure can commit after the browser lost its answer (pre-existing).

  No 60-second or provider-derived guarantee is claimed anywhere.
- **Withdrawn account requests (Consistency exception).** The erasure writes no per-request audit row when it withdraws a
  pending account request; `user.deleted` plus the `withdrawn` resolution of its waiting task is the record. Each
  scheduled message it cancels does get exactly one `message.cancelled` row, as an author's own cancel does.
- **Dead jobs.** `dead` rows stay until an admin Retries or Dismisses (`internal/jobs/jobs.go`, `api_admin_jobs.go`).
  A retry re-prepares from current facts, so a revoked or cleared source stops quietly; a `webhook.process` retry
  after contraction must not reintroduce a raw address (R4-SC).
- **Files.** An upload that races an erasure can `Put` a blob that no row references (F-5); `avatar.cleanup`
  (R4-5) takes a user lock, reloads the current key, and removes the rest of the namespace; a stale task preserves
  a newer committed upload and a rolled-back erasure cannot delete its photo. Implemented by R4-5 (8.1); the cases are tested by `TestAvatarCleanupKeepsCurrentPhotoByDeletedAtAlone`, `TestAvatarCleanupWaitsForBlockedUploadAndKeepsTheNewPhoto`, `TestAvatarReplacementsInterleavedWithCleanupKeepOnlyTheLatest`, `TestAdminDeleteRolledBackLeavesPhotoAlone`, `TestAdminDeleteWaitsForBlockedUploadThenClearsTheNewPhoto` and `TestStoreAvatarWithSnapshotFromBeforeErasureRefuses`, with the storage cases in `storage/storage_test.go` (`TestCleanOwner*`, `TestRaceStepsAreRefused`, `TestScanOwners*`).
- **Queued push.** `push.deliver` is one job per device that re-runs its eligibility checks (`AGENTS.md` web push section; not re-read line by line here); devices are deleted
  before the session bump so no `push.devices_off` job is created.

### 9.5 Lock order and call census for the new selectors (R40 revision)

Facts the later slices must keep, all read from the source and none executed:

- **Capture before scrub, under the gate.** The erasure runs inside `contactMutation` (the global gate first, then the
  user row `FOR UPDATE`, `api_admin.go:852-853`). `REQ`, `INV` and `PERS` (3.1) are computed in that transaction after the
  user lock and before the first scrub. `INV` reads `audit_logs` (no lock; no production code updates an audit row, 6.1) and `invitations`; `REQ` rows are then locked by the scrub `UPDATE`. A request that accepts an invitation
  concurrently takes the same gate first (`api_invitations.go:319`), so it either finishes before capture or fails
  `liveInvitation` after it; R4-2a asserts this with a barrier test.
- **Staged conversion locks.** Anything that reads or writes `PERS` or a staged conversion's household must take the
  participants the existing way: `lockTeenLifecycleUsers` (gate, then `stagedTeenConversionIn`, then
  `lockConversionParticipants`: users `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` sorted by id, then `lockHousehold`, then a re-check that the lock set
  did not change, `api_teen_conversion.go:60-125`). R4-3 reuses that helper rather than locking a household ad hoc, because
  household-then-user would invert the order every conversion path uses. Its `errConversionRetry` loop applies.
- **Household predicate.** S1 and S2 (T16) are read after `lockHousehold` and in the same transaction as the seat delete and
  the `PERS` scrub; every household person and seat writer takes that lock (T16 cites), so a person or seat cannot appear between
  the read and the update, **provided that writer revalidates its account** (a writer that completes before the erasure is covered; a request already past its authorization is covered only by 9.6). R4-3 re-greps for any writer of `household_accounts` or `household_people` that does not take the lock.
- **Consent writers (F-9).** All five callers in 7.2 reach one `setConsent`; it takes only the user row lock, so adding
  the `deleted_at` re-read adds no lock and no ordering constraint. It does add a rollback path the five callers must map.
- **Call census for F-9 and the new join helpers.** `setConsent` callers: `setEmailConsent` (`email_unsubscribe.go:125`),
  `api_push.go:273,332,481`; `setEmailConsent` callers: `email_unsubscribe.go:110`, `api_email_settings.go:108`. A change
  in any of the three selectors above that adds a caller must be added here.

**R4-1 lock order (implemented; `apiAdminDeleteUser`, `account_erasure.go`).** Inside `contactMutation`, in this order:
gate → hosted events `FOR KEY SHARE` → target `FOR UPDATE` → actor plain read → in-gate admin count (plain count) → capture
(memory only) → `DELETE account_tokens` → `DELETE calendar_tokens` → `DELETE push_subscriptions` → `handOffLeadership` →
`handOffHosting` → tombstone `UPDATE users` → `queueAvatarCleanup` → audit `INSERT` → `DELETE notifications` → soft-delete
`UPDATE users` → `promoteOnDisable` → `DELETE login_throttles` (owned keys) → `contactMutation`'s `reconcileContacts`
(unchanged) → commit. The three credential deletes take the slot `revokeAccountTokens` held, before the handoffs, so the handoff
and notice locks R4-H ordered keep their place. The throttle delete is the last statement of the callback, so the erasure holds
sign-in rows only from that statement to commit. Later slices (R4-2a, R4-3, R4-4) keep this order or re-derive it, and never assume
that no cycle exists.

**R4-2a lock order (merged in PR #275, `b4092e7`; `apiAdminDeleteUser`, `account_erasure.go`).**
Inside `contactMutation`, in this order: gate → hosted events `FOR KEY SHARE` → target `FOR UPDATE` → actor read → in-gate admin
count → capture → capture sets (plain reads, no locks) → `DELETE account_tokens`, `calendar_tokens`, `push_subscriptions` →
`handOffLeadership` → `handOffHosting` → tombstone → `queueAvatarCleanup` → audit `user.deleted` → withdraw pending requests and
`notify.Resolve` → `REQ` scrub → `member_invite` ip → `guardian_email` → `DELETE notifications` → soft-delete → `promoteOnDisable` →
invitations → `guest_signups` → `mail_outboxes` → messages and audit → `message_recipients` (`statement_timeout` 20 s) →
`series_undo_receipts` → `manager_add_blocks` → registrations → `notification_deliveries` → `DELETE login_throttles` →
`reconcileContacts` → commit. The throttle delete is still the callback's last statement. The other writers it meets agree with it:

- **L1 (announcement snapshot).** `prepareMessageOnce` loads recipient facts unlocked, so a row could be inserted after the erasure
  with the erased address (its `user_id` and `copy_of_user_id` foreign keys take `FOR KEY SHARE` on the user). The snapshot
  transaction now locks every user it names `FOR KEY SHARE`, ascending id, 500 to a query, before the claim and revalidates. The
  order is users, then the message; the erasure takes the user `FOR UPDATE` and the messages later, so the orders agree.
  `TestMessageSnapshotRevalidatesRecipientsUnderLock`.
- **L2 (account-request expiry).** Expiry locks the request and then `notify.Resolve` updates its notifications; the erasure used to
  delete the person's notifications first. Every `account_requests` statement of the erasure now runs before the `notifications`
  DELETE, so both paths go requests, then notifications. `decideAccountRequest` takes the contact gate first and is serialized.
  `TestAccountRequestExpiryRacingErasure`, `TestAccountRequestApprovalLosesToErasure`.
- **L3 (guest redemption).** Redemption (no contact gate) now locks the guest's user row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, then the occurrence,
  then the signup: holder, then occurrence, then signup, the order of `api_registrations.go` and
  `api_occurrence_mutations.go`. The erasure holds the guest row and wants the occurrence (`promoteOnDisable`) or the signup, so the
  two agree. `TestGuestRedemptionRacingErasure`.

Registration removal runs in contactMutation and takes the gate first, so it serializes with erasure in both orders
(`TestRegistrationRemovalSerializesWithErasure`). A leader's removal (`apiRemoveRegistration`) is not an occurrence-first writer
racing the erasure, and the inventory records no cycle for it.

**R4-2d1b consent step (merged as PR #278, `4bb3e1e`; `eraseConsentEvidenceIn`, `account_erasure.go`).**
The R4-2a order above gains one statement: the consent scrub `UPDATE consents SET ip = '', user_agent = '', evidence_text = ''`
runs **right after the soft delete and before `promoteOnDisable`**, in the same transaction. The place is forced: migration
`00003_consent_erasure_scrub.sql` lets `reject_consent_change()` allow the update only while the owner's `users` row has
`deleted_at` set, and the transaction sees its own soft delete; before it, the statement raises `P0001` ("consent history is
append-only"). *Serialization with `setConsent`.* The erasure holds the user row `FOR UPDATE`; every consent writer reaches
`setConsent`, which takes that same row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` before it inserts. So a writer that started before the erasure
either finishes first (its row is committed before the scrub statement starts, so the scrub blanks it) or waits on the user
lock and, after the erasure commits, meets the R4-2d1a guard and writes nothing. The scrub's row locks on `consents` are taken
while holding the user row, writers only insert and take the user row first, and readers take no lock, so the scrub adds no
wait cycle and no new deadlock class. Its filter skips rows that are already blank, so a retry is idempotent. Tests:
`TestAccountErasureScrubsConsentEvidence`, `TestAccountErasureRollsBackWhole` (the whole erasure rolls back with the scrub) and
the "real erasure" subtests (`writer first`, `erasure first`) of `TestConsentWritersRefuseAfterTheOwnerIsSoftDeleted`; the
trigger and migration tests are listed under T05. The opt-in `TestAccountErasureScale` (`FOLD_ERASURE_SCALE=1`) measures the
`UPDATE` on a representative seed. This changes no D1 to D4 limit, and the erasure is still not proven deadlock-free.

*Per table.* `account_tokens`: every writer found takes the gate first (`lockCurrentCredentials`, `lockAccountTokenUser`, and the mail
prepares that call `lockContacts`), and the erasure holds the gate. `calendar_tokens`: create and scope change are gate-first
(`lockSessionCredentials`); `revokeCalendarToken` holds only the calendar row and then inserts an audit row with no foreign key, so it can
delay an erasure but never waits on it. `push_subscriptions`: subscribe locks the user before any device, so it waits on the erasure's
target lock while holding none of the account's devices; moving a device to another account locks that account and the device and needs
no lock on the erased user, and the erasure's `DELETE` re-reads `user_id` under READ COMMITTED and skips the moved row; turn-off locks the
user through `setConsent` first; single-device statements hold one row and wait on nothing else. `login_throttles`: no foreign keys;
`managerDateAddAllowedIn` runs under the gate (its callers take `lockContacts` or run inside `contactOperation`), so it is serialized
with the erasure; the single-row statements wait on at most one row while holding none, so they can delay an erasure but cannot close a
cycle by themselves; no throttle writer outside the gate holds a lock other than throttle rows while it waits, so any cycle through the
erasure's throttle rows is made only of throttle-row waits. The tombstone `UPDATE` touches the row already locked, and its trigger finds no devices.

*The erasure is not proven deadlock-free.* Each pair below is a cycle that Postgres's deadlock detector finds and breaks by aborting one
side. An aborted erasure rolls back whole and answers a generic 500 server error, and the admin can retry (the handler logs `could not delete user`).

- **D1 (pre-existing, unchanged).** `push.deliver`'s Gone transaction deletes a device, then may insert a `notification_deliveries` row whose
  `user_id` foreign key needs `KEY SHARE` on the user, which conflicts with the erasure's `FOR UPDATE`. Today's trigger delete at the session
  bump makes the same pair. An aborted push job retries.
- **D2.** `login_throttles`: the erasure's `IN`-list delete, `reserveLoginAttempt`, `reauthenticate`'s refund and `pruneLoginThrottles` lock
  rows in scan or call order, not one key order, so no common order exists. Example: the erasure locks `user:U` and waits on `email:A`, held
  by a sign-in's `reserveLoginAttempt` that waits on `ip:X`, which a refund over (`user:U`, `ip:X`) locked before waiting on `user:U`. The
  erasure against the prune over two of the account's stale keys taken in opposite order is another. A common order would need sorted locking
  in the sign-in SQL, which R4-1 does not change. An aborted sign-in or reauthentication answers its existing 500; an aborted prune returns
  its error and the daily job retries.
- **D3.** `push_subscriptions`: the erasure's `user_id` delete against `prunePushDevices` or `writeVAPIDKeys`' delete-all, when both want two
  or more of the account's devices in opposite order. `ensureVAPIDKeys`' boot reset also takes `KEY SHARE` on every active admin after
  deleting every device, which conflicts with the erasure's `FOR UPDATE` on an admin target; it runs before its own process serves, so it can
  meet an erasure only when another process serves the same database. An aborted prune retries with its daily job, and an aborted rotation
  returns its error to the CLI or the boot step.

- **D4 (R4-2a; kept limit, recorded and not fixed).** A guest redemption that asked for an account holds the occurrence `O` and the
  signup, then `createGuestUpgradeRequest` and `notifyAccountRequested` call `notify.Send` for every active admin, whose recipient
  lock takes `FOR KEY SHARE` on each admin's user row. The erasure of one of those admins holds that row `FOR UPDATE` and, in
  `promoteOnDisable`, waits for `O`. Postgres finds the cycle and aborts one side with SQLSTATE `40P01`. The class predates R4-2a
  (`promoteOnDisable` is PR 109). *Evidence.* `TestErasureDeadlockWithGuestAdminNoticeRollsBackLoser` records each statement's
  SQLSTATE through `erasureTestSQLStates` (a generic HTTP 500 proves nothing) and requires exactly one `40P01`, on redemption's
  recipient lock. *Deterministic loser.* The schedule makes the erasure wait for `O` first and pass its single deadlock check before
  redemption starts waiting, so redemption is the later waiter, detects the cycle and loses: the erasure answers 200 and
  redemption a generic 500. That order is a property of the test's schedule only; in production either side can be the one
  aborted. *Rollback.* The race must leave exactly the committed state of a control run that executes only the erasure on an
  identical seed (a winner-only comparison): no guest user, registration, `guest_upgrade` request, waiting task or
  `account.requested` audit row from the loser, and the signup stays pending, so the guest can retry. *Fixes considered and not
  taken.* Locking occurrences before the user needs facts that are known only after the user lock, and reverses L3. Locking the
  admins in redemption would change `notifyAccountRequested` and still miss an admin promoted during the transaction. Taking the
  exclusive contact gate in redemption would queue every public guest confirmation behind every contact mutation. If the reviewer
  rejects this limit, the admin prelock becomes a separate later task. An aborted erasure rolls back whole, as for D1 to D3.
  Not proven deadlock-free.

*Cross-connection re-grep (R4-1-2).* Every call of `reserveLoginAttempt`, `lockLoginKeysAtLimit`, `reauthenticate`, `throttleHit`,
`throttleAllow`, `targetAllow` and `accountRequestHit` runs at handler level, outside any transaction callback (`api_auth.go`,
`api_profile.go`, `api_account_requests.go`, `api_guest.go`, `api_teen_guardians.go`, `api_guardian_email.go`, `api_people.go`; in
`apiRequestEmailChange` the `lockCurrentCredentials` transaction closes before the two throttle calls), and `managerDateAddAllowedIn` runs
only under the gate. So no throttle helper waits on `a.DB` from inside another transaction's callback, which would be a wait between two
connections that Postgres cannot see. This is a source reading at R4-1 and must be repeated by any slice that adds a throttle caller.

### 9.6 Household writers racing an erasure (R40-SEC-08; F-10; closed in R4-3)

**R4-3a status:** requirements 1 to 4 are implemented by PR #277 (`e806cf3`). The diagnosis below describes the pre-R4-3a writers unless otherwise stated. Requirement 5's closure was chosen in [15.5](#155-r4-3b-claim-household-and-safeguarding-verdicts-and-the-requirement-5-choice-recorded-nov-5-2026) (option (a), `NOWAIT` plus a bounded whole-transaction retry).

**R4-3b status (staged):** requirements 5 and 6 are implemented by R4-CTX and R4-3b-1/2, committed locally on the integration branch and recorded in [9.6.1](#961-r4-3b-implementation-record-lock-census-retry-and-limits-staged) and [15.6](#156-r4-ctx-and-r4-3b-implementation-record-staged-recorded-nov-5-2026). They are not pushed or merged, and the landing evidence is the lead's. **The system is not proven deadlock-free**: the `NOWAIT` closure covers the household acquisition only, and the occurrence/notice cycle (D4-class) it does not cover is recorded, with its single 40P01, in 9.6.1.

*Traced from source; no interleaving was executed.* `authenticatedSessionUser` (`api.go:353-376`) loads the user once per
request and compares `session_version`, `logout_version` and `CanSignIn()` at that moment. A request already past it keeps
that `*models.User` (`apiUser(c)`); erasing the account afterwards changes the session for *later* requests only.

**The two orders** for the household update (`apiUpdateMyHousehold`); person writers and first-use creation have the same shape:

- *Erasure first (the defect).* (1) The request authorizes from the preloaded user: `manageMyHousehold` →
  `ensureHousehold` → `CanManageHousehold` (`api_profile.go:763-773`). (2) The erasure transaction takes the account, deletes
  its seat (T14), anonymizes the sole-subject household and commits. (3) The request's transaction takes `lockHousehold` (it
  waits only for the erasure's household lock) and runs `UPDATE households SET name, address` (`:1067-1079`): the submitted
  name and address are back on the scrubbed household, or are written into a shared household by someone who no longer has
  an account. Nothing between (1) and (3) rereads the account, its session or its seat.
- *Writer first.* The request holds the household lock; the erasure, which takes the user row `FOR UPDATE` first, then the
  household lock, so it waits for the household lock today (and for the user lock once requirement 1 below is in place), then
  reads the committed `name`/`address` for S1/S2 and scrubs or retains by T16. Safe only because the erasure reads after the lock.
- *First use.* A user with no household sends `PUT /me/household` or `POST /me/household/people`. `ensureHousehold` finds no seat
  (`householdAccountIn`, `:721`), and `a.DB.Transaction` creates `households` (`defaultHouseholdName(u)`, the preloaded
  surname) and the seat (`:733-744`). If the erasure committed after the user was loaded, the foreign keys
  `household_accounts.user_id` and `households.created_by` (baseline 3491, 3523) are satisfied by the soft-deleted row, so a
  surname-bearing household and a seat appear for the tombstone.

**Writers that can run from a stale request, and writers that cannot** (the relevant inventory; *read*, not run):

| # | Writer | Loads user and seat before its transaction | Locks inside | Revalidates account, session, seat | Personal data it could write after an erasure |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `apiUpdateMyHousehold` `api_profile.go:1032` | yes (`manageMyHousehold`) | `lockHousehold` `:1068` | **no** | `households.name`, `address` |
| 2 | first-use creation in `ensureHousehold` `:720-757` (reached through 1 and 3 only) | yes | none (plain transaction) | **no** | new `households.name` from the stale surname; a `household_accounts` seat; `household.created` audit row |
| 3 | `apiAddHouseholdPerson` `:1188` | yes | `lockHousehold` `:1205` | **no** | `household_people` names, birth date, care notes; a pending `guardian_links` row; an admin waiting task |
| 4 | `apiUpdateHouseholdPerson` `:1269` (`loadMyHouseholdPerson` `:1237-1265`) | yes | `lockHousehold` `:1338`, then re-reads the person | **no** | `household_people` names, birth date, care notes |
| 5 | `apiDeleteHouseholdPerson` `:1443` | yes | `lockHousehold` `:1450` | **no** | none new; a removal audit row and notice naming the erased actor |
| 6 | `apiAdminCreateHousehold` `api_admin_household.go:115`, `apiAdminAddHouseholdPerson` `:181` | no | gate, then users `NO KEY UPDATE` sorted (`lockAdminHouseholdParticipants` `:49-72`), then `lockHousehold` `:209` | **yes**: `CanSignIn()`, session version, `CanDelegateHousehold(holder.CanSignIn())` | none after an erasure (assert only) |
| 7 | conversion issue `api_household_conversion.go:268-273`, cancel `:429-433`, accept `:505-513`, approve `:678`, completion `api_teen_conversion.go:68,114` | no | actor or participant users `NO KEY UPDATE` (sorted), then `lockHousehold` | **yes** (status and `DeletedAt` of holder and inviter are checked; they add seats only for the *new* account) | none for the erased account (assert only) |
| 8 | `apiGetMyHousehold` `:1007` | yes | none | n/a: read-only, creates nothing (`:1000-1006`) | none |

The registration routes that read `householdAccountIn` (`api_registrations.go:984,1926`) write registrations, not household
rows; they are the self-service-writer class of 9.1 and F-9, which R4-1 and R4-2a own, not this table's.

**What R4-3 must do** (requirements; the code is the slice's):

1. In rows 1 to 5, inside the writer's transaction and **before** `lockHousehold`, lock the acting user's row
   `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, as `apiCancelHouseholdConversion` does (`api_household_conversion.go:429-431`), and refuse when the
   fresh row is not `CanSignIn()` or its `session_version` differs from the request's, the check
   `lockCurrentCredentials` makes (`account_security.go:28-30`), mapped as `errSecuritySession` is mapped elsewhere
   (`api_profile.go:187`). No new status, error code or wording (DEC-52, DEC-61).
2. Under that lock and the household lock, re-read the account's seat and recompute `CanManageHousehold` from the fresh user
   and seat; a missing seat, a role change or a teen account refuses. The household used for the write is the one the fresh
   seat names, not the one loaded before the transaction.
3. First use (row 2) takes the same user lock and checks `householdAccountIn` again under it; it builds the default name from
   the **locked** user, not the preloaded copy. The existing unique-seat conflict path (`:747-754`) stays.
4. **Lock order** is user, then household, with no route taking a household before a user. That matches the erasure (gate,
   user `FOR UPDATE`, household), every conversion path (`lockConversionParticipants`, `api_household_conversion.go:505-513`)
   and `lockAdminHouseholdParticipants`, so it adds no inversion against erasure.
5. **One inversion remains and must be closed or shown absent in R4-3's plan, with a test.** Rows 3 and 4 write the
   pending-link notice and admin waiting task while holding the household lock (`notifyGuardianLinkPending`, N-11); that insert takes a
   key-share lock on each admin recipient. An admin `A` with a seat in household `H` is erased by an erasure that holds `A`
   `FOR UPDATE` and waits for `H`, while a household member's person-add holds `H` and waits for `A`: a cycle the database
   would break by aborting one side. Two admissible closures, neither chosen here: the erasure takes `H` with `NOWAIT`
   after the user lock and retries the whole erasure a bounded number of times on SQLSTATE 55P03, so it never waits while holding the user
   row; or the writers lock their recipients (sorted) before `H`, which belongs with R4-N's recipient locking (T26,
   9.2.1). R4-3 and R4-N each state which, in their PR. (R4-N's statement is in 9.2.4: neither; it adds only the `notify.WithRecipients` / `Msg.Scope` seam.)
6. **Shared households and their members are not touched.** The revalidation only stops a stale writer. Seats, persons and
   guardian links of other living members, the shared household's name and address, and T15/T16's retained cases stay as
   written; nothing here deletes a household or a member.

**R4-3 barrier tests** (real router, real session cookie, so the middleware loads the user and the handler authorizes from it
before the transaction; every pause point is a test-owned lock or a test-created trigger in the scratch database, never a
production hook or a sleep; the wait condition is a backend in `pg_stat_activity` with `wait_event_type = 'Lock'`):

- *Erasure first.* For each of rows 1, 3, 4 and 5, the test opens an uncommitted transaction that runs the erasure's
  statements for user `U` (user `FOR UPDATE`, seat deleted, household anonymized, soft delete), sends the request, waits for its
  backend to block, then commits. Assert: the route's refusal answer; the household is exactly the scrubbed values
  (`Removed household`, `''`) or, for a shared household, unchanged; no new `households`, `household_accounts`,
  `household_people` or `guardian_links` row for `U`; no `household.*` audit row by `U` after the erasure; no notification.
- *Writer first.* The request is paused after it holds the user and household locks (a test-created trigger on the route's
  audit insert that waits for a test-held advisory lock); the test starts the real erasure and waits until it blocks on the user
  lock; the test releases; the request commits; the erasure then reads the committed rows. Assert: a sole-subject household ends
  `Removed household` and `''` with none of the submitted text left in a household, person, link, notice or audit column; a shared one
  is retained and counted; neither side reports SQLSTATE 40P01 or a 500.
- *First use.* For `PUT /me/household` and `POST /me/household/people` by a user with no household, the same two orders:
  erasure first leaves no household bearing the surname, no seat for `U` and no `household.created` row; writer first yields a
  sole-subject household that the erasure then scrubs and unseats. Two concurrent first uses for one user still leave one household.
- *Admin recipient.* Requirement 5's cycle: an erased admin who is a recipient of a writer holding the same household.
- *Assertions only.* Row 6 and row 7 writers refuse a soft-deleted holder, inviter or actor.

*Limits.* No other self-service writer was re-traced here (profile, avatar, push, consent): 9.1's re-grep and F-9 own them.

#### 9.6.1 R4-3b implementation record: lock census, retry and limits (staged)

Written for task R4-3b-V from the committed code (`account_erasure_households.go`, `contact_lifecycle.go`, `api_admin.go`) and the reviews `r43b1-recheck.json`, `r43b1-integrated-review.json`, `r43b2-recheck.json` and `r43b2-integrated-review.json` (all verdict `pass`, no open findings). It is a record, not a review signature, and it states no test result for the landing head: the evidence for that head belongs to the lead's landing record (15.6). The text above this subsection is the pre-implementation diagnosis and the requirements; where this record departs from it, this record says so.

**The census (requirement 5, condition 1).** Partly rechecked by `TestErasureHouseholdLockCensus`, which parses the production source and fails when the set of `lockHousehold` callers changes, when a `households ... FOR UPDATE` literal appears outside the erasure helper, or when the deletion callback's three calls move; it does not check the lock orders and classifications written below.

*A. What the deletion callback of `apiAdminDeleteUser` locks, in order.*

| Step | Lock | Taken by |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | the contact gate (advisory, exclusive) | `contactMutationCtx`; for a call with a deadline its first statement is a `set_config` of `statement_timeout` and `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` (15.6) |
| 2 | hosted events `FOR KEY SHARE`, then the account row `FOR UPDATE`; the acting admin is a plain read | `deletionHostPlan` |
| 3 | none (plain reads of both PERS arms, unscoped) | `captureErasureSetsIn`, `validatePersIn` |
| 4 | the households of the account's seat and of PERS, `FOR UPDATE NOWAIT`, ascending id | `lockErasureHouseholdsIn`; the only place the erasure takes a `households` row |
| 5 | the callback's writes in their existing order: `eraseAccountCredentialsIn`, `handOffLeadership` (groups), `handOffHosting` (events), the tombstone, the `user.deleted` audit row, `eraseAccountRequestsIn`, the notification delete, the soft delete, the consent-evidence and contact-request-text scrubs | unchanged since R4-1, R4-2a and R4-T07A (9.5) |
| 6 | the household step: the staged person's occurrences ascending (inside `reconcileHouseholdPerson`, under the household), then `guardian_links` `FOR UPDATE` in ascending id; no household row | `eraseHouseholdRecordsIn` |
| 7 | `promoteOnDisable`'s own ascending occurrence set, then `eraseDependentRecordsIn` and last `eraseOwnedThrottleKeysIn` (D2) | unchanged |

No erasure helper (`handOffLeadership`, `handOffHosting`, `eraseAccountRequestsIn`, `promoteOnDisable`, `eraseDependentRecordsIn`) reaches `lockHousehold` or a `households` row; `promoteLocked` and `eligibleRegistration` read households and seats with plain `SELECT`s, which take no row lock. The test also fails on any file other than `account_erasure_households.go` that locks a `households` row in SQL, and on a deletion callback that does not call `captureErasureSetsIn`, `lockErasureHouseholdsIn` and `eraseAccountCredentialsIn` exactly once each, in that order.

*B. Every caller of `lockHousehold` (15 at this commit; the plan's "16 sites" counted the definition at `registration_subjects.go:89`).* Each row was traced from the committed source. The column "Order into the household" lists the locks the transaction takes before `lockHousehold`; "Holds the household while it" lists what the same transaction does afterwards, and several callers only write the household's own rows and an audit row. What the erasure needs from the census is narrower than "everyone notifies": a caller that takes only the household and then writes rows nothing else waits on cannot be the second party of a cycle, and a caller that goes on to `notify.Send` (key-share locks on its recipients, ascending), lock another user, a guardian link or an occurrence while holding the household can be, in either order, which is why the erasure's household lock is `NOWAIT`. "Gate" is the contact gate (`lockContacts`, advisory); "actor" is the acting user row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`. A new caller fails the census until a person adds its name to the test; this document's classification of it is then added by the same person and checked in review.

| Caller | Order into the household | Holds the household while it |
|---|---|---|
| `apiUpdateMyHousehold` (`api_profile.go`) | actor (`lockHouseholdSeat`, no gate), then household | updates the household's name and address and writes one audit row; no notice and no other lock |
| `apiAddHouseholdPerson` | actor, then household | inserts the person; for a kind that needs a guardian, `createGuardianLink` and its admin waiting task (`notifyGuardianLinkPending`, N-11: key-share locks on every admin); audit |
| `apiUpdateHouseholdPerson` | actor, then household | person edit; conditionally `revokePersonConversion`, `revokeGuardianLinks` (resolves a pending task) or `createGuardianLink` (admin task) when the kind changes; `reconcileHouseholdPerson` (occurrences `FOR UPDATE` ascending, registration audit, `notifyRegistrationRemoved`, `promoteLocked`); audit |
| `apiDeleteHouseholdPerson` | actor, then household | `revokePersonConversion`, `reconcileHouseholdPerson` (as above), `revokeGuardianLinks`, audit, soft delete |
| `apiAdminAddHouseholdPerson` (`api_admin_household.go`) | gate, actor, holder and guardian (one statement, ascending id), then household | inserts the person, `createGuardianLink` and its admin task, audit |
| `issueHouseholdConversion` (`api_household_conversion.go`; create and resend) | actor, then household | invitation address checks (`lockInvitationEmail`), invitation insert or rotation, audit, and an invitation-mail job; no notice and no other user lock |
| `apiCancelHouseholdConversion` | actor, then household | revokes the open invitation and writes its audit row; no notice |
| `acceptHouseholdConversion` | no gate; every participant user (`conversionLockIDs`: inviter, the household's seat holders, the registrations' holders, a teen's confirmed guardians) ascending, then household | the invitation row `FOR UPDATE`, `createConversionApprovalRequest` (request row, audit, admin notices: key-share locks on admins) |
| `approveHouseholdConversion` (reached from `decideAccountRequest`) | gate (`contactMutation`), then `lockConversionParticipants` (participants and the deciding admin, ascending), then household | the new account, a teen's pending guardian links (`requestTeenGuardian`), and for an adult the transfer: occurrences `FOR UPDATE` ascending, registrations, the seat, the `HouseholdConverted` notice |
| `lockConversionParticipants` (`api_teen_conversion.go`) | participants ascending (the caller holds the gate), then household | a lock helper: returns with the household held; its callers (`decideAccountRequest`; `lockTeenLifecycleUsers` for a staged teen) continue with requests, guardian links, groups or occurrences |
| `lockTeenLifecycleUsers` | gate, then either (staged teen conversion) `lockConversionParticipants`, or the named users ascending and then the household of the teen's own seat, if any | a lock helper: returns with the household held; its callers (`decideGuardianLink` teen links, `lockConsentUsers` for the teen-consent changes, the teen rollover in `rolloverAges`) continue with links, consent rows, `completeTeenConversionIfReady` and its notices |
| `decideGuardianLink` (`api_admin.go`), **teen link** (`teen_user_id` set) | `lockTeenLifecycleUsers`: gate, users ascending, household if any, then the link row `FOR UPDATE` | `notify.Resolve`, audit, on confirm `completeTeenConversionIfReady` and a consent-mail job |
| `decideGuardianLink`, **person link** (`person_id` set) | no gate and no user lock; the person's household, then the link row `FOR UPDATE` | `notify.Resolve` and audit; no teen lock and no conversion |
| `rolloverAges` (`age_rollover.go`, the household-child loop, `flagHouseholdChild13`, N-05) | household first; no gate and no user lock | the audit row and `notify.Send` to the child's confirmed guardians (key-share locks on them, after the household) |
| `apiRegister` (`api_registrations.go`) | gate only on the gated repeat; the signed-in user and the holders of already registered household people ascending; household **only when the request names household people**; then `lockOccurrence` | registrations, notices and `promoteLocked` |
| `addHouseholdRegistration` | the holder user (no gate, not the acting manager), then household, then `lockOccurrence` | the registration, audit, the notices and promotion that follow |

Reading the table: A caller serializes before holding the household when it takes the contact gate or an earlier conflicting lock on the erased account. A lock on a different acting user does not establish that serialization. A caller that takes the household with no earlier user lock (`rolloverAges`, the person-link branch of `decideGuardianLink`) can hold a household while a later lock waits, and `apiRegister` and `addHouseholdRegistration` take user rows of other people before it; those orders are what the erasure's `NOWAIT` acquisition answers by failing fast and retrying rather than waiting. This is a reading of the source, not a deadlock-freedom proof (see the limits below). `TestErasureHouseholdLockCensus` verifies only the set of caller names (a new or removed caller fails), that no `households ... FOR UPDATE` string literal appears outside `account_erasure_households.go`, and that `apiAdminDeleteUser` calls `captureErasureSetsIn`, `lockErasureHouseholdsIn` and `eraseAccountCredentialsIn` once each in that order. The rows' classifications and lock orders are checked by review, not by the test.

**Requirement 5's closure and the reviewer's six conditions** (15.5, option (a)).

1. *Every acquisition.* One site, `lockErasureHouseholdsIn`; the census above found no other household acquisition on the erasure path, including the helpers a staged conversion uses.
2. *Rollback releases first.* On 55P03 the callback returns, `contactMutationCtx` rolls back, and a new attempt starts only if that ROLLBACK was acknowledged (`rollbackConfirmed`): the acknowledgment proves the server released the gate and the user locks. A 55P03 inside a `*contactRollbackError` (rollback not acknowledged) ends the loop at once with no further BEGIN.
3. *Fresh state.* Each attempt builds `&models.User{ID: targetID}` and reads the account, the actor, the sets and the household facts again under its own locks; `body` and `hostPicks` are only read.
4. *Bounded.* `householdAttempts` = 3; a new attempt starts only within `deleteRetryWindow` (2 s) of the first and while the request budget (`adminDeleteBudget`, 40 s) is live, after backoffs of 25 ms and 100 ms taken with `select` on `ctx.Done()`; inside an attempt the bound is the context and the server cap of 15.6. Exhaustion answers 409 `delete_household_busy` and nothing is deleted (`COMMIT` was never sent); context expiry answers 503 `delete_timed_out`.
5. *Transactional.* Audit rows, notices and delivery intents are written in the attempt's transaction, so a rolled-back attempt leaves none and a retry duplicates nothing committed.
6. *Both schedules, with evidence.* `TestHouseholdNoWaitAdminRecipientOfPersonAdd`, `TestHouseholdNoWaitHolderNotifiedByPersonRemoval`, `TestHouseholdNoWaitGuardianNotifiedByAgeRollover` and `TestHouseholdNoWaitPromotionRecipient` run writer-first and erasure-first with real writers, `erasureTestSQLStates` and `pg_blocking_pids`; writer-first gives 55P03, rollback and a committing later attempt, with no 40P01. `TestHouseholdNoWaitPersOnlySoftDeletedPerson`, `TestHouseholdNoWaitSeveralHouseholdsAscending` and `TestErasureHouseholdLockSelection` prove households selected only through PERS (soft-deleted people included), deduplication and ascending acquisition, a real 55P03 on the held highest household, release of the earlier locks before the retry and an unchanged database after the rollback. Through the fault proxy (`internal/testdb/pgproxy.go`): `TestAdminDeleteHouseholdBusyExhaustsThreeAttempts` (BEGIN/ROLLBACK three times, three real 55P03, database unchanged), `TestAdminDeleteHouseholdBusyRollbackNotAcknowledged` (one attempt, no BEGIN after the failed ROLLBACK, 409), `TestAdminDeleteHouseholdBusyThenCommitUnknown` (attempt 1 busy, attempt 2 commits with the acknowledgment dropped: BRBC, 500 `delete_outcome_unknown`, no third attempt, exactly one `user.deleted` row) and `TestAdminDeleteHouseholdBusyBoundedAttempt` (503 `delete_timed_out`, the attempt's server release observed). Unit: `TestHouseholdRetryOnlyRepeatsAConfirmedBusy`, `TestHouseholdRetryStopsWithTheWindowAndTheContext`.

**Requirement 6** (shared households and their members are not touched): the household step touches only households `lockErasureHouseholdsIn` locked, writes only the erased account's seat, PERS and the households the T16 predicate proves sole-subject, and never another person's seat, person row, or the name and address of a household with a living holder. Guardian links are the one relationship it does change beyond PERS: the selector in `eraseHouseholdRecordsIn` revokes every pending or confirmed link with `teen_user_id = U`, `guardian_user_id = U` or `person_id` in PERS, so erasing a guardian revokes that guardian's links to surviving children or teens (outside PERS and outside any locked household's erased set), one `GuardianLinkRevoked` audit row each (M16 and M17, O-4; T12 for PERS). That revokes the relationship only; the surviving person, their row and their household are not anonymized or scrubbed, and a household with another seat of any status is retained and counted.

**How the retry answers** (the commit phase first, `classifyContactErr`): an unknown COMMIT result is never retried and answers 500 `delete_outcome_unknown` ("Fold couldn't confirm whether the account was deleted. Reload this person's page..."), never copy that says nothing changed; a timed-out transaction answers 503 `delete_timed_out`; a busy household that survives the attempts, or whose rollback was not acknowledged, answers 409 `delete_household_busy` (COMMIT was never sent in either case, so "nothing was deleted" is true; only the acknowledged ROLLBACK also proves the gate and the locks were released, which is why an unacknowledged one stops with no further BEGIN and is never retried); any other error is the generic 500 as before. The three codes are sent through `jsonErrorCode` with lines in `ERROR_COPY` (`web/src/lib/errors.ts`) and need no `api_errors.go` entry; `TestWebErrorCopyCoversEveryCode` finds them from the call sites.

**Limits (plainly).**

- **The system is not proven deadlock-free.** `NOWAIT` closes the household acquisition only. The erasure takes occurrence locks in two ascending runs (the staged person's, inside `reconcileHouseholdPerson`, then `promoteOnDisable`'s own set), and two ascending runs are not one global order. A writer that holds a later occurrence and then asks for the erased user's row (a recipient lock) closes a **D4-class cycle** through an occurrence, not a household. PostgreSQL breaks it with exactly one 40P01; if the erasure is the side it aborts, the whole transaction rolls back and the admin gets the generic 500 `could not delete user` and can retry; **nothing retries 40P01 automatically**. Recorded instances, both with one 40P01, the test-owned writer as the loser and no 55P03: `TestHouseholdDeadlockThroughAnOccurrenceIsRecordedNotRetried` and `TestHouseholdStagedErasureMeetsOccurrenceWriters` (a staged person on two dates). The review notes that these force the writer to lose, so they show the cycle exists and is detected; they do not show an aborted production erasure.
- The other schedules of R4-3b-LOCK-UPGRADE close or are serialized: a household writer in either order (`TestHouseholdStagedErasureAgainstAHouseholdWriter`) and the multi-occurrence series writer (`api_series_registrations.go`), which takes the contact gate first and so queues behind the erasure instead of closing a cycle (`TestHouseholdStagedErasureAgainstASeriesWriter`). The pre-existing qualified admin email lock upgrade is unchanged and stays a qualified limit.
- The bounded retry can starve under sustained household contention; after three attempts or 2 seconds the admin sees 409 and nothing was deleted. It is a bound, not a fairness guarantee.
- **After a failed ROLLBACK or a client deadline, return is not release.** The server may keep the gate and the row locks until Terminate, socket close or the idle timeout; the bound is D + S + I (15.6): for the admin route, the 40 s deadline D plus the 20 s statement cap S plus an idle timeout I of at most 40 s. No test equates the return time with the release time.
- First-use creation is two transactions (T16): an erasure that commits in the gap scrubs the new household and refuses the second step; the first step's `household.created` row stays.
- `FOR UPDATE NOWAIT` and the lock-mode facts are measured only on the disposable PostgreSQL 16 databases of the tests above; no real database or provider was examined. This record states no pass for the landing head.

## 10. Migration authority and the baseline guard

1. **Authority.** PLAN 14.1 (`PLAN.md:1520`): "Before the first church, DEC-22 and DEC-138 together allow editing
   the single baseline migration directly; the window closes at the first tagged release or first church
   deployment." DEC-138 repeats the boundary (`PLAN.md:2354`, "allowed only until the first tagged release or first
   church deployment, whichever comes first"), `AGENTS.md` states the exception, and the baseline file's own header
   (`00001_baseline.sql:6-8`) says the same. PLAN 14.10 (`PLAN.md:2151-2153`) orders "M7.7 (baseline) before any brief
   that adds schema (…parts of M7.4/M7.5) unless its table folds into the baseline". The accepted plan read that as
   M7.4 schema work being allowed to fold into the baseline; the sentence actually sets an *order* (M7.7 first, now
   done) with an "unless it folds in" escape, and the authority to edit the baseline is 14.1 and DEC-138. This draft
   relies on 14.1/DEC-138, not on a reading of 14.10.
2. **`00-index.md` item 6** ("except M7.7, which is the one licensed baseline rewrite") is older R9-era wording,
   not an additional M7.4 gate; it is corrected narrowly in this PR (M7.7's status is untouched).
3. **Evidence.** R9 S4/S5 records: "The S4 merge refresh found no tags, releases, deployments or retained
   release-workflow runs" and "DEC-138's pre-live baseline window still closes at the first tagged release or
   church deployment" (`R9-baseline-squash.md:1692-1697`). `git tag` lists nothing in this checkout (read in this task). **No remote
   refresh was performed for R4-0.** Before any baseline edit, a freshly refreshed and independently
   reviewed check of remote tags, releases, published images and recorded deployments is required, and again
   immediately before schema work. Absence evidence from an unauthenticated registry call is not enough. If the
   window is not shown open, use the additive route. No owner database is reset, no tag or release is created.
   *R4-2d1b refresh (Nov 5, 2026, 05:26 UTC, GitHub as `grierj`, read-only).* Queried: remote tag refs, releases, release-workflow
   runs, GHCR package versions (active and deleted), deployments, and `STATUS.md`, `docs/FIRST-CHURCH-CHECKS.md` and
   `docs/DEPLOY-DIGITALOCEAN.md` for any church-deployment receipt. Result: tags, releases (0), release runs (0) and deployments
   (0) listed complete and empty; both GHCR package-version reads answered HTTP 404 "Package not found" (coverage incomplete); no
   doc records a church deployment. Overall coverage incomplete, so **window unestablished**; the remote tag listing contained no `v*` refs at that time, so it showed no tag containing 00002 and no R4-SC eligibility. Additive route kept; O-15 stays gated for any baseline edit. O-15 and O-2
   stay open; this refresh establishes no publication authority and authorizes no publication. Raw outputs:
   `results/r4-2d1b-o15.json` (autoflow state, not in the repository).
4. **The guard.** `TestBaselineFileShape` (`migrations/migrations_test.go:1766-1784`) currently requires that the
   migrations directory contain exactly `00001_baseline.sql` ("until the DEC-138 window closes", the comment at
   `1766-1769`). The first slice that adds a numbered migration (`00002_...`) must update that test and comment in
   the same pull request to permit valid numbered files after the baseline, **keeping** every baseline-content
   check, the migration validation and the schema drift check, with independent database/performance review.
   Never delete the guard, and never switch to a baseline edit merely to satisfy its file count.
   *Updated by R4-S:* the test now allows the baseline plus consecutive numbered files from `00002`, checked by the pure
   helper `migrationFileShapeProblems` (with its own rejection tests), and still checks the baseline's content, the
   migration validation and the schema drift check. R4-S added `00002_suppression_address_hash.sql`. R4-SG (PR #276) runs scripts/contract-gate.sh in the test job; a contract migration must name its expand on a `-- contracts:` line.
   *R4-2d1b:* `00003_consent_erasure_scrub.sql` is additive; it relaxes reject_consent_change only for the erasure scrub of a soft-deleted owner's rows; its Down restores the baseline body.
5. No migration is written in R4-0.

## 11. Restore, backups and what erasure cannot promise

- Erasure reaches the live database and the blob store. Copies in nightly dumps (14 days; 1st-of-month dumps 180
  days), offsite age-encrypted copies (retention set by the operator's bucket), weekly provider droplet backups and
  snapshots (until the operator deletes them), and `uploads-*.tgz` age out under that retention. The delete dialog
  must say so before the admin commits (R4-6).
- **A backup taken before an erasure has no knowledge of it.** Restoring it brings the person's rows back
  active. Operators must, *before reopening access or mail delivery after a restore*, reconcile every erasure made
  since that backup (re-run the deletions; the R4-6 sweep catches restored *tombstones* but cannot know about
  accounts erased after the dump), restore database and uploads together, and re-check the suppression and
  opt-out state. No universal promise is made that every copy is gone. Anyone who already held database or backup
  access had the data; that is an access decision.
- **After R4-1, clearing `deleted_at` cannot undo an account deletion.** The user row's name, phone, birth date and password hash
  have been scrubbed, and its tokens, calendar link and devices deleted. A backup may restore those values, subject to the
  reconciliation above. Dependent records can still hold personal details until their owning slices, including R4-2a and R4-3, land.
- No external erasure ledger is invented.

---

column, seeds adult, teen, guardian, guest, converted-person and legacy cases with scalar, JSON, error and blob
sentinels, and runs the opt-out and suppression stages separately so a retained plaintext address or a missing
address-level opt-out is never hidden by a generic allowlist. Hand-checks that need a real server go into
`docs/FIRST-CHURCH-CHECKS.md` in that slice's PR.

**A merged slice is not a finished milestone.** After R4-0 is signed, each slice above can merge on its own evidence, described
as staged. M7.4 itself is declared complete only when (1) hashed suppression storage and the signed opt-out behavior exist, or an
authorized PLAN change says otherwise (7.7), **and** (2) the personal IP residue of 6.5.2 (IR-1 to IR-4) is empty, or the owner has

true: it needs an approved, implemented and independently reviewed design with the safeguarding judgment, and a disposition for
what the scope does not reach. No reviewer acceptance, report count or coder statement substitutes
for (2). Work that does not depend on the DG-9 choice (R4-N, R4-H, R4-5, R4-S, R4-1, R4-2a apart from its T07 statements,

### 12.1 R4-6b tombstone pipeline (staged, Nov 6, 2026)

`f95a474` implements `eraseTombstoneOnce` and the unavailable-address interface.
`e0324e7` registers the sweep and its page and subject jobs. Both are local
commits. The R4-6b-V record was committed locally in `2d43d8b` and merged
locally as `eee88af`; it is not published (12.3).

One subject runs in `a.contactMutationCtx(ctx, contactChange{UserID: id}, cb)`.
It takes the exclusive contact gate before the user row. The worker supplies a
two-minute context. R4-CTX bounds server lock release by D + S + I, here two
minutes + 20 seconds + two minutes, within the runner's 15-minute lease.
Return is not release. Reserve, rollback and COMMIT errors propagate to the
runner; a confirmed no-work rollback alone becomes a successful no-op.
The job does not use the admin handler's immediate household retry loop.

| Step | Implemented work and condition |
|---|---|
| L0 | `lockTombstoneIn` reads unscoped and locks `FOR UPDATE` only when `deleted_at IS NOT NULL` and email is exactly `'deleted-' \|\| id \|\| '@deleted.invalid'`. An arbitrary deleted row or living lookalike is a no-op. A committed numeric marker at least the requested version is also a no-op. |
| L1 | Create an id-only `erasureSubject`, capture REQ, PERS and INV by retained id links, validate PERS, then lock all selected households `NOWAIT` in ascending id. A contradiction aborts before writes; 55P03 rolls back the subject and returns to the runner. |
| L2 | Delete existing account tokens, calendar links and push devices by user id, before changing status. |
| L3 | Scrub only a dirty tombstone's personal user fields and disable it. Email and `session_version` stay unchanged. The sole users trigger, `push_devices_end_with_sessions`, finds no devices after L2, so it queues no device notice. Clean rows keep `updated_at`. |
| L4 | Queue `avatar.cleanup` even when `avatar_key` is empty, since only storage can find orphan blobs. At most one identical queued cleanup exists; this is a named maintenance effect. |
| L5 | Withdraw pending REQ and resolve their tasks with a system actor; scrub only dirty REQ, preserving member-inviter IP unless the erased user is that inviter. Skip the guardian-address match. |
| L6 | Delete the subject's stored notifications by user id. |
| L7 | Blank only nonempty consent IP, user agent and evidence; keep choices, scope, channel and time. No address-level opt-out export exists in this stage. |
| L8 | Blank only nonempty occurrence-add ages notes for the subject through `eraseContactRequestTextIn`. `reason` stays untouched under R4-T07B (migration `00007` is a dormant licence with no caller). |
| L9 | Run `eraseHouseholdRecordsIn` with system actor and empty IP. Delete the subject's seat, anonymize PERS without completing a staged conversion, reconcile future active staged registrations, revoke live guardian links, delete unused teen tokens and scrub only proven sole-subject households. Re-evaluate every locked household, including a single erased owner's household even if that owner already has a marker. Retain and count shared or ambiguous households. O-4 safeguarding prose stays. |
| L10 | `promoteOnDisable(tx, id, false)` promotes eligible waiters that fit outside the cutoff. Maintenance skips cutoff notices about seats that already did not count. |
| L11 | Scrub only dirty or still-open INV, cancel only scheduled authored messages, scrub dirty or queued recipient rows and registration fields, and delete existing id-owned activity rows. Guest-signup and development-outbox address arms are skipped. |
| L12 | Delete existing tombstone host rows, auditing each as `deleted_host_cleanup` (T08). No successor is chosen and no event is changed. |
| L13 | `eraseAuditDetailsIn` blanks attributable IPs and subtracts only the listed subject-owned metadata keys. All conversion-IP retention rules, safeguarding metadata and G-8 and G-9 limits remain. |
| L14 | Delete existing owned throttle keys by id only. Shared IP and site keys and all address keys stay. D2 still allows 40P01; it returns to the job runner. |
| L15 | Write `user.erasure_swept` for target user, with numeric version and integer `householdsKept` and `activityEntriesKept`, no actor or IP. Marker, report and scrub commit together. |

The held-address interface is explicit. A fresh admin deletion captures the
freshly locked row's address as `heldAddress{value, ok: true}`; a tombstone's
`Address.ok` is false. REQ's address arm, INV arm (d), guardian-email matching,
guest signups, outbox matching and address throttle builders all skip it.
No selector binds an empty or placeholder address in place of the lost one.
No address is reconstructed from audit addresses, names, IPs or later reuse,
and no hashed opt-out is seeded from them (O-3, O-17, 7.6).

A maintenance job cannot choose a group successor. It leaves any unexpected
memberships untouched and returns `MembershipsKept` in its internal report;
the job discards that report and the marker does not store this count. The
old delete handler hard-deleted memberships, so such rows are exceptional.
Other repeat effects are conditional. A same-version repeat changes no table;
a version bump with no new work adds only its marker and at most one queued
avatar cleanup, with no timestamp churn. This is not a promise that later
versions do no work. Bump `tombstoneSweepVersion` only for a reviewed new slice.

R-6 now permits exactly two direct `eraseContactRequestTextIn` sites, one in
`apiAdminDeleteUser` after `tx.Delete(u)`, and one in `eraseTombstoneOnce` after
`found, err := lockTombstoneIn(tx, id)`, `if err != nil { return err }` and
`if !found { return errTombstoneNoop }`. Each passes its callback's own `tx`
directly in that callback's body. The callback must be the last argument of
`a.contactMutationCtx` on the method's own App receiver. The mutation call
itself sits in the method body, not another function literal. Nested
transactions, shadowed bindings, `go` or `defer` calls, page or job-handler sites
and any third site fail the parse-only guard. Its other limits stay as
recorded in T07 and `AGENTS.md`; it is not a general control-flow proof.

### 12.2 Discovery, recovery and count limits

`erasure.tombstone_sweep` joins the existing daily scheduler, queued at boot
and each church date under `erasure.tombstone_sweep:<day>`. Its persisted
`{afterId: 0, day}` starts at zero, so a delayed run keeps its original date.
`erasure.tombstone_page` continues that date's keyset chain. One 30-second
transaction selects at most 200 ids with `tombstoneCandidatesSQL` and queues
them plus a continuation only for a full page. `jobs.EnqueueMany` writes at
most 201 rows in one INSERT, below its batch size of 500. Discovery does not
wait for the subject jobs to finish.

Subject payloads contain only `{userId}`; page payloads contain only
`{afterId, day}`. Subject keys are `erasure.tombstone:<id>:v<version>:<day>`;
continuation keys are `erasure.tombstone_page:v<version>:<day>:<last id>`.
The date is parsed and the page size is limited to 1-200. All three kinds
use `jobs.DefaultMaxAttempts`, currently five, and have System labels. Errors go through
`jobs.CleanError`; no address or reconstructed identity enters a payload.

A failed page commits no jobs and retries with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`.
A crash after commit leaves its continuation durable. A poisoned subject
dies alone and alerts on System without blocking later ids. It gets a new
job and potentially another alert on each new church date until fixed.
A dead sweep or page recovers through the next day's chain from zero.
Lease takeover and a post-commit crash serialize on the contact gate and
recheck the marker. Pruning done jobs cannot undo a marker. After a restore,
a tombstone without its marker is eligible again, but a retained done job
can block today's unique key; the next church date supplies a new key.
An account restored active is outside this sweep. The operator must still
reconcile post-backup erasures before reopening access or mail (section 11).

Current `tombstoneSweepVersion` is 1. Both discovery and the locked worker
accept a marker only through `CASE WHEN jsonb_typeof(meta_json->'version') =
'number' THEN (meta_json->>'version')::numeric ELSE 0 END >= version`.
Strings, null, arrays, objects, booleans and missing values do not qualify.
The predicate accepts numeric values at least the version, including 2.5;
the writer emits integers. It never casts the other JSON shapes.

This read-only query returns the latest qualifying marker per target for
version 1. It selects only structural ids, time and the three allowed numeric
fields, not other audit metadata or personal text.

```sql
SELECT DISTINCT ON (target_id)
	target_id AS user_id, at,
	meta_json->'version' AS version,
	meta_json->'householdsKept' AS households_kept,
	meta_json->'activityEntriesKept' AS activity_entries_kept
FROM audit_logs
WHERE action = 'user.erasure_swept' AND target_type = 'user'
	AND CASE WHEN jsonb_typeof(meta_json->'version') = 'number'
		THEN (meta_json->>'version')::numeric ELSE 0 END >= 1
ORDER BY target_id, at DESC, id DESC;
```

`householdsKept` counts selected households retained in that committed run.
`activityEntriesKept` has 6.9's limits: subject-linked RETAIN_* rows with
nonempty IPs plus distinct discovered conversion rows, including already-empty
conversion IPs. It is neither a complete residue count nor proof of IP
ownership. Markers are historical reports, not a current church-wide census.
They do not report memberships, address-only residue or all unknown audit keys.
A version marker means only that version's implemented steps committed.

**O-17 is unknown per subject, never zero.** The accepted limitation remains; plan revision 8 and `r4-gr-o1` item 7 narrow the earlier proposed per-subject report to unknown plus church-wide candidate counts. The prior review did not sign this later reporting change. The following read-only query
counts church-wide candidates without matching an address to any account.
Each denominator is the entire named table, soft-deleted rows included where
applicable. Candidate rows include living people's data. The invitation arm
excludes obvious retained links, including any acceptance `userId` key even
if malformed. It does not infer an owner from that key. The audit arm counts
known address-bearing shapes, including linked rows, not arbitrary historical
keys. Counts are by row, not distinct address or person. Categories overlap
and must not be summed as an erasure total. No population query was run for
this documentation task and no real population count is claimed. Both query blocks were executed in a read-only transaction on an empty disposable database (`r46bv-sql`); this checks execution, not population results.

```sql
SELECT 'account_requests.unlinked_email' AS source, count(*) AS table_rows,
	count(*) FILTER (WHERE approved_user_id IS NULL
		AND email <> 'deleted-account_requests-' || id || '@deleted.invalid') AS candidates
FROM account_requests
UNION ALL
SELECT 'account_requests.guardian_email', count(*),
	count(*) FILTER (WHERE guardian_email <> '') FROM account_requests
UNION ALL
SELECT 'invitations.unlinked_email', count(*),
	count(*) FILTER (WHERE i.email <> 'deleted-invitations-' || i.id || '@deleted.invalid'
		AND i.household_person_id IS NULL
		AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM account_requests r
			WHERE r.invitation_id = i.id AND r.approved_user_id IS NOT NULL)
		AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM audit_logs l
			WHERE l.action = 'invitation.accepted' AND l.target_type = 'invitation'
				AND l.target_id = i.id AND l.meta_json ? 'userId'))
FROM invitations i
UNION ALL
SELECT 'guest_signups.email', count(*), count(*) FILTER (
	WHERE email <> 'deleted-guest_signups-' || id || '@deleted.invalid') FROM guest_signups
UNION ALL
SELECT 'mail_outboxes.to_address', count(*),
	count(*) FILTER (WHERE btrim(to_address) <> '') FROM mail_outboxes
UNION ALL
SELECT 'login_throttles.address_keys', count(*), count(*) FILTER (
	WHERE key LIKE 'email:%'
		OR key ~ '^(pwreset:addr:|emailchange:addr:|acctreq:addr:|guestreg:hint:|guardian:addr:|guardian-email:addr:)')
FROM login_throttles
UNION ALL
SELECT 'audit_logs.known_address_keys', count(*), count(*) FILTER (
	WHERE (action IN ('user.email_changed', 'email.change_requested', 'email.changed')
		AND (COALESCE(meta_json->>'oldEmail', '') <> ''
			OR COALESCE(meta_json->>'newEmail', '') <> ''))
		OR (action = 'invitation.created' AND COALESCE(meta_json->>'email', '') <> ''))
FROM audit_logs;
```

These are candidate counts, not a lower or upper bound on all identifying
residue. They omit unknown historical shapes and identifying text outside
these predicates, and cannot distinguish a tombstone's past address from
another holder's. They authorize no UPDATE, attribution or opt-out export.
The sweep counts, attributes and modifies no address-only row for a tombstone.

### 12.3 Evidence and measured bound

The evidence directory is
`/home/grier/.local/state/t3-autoflow/m74-remaining-20261105/`.
`r46b1-recheck.json` found the earlier correctness, security and lock-order
issues resolved and explicitly marked UI/UX unaffected. Its remaining P2
items were real-driver cancellation cleanup and evidence ordering.
`r46b1-recheck3.json` records a pass after the real-driver tests and
committed-head reruns, including full `make test` at clean `f95a474`.
`r46b2-review.json` records no P0, P1 or P2 finding for the then-uncommitted
R4-6b-2 candidate on base `f95a474`. These are prior implementation reviews,
not the later integration-head review of `eee88af` recorded in 15.7.

`r46b2-repeat2-evidence.json` and `r46b2-repeat2.log` record
`go test -count=8 -v -run Tombstone .`: 240 top-level passes, no FAIL or SKIP
and no 40P01. `r46b2-full-evidence.json` and `r46b2-full.log` record a passing
`make test`, 1,298 root tests in eight shards plus all internal packages.
Both evidence records name base `f95a474`, with R4-6b-2 still uncommitted;
they do not by themselves establish green on a later integration commit.
On `eee88af`, `r46bv-integrated-full`, `r46bv-integrated-lint`,
`r46bv-integrated-deadcode` and `r46bv-integrated-web` passed the full Go suite,
Go lint, plain deadcode, TypeScript, knip and web tests.
The poisoned-subject fixture forces `max_attempts = 1`; it does not test
exhaustion of the production default. The review's reference to eight attempts
is not the implemented limit: `jobs.DefaultMaxAttempts` is five.
No tests were run for this documentation task.

`TestTombstoneDiscoveryMeasuredPageBound` logs `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)`
and one real page job's wall time on an isolated `testdb.Fresh` database with
10,000 tombstones, 5,000 already marked, and 200,000 audit rows. Across the
eight repeat2 runs the query took 50.505 to 52.947 ms and the page job took
54.707 to 59.501 ms, under the plan's one-second page bar. The plan uses an
anti-join with `idx_audit_logs_target`; the users scan repeats per page and
is O(N) per page. This is a measured fixture bound, not a bound for every
church size or workload. No index or migration was added. A future failure
of the bar requires design review before any index or migration change.
The fresh database prevents its ANALYZE statistics from affecting deadline
fixtures; the earlier repeat log failed before that isolation fix.

This stage adds no schema, API, UI, visibility or security policy and makes
no new product decision. It does not implement R4-4b conversion-IP
minimization, R4-T07B reason review, address-level opt-out behavior or R4-SC
suppression contraction. Later R4-6 coverage, dialog, restore and hand-check
work remains separate. G-8 stays unestablished, G-9 and DG-9 OPEN; O-1, O-2,
O-15, C4 and the T07 gate dispositions are unchanged. The four-concern integration review at `eee88af` found no code or privacy
defect and identified a status-record correction; this record addresses that
finding. No push or publication is claimed.
M7.4 remains staged and incomplete.

## 13. OPEN register

State vocabulary: **Settled by source** (a fact read in the tree answers it; no decision is needed, the reviewer only
confirms the reading), **Proposed** (a disposition this draft recommends; it takes effect only if the independent
reviewer signs it), **Gated** (cannot be settled from source and the five recorded owner decisions; it waits for the
named evidence or gate; where an entry says *owner-only*, no reviewer signature can close it; O-7 was owner-only and is now answered). The prior candidate's conditional signatures and accepted classifications are recorded in sections 15 and 15.1; independent review of the later additions must also be recorded in the R4-0 PR before merge. Where an item is *Proposed*, the owner has not been asked and is not
asked now: review, not a new question, is the next step. Two items were put to the owner and answered: O-7 (decision 4), a settled authority with a Proposed design, and the bounded P2 scope under O-19/O-20 (decision 5), a settled authority for a concept, not a design and not a closed gate. Neither is an open question; neither is implemented.

| ID | Item | State | Basis (traced) | What settles it |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| O-1 | Address-level optional-mail opt-out after address reuse | **Implemented locally only; closure on master pending** | Owner decision 8 and approved `r42d2-design-rev2.json`, signed by `r42d2-review-rev2.json`. The local code exports held-address choices, combines them through `readEmailOff`, checks actual destinations at send boundaries, limits turn-on to the current holder and explains link authority. T38 and 7.6 describe behavior and limits; 15.8 records prior reviews and evidence | Final integration verification, four-concern review and local landing remain pending. PUB-1 must merge the reviewed implementation before O-1 closes on master and C2 can be met. No publication or completion is claimed |
| O-2 | Suppression plaintext → hash: Route A (baseline edit) or Route B (additive migration, contraction after a tagged expand release) | **Gated** (release evidence) | Route A needs fresh proof the DEC-138 window is open; the checkout has no tags and no remote query was made; Route B is the default and leaves plaintext until R4-SC. Route B expand implemented by R4-S (00002). It is valid whether or not the DEC-138 window is open (7.7, 10.3), so it needed no release evidence. Route A is no longer available for suppressions. The remaining gate is R4-SC. It needs an earlier `v*` tagged release containing 00002 whose publication is evidenced: either one `release.yml` run on `refs/tags/v<version>` at the tag's commit with plan, publish, smoke, finalize and notes all successful, or a run whose plan and publish succeeded completed by a `finalize_only` run on that tag at that commit with publish skipped and the other four jobs successful; plus the GHCR digest, the GitHub release and the attestation.
