## 14. Not traced in R4-0 (plainly)

- **Rows from earlier releases.** Audit rows and JSON payloads written by code no longer in the tree (6.1) and any
  column data in a real database were not read; no population scan was made and none is asked for. Testing uses
  scratch databases only.
- **Later locks inside entry transactions.** 9.2.2 names the first lock of each entry and the gate; the group, event,
  occurrence and registration locks taken inside ~45 callbacks were not enumerated, and push-side rechecks were not re-read.
- **PostgreSQL behavior is stated from its documented rules, not measured**: that an FK insert takes a key-share
  lock, that an UPDATE leaving the FK column unchanged skips the FK check, that a conflict-skipped insert fires no FK
  check, and the lock-mode compatibility used in 9.2.1. R4-N's barrier tests are what would measure them.
- **Reachability of admin PATCH and delete for every user status** (guest, invited, pending-consent) was read, not
  run (9.1).
- **`messages.audience` JSON** contents and `message_recipients.reason` values were not traced.
- **Trigger and lock behavior in this revision's new claims is read, not executed**: what `add_request_identity` allows
  for a pending add row's `reason` (T07), the blocking behavior behind the F-9 barrier test (a plain read sees the
  pre-commit row; a `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` waits for an uncommitted update, then proceeds on the committed row), the
  `INV`/`REQ`/`PERS` joins, and the sole-subject household predicate. R4-2a, R4-2d and R4-3 tests are what would run them.
- **Whether an erased consenting admin's, enabling admin's, link-deciding admin's or guardian's address sits on a teen
  conversion's completion rows** (6.5.1, C-completion) cannot be determined from stored keys; no population scan was made. An
  erased approving admin who also completed the conversion keeps that address on those rows, and the draft does not say
  otherwise. The approving admin's address on `account.approved` and on a teen's `guardian_link.requested` rows is
  determined by the retained `decided_by` (6.5.1), read from source and not executed. On an adult person's transfer rows
  it is determined only through the verified join and the exclusion (6.5.1), which depend on the writer lists, on each
  `household.person_converted` row being unique and its `registration.changed` rows numbering `transferCount` (a property of
  the writers that `audit_logs` does not enforce), and on the **history precondition (G-8)**: that the audit history was written by
  binaries that contain the staged teen conversion and the age-rollover marker (commits `93fdcb1` and `0bfafdc`, Oct 22,
  2026; no release tag exists at this commit). No row records the writing binary, so the precondition is **not established**
  by anything in this file; the default retains and reports the whole discovered group of transfer rows, which leaves the erased approving admin's
  address on them. Whether any real database holds history that fails the precondition is **unknown**: no check of any real
  database was made, and the older-binary population was not counted. Neither proposed G-8 route (an operator's statement, a
  database-level provenance proof) has been tested against a database or any operational history.
- **Candidate discovery (6.5.1, V0) is read from source, not executed, and cannot find everything.** It reaches rows with
  a text-equal retained identity key and, through arm (d), same-household conversion rows with an absent or non-number identity
  key. A row outside those rules, or excluded by the stated action/target filters, is invisible and is part of IR-4 (6.5.2).
  Normal event-registration rows remain outside the transfer candidates even when their ids match. Arm (d), its treatment of
  another malformed conversion in the household, and comparisons of JSON types were reasoned from `jsonb` operator documentation,
  not run; R4-4's tests 11 and 15 are what would run them. No count of malformed or duplicated conversion rows in any database exists.
- **The minimization options of 6.5.2 (P1 to P5) were found by reading source and PLAN, not by building or testing any of them.**
  The owner's answer (decision 5) authorizes P2's concept in a bounded scope; no implementation of it exists or was tried, and the
  number of groups, callers or living participants it would affect in any real database is unknown.
  That the same request writes a named-actor step row for three live completion callers is read at the lines cited. That no consumer
  reads `audit_logs.ip` was shown by search of non-test Go code and the cited SQL, not by a runtime trace. Whether an update other than an
  Enable can complete a conversion through `api_admin.go:797`, and whether any address is essential safeguarding evidence, are not
  settled. Who each option would affect in a real database was not counted.
- **Section 6.5.1 and section 9.6 are read from source, not executed.** The `decided_by` join, its cardinality (two partial
  unique indexes), the C-approval versus C-completion split and the household interleavings, including the admin-recipient
  lock cycle, were traced by reading the callers named; the R4-4 and R4-3 tests are what would run them. The cycle in 9.6
  requirement 5 is derived from PostgreSQL's documented lock modes, not measured.
- **Provider-side retention** (Postmark, SMTP relays, push services), mail clients, browser caches, shared downloads
  and logs were not examined (section 8).
- **The call-graph script** used for 9.2.2 is a throwaway kept outside the repository; its results are the table. A
  reviewer who doubts a row should re-derive that row by reading the callers named.
- **Author's check scope.** The inventory author ran no project test, build, lint, documentation guard, database command or
  network action while writing this revision. Separate workflow checks and review belong in the R4-0 PR. No real database or
  provider was examined, and fresh remote release evidence is still required for applicable schema work.

### 15.2 R4-1 O-21 privacy review

On 2026-11-04, GPT-6.1 Sol through the Codex harness, independent of the R4-1 author, signed O-21 for the R4-1 candidate as the narrow shared-budget retention limit described in the register. The review accepts that plaintext addresses may remain in renewed or recreated throttle keys indefinitely and that surviving audit rows can associate an address with the erased person until R4-4 addresses those records. Neither a pooled throttle row nor an audit association proves exclusive ownership of its spends; preserving the budgets protects other address users. This is not a claim of anonymity or bounded retention. Record this signature in the R4-1 pull request as well. It changes no owner decision and leaves G-7a to G-7d, G-8, G-9/O-20, O-1, O-2 and O-15 open.

- **G-7a**: reject. The predicate is FALSE: no automated scrub of reason for any purpose or status. The row's subject does not tell which parts of free reason text identify that person.

Assignment: SET add_childcare_ages_note = ''. Statuses: pending, granted, denied, withdrawn, expired, ended. D(U): the exact subject's existing users row has deleted_at IS NOT NULL, checked under FOR SHARE OF u NOWAIT. Excluded: reason on every row; rows where only the requester or an actor was erased; contact and group_add rows; every other request column, including counts, decisions, identity, actors and provenance. Condition: record the narrowed signature in the required merged claim before code.
- **G-7d**: narrow conditionally (not effective now). Identity exception: UPDATE only; OLD.purpose = 'occurrence_add'; a nonempty OLD note becomes an exactly empty NEW note; restore only the note in a copy of NEW and require the whole row IS NOT DISTINCT FROM OLD; then verify and lock the deleted subject. Completion exception: UPDATE only; explicit OLD.purpose = 'occurrence_add'; OLD.status = 'granted'; the same nonempty-to-empty transition and whole-row comparison; skip only this event's completion recheck. Conditions from the review of revision 3, kept exactly: (1) resolve T07A-R11 and T07A-R12 and incorporate the specification notes (this revision answers them, and that answer needs independent review before the implementation specification counts as approved); (2) preserve the original fallback bodies, trigger installation, timing, deferral and the identity-trigger dependency; (3) record the staging revision, narrowed signatures and two-function reconciliation in a merged claim before code; (4) after the consent dependency merges, recheck the callback, writers, lock inventory and the function definitions immediately before this migration; (5) keep every implementation evidence, migration-token, MG, O-15, SCR, merge-binding, CI and independent review gate.

### 15.4 Owner answers on the T07-B reason review (recorded Nov 5, 2026)

This section is a factual record of two owner answers about `contact_requests.reason` and of the status of the engineering design that followed them. It is documentation only. It does not design, adopt, approve or implement anything, and it changes no disposition in this file. It supersedes no signed text: owner decisions 1 to 5 (section 1), the DEC-141 amendment in `PLAN.md`, the gate verdicts in 15.3 and the signed history in 15.3 (f) are unchanged.

Decision 5 (conversion-audit IP addresses) is untouched. **Owner decision 6: contact-request reasons on erasure (recorded 2026-11-05T15:27:35.791053+00:00 UTC).**

- Question put to the owner, verbatim: **"Contact-request reasons are free text and may contain details about several people. When an account is deleted, how should Fold handle those reasons?"**
- Owner's answer, verbatim: **"Add an admin review step during deletion to decide whether to blank each reason"**
- Scope recorded with the answer, verbatim: "Product direction. Exact review workflow, safeguards and implementation require design and independent review."

**Owner decision 7: reasons of accounts deleted before the review step exists (recorded 2026-11-05T15:57:54.134083+00:00 UTC).**

- Question put to the owner, verbatim: **"For accounts deleted before the new reason-review step exists, how should Fold handle their retained contact-request reasons? Restored backups may also contain unreviewed deleted accounts."**
- Owner's answer, verbatim: **"Create a one-time admin review list for unreviewed deleted accounts (Recommended)"**
- Scope recorded with the answer, verbatim: "Design and implementation of the list, restoration behavior, access controls and safeguards require independent review."

The answers were given by the owner. Nothing is inferred from elapsed time or from any other answer. - **Identity branch.** A `reason` may go from nonempty to `''` only when the request is a non-contact,

  baseline refusal), no other column changes (a whole-row comparison that also covers future columns
  with equality operators), and the requester or subject is soft-deleted and its `users` row can be
  share-locked without waiting (`FOR SHARE OF u NOWAIT`). A living-only pair, a nonempty replacement
  and a blank combined with any other change fall through to the unchanged check and are refused with

  confirmed-rollback retry already handles.

  before the completion checks, which a deleted subject would otherwise fail with 23514. It relies on
  the BEFORE identity check for the deleted-party licence.

  or status guard, which contradicted its own statement that contact and pending rows "run the
  original body unchanged" and acceptance that they "behave as the baseline". Under contention the

  a reason-only blank on a contact or pending row could fail with 55P03 or block the R4-6b tombstone
  scrub or an account erasure holding that user row. The branch is therefore scoped to non-contact,
  non-pending rows, which preserves the baseline for contact and pending rows (no extra lock, no new

### 15.5 R4-3b claim: household and safeguarding verdicts, and the requirement 5 choice (recorded Nov 5, 2026)

This section is a documentation-only claim record for slice R4-3b (task R4-3b-0). It records what an independent review said and what the lead chose, before any household code. **Every entry below is a signed disposition; implementation unverified until R4-3b-V.** Nothing here implements, tests or closes anything.

**Source of the verdicts.** `results/r4-gr-hh.json` (task `R4-GR-HH`, sha256 `67d8621b3e0ad3552d1fd4b47dd663bb2089b10b9f3d8e66649407246e9dfa48`), reviewer gpt-6.1-sol through Codex, written Nov 4, 2026 22:17 (UTC-07:00), reviewed commit `06cc31d0ac3f01eddf8bb100e65da3c4cdc603fd`. Its stated method is local source reads only: no edit, build, test, database, network or concurrency schedule was run. Its scope is "independent safeguarding and privacy verdicts on the specified inventory dispositions"; it "does not approve an implementation or establish runtime behavior". It preserves owner decisions 1 and 2, creates no new product decision, authorizes no broader audit-IP minimization and closes no unrelated gate (G-9 and the others stay as they were). The source tree has moved since `06cc31d` (R4-3a in #277 and R4-T07A in #282 merged), so every file and line citation in the review is a pointer to re-read, not a fact about the current tree; R4-3b-V re-verifies them.

**O-4: CONFIRMED** (retention of named safeguarding prose; no arbitrary free text and no audit IP retained).

- `guardian_links.check_note`: RETAIN_VERBATIM (the fixed age-rollover sentence also stays).
- `audit_logs.meta_json.checkNote` on `guardian_link.confirmed` and `guardian_link.rejected`: RETAIN_VERBATIM.
- `teen_consents.evidence_text`: RETAIN_VERBATIM. Database immutability supports this but is not the sole reason.
- `audit_logs.meta_json.evidenceText` on `teen_consent.granted` and `teen_consent.revoked`: RETAIN_VERBATIM.
- `membership.removed.note` and `registration.removed.note` audit metadata: SCRUB_WHEN_SUBJECT_IS_ERASED, selected by the erased subject's retained `userId` or applicable `personId` in `PERS`, never by the erased actor alone. Decisions, actors, dates and authorization metadata are preserved. **Audit removal-note scrubbing stays with R4-4**, not R4-3b.
- `registrations.removal_note`: CONFIRM_EXISTING_SUBJECT_SCRUB. R4-2a already clears it where `user_id` is the erased account; R4-3b extends it to staged-person registrations. Another registrant's note is untouched when only its author or `removed_by` actor is erased.
- No name matching or speculative text redaction is approved. R4-3b must assert the safeguarding rows and mirrors above stay unchanged.

**O-9: CONFIRMED.** The sole-subject test is evaluated in the erasure transaction after the household lock is held, the erased account's seat is deleted and `PERS` is anonymized. S1: no `household_accounts` row remains for the household, whatever the linked user's status or deletion state. S2: every `household_people` row in the household, soft-deleted rows included, belongs to `PERS` (an empty people set satisfies S2). A sole-subject household gets `name = 'Removed household'` and `address = ''`; its id, attribution and history stay. A shared or ambiguous household (another seat belongs to a legacy tombstone, or another person is soft-deleted, included) keeps its name and address unchanged and is counted without names; R4-6 re-evaluates after its legacy sweep. Implementation conditions: unscoped person reads and seat counts that do not filter linked users; surviving children, siblings and other holders preserved; abort on a lock or predicate failure rather than guess; and the section 9.6 writer revalidation and concurrency work done so a stale request cannot restore scrubbed data.

**O-14: CONFIRMED_SOURCE_READING.** Completed conversion does not revoke the duplicate person's guardian links. R4-3b selects `guardian_links.person_id IN PERS` and revokes every pending or confirmed match, auditing each with reason `account_deleted` and resolving its pending task where one exists. Rejected and revoked history and the O-4 evidence stay. The completed person's `converted_user_id` is left exactly as stored. A sibling's links are not selected merely because they share the household; the links of an erased guardian still follow the guardian-erasure rule. The review calls this required behavior, "not implemented or runtime-verified by this review".

**O-18: CONFIRMED.** Owner decision 2 applies to an approved but incomplete teen conversion: the linked household person is the erased individual while `converted_user_id` is NULL. Identity comes from the approved `account_requests` row with `source = 'household_conversion'`, `status = 'approved'`, `approved_user_id = U` and a non-null `household_person_id`, soft-deleted person rows included. **`PERS` integrity is validated before any mutation; on a contradictory identity link the erasure aborts and never widens the selector.** Required behavior: clear the person's names, birth date and care notes, set `photo_consent = false`, soft-delete the person and leave `converted_user_id` NULL; revoke pending and confirmed person-side and teen-account-side guardian links, keeping O-4 evidence and auditing the revocations; after anonymization, cancel future scheduled going, maybe and waitlisted person registrations through the existing person-removal reconciliation (audit, safe notices, waitlist promotion); scrub capability and note fields on all selected person registrations while keeping identity links, `registered_by` and access provenance, and never assigning `user_id` as if conversion had completed; revoke unused guardian-owned teen-consent tokens naming the erased teen and keep used tokens of a living guardian; and prove later consent, guardian confirmation, enablement and age rollover cannot complete or revive the erased pair. R4-3b-V must test deletion before consent, after consent but before guardian confirmation, completed conversion, sibling preservation, registration cancellation without transfer and later non-revival; source guards alone do not satisfy that.

**Section 9.6 requirement 5: the lead chose option (a).** The review's requirement 5 recommendation is explicitly `binding: false` and names the same option as preferred; the choice below is the lead's, recorded here as the closure 9.6 left open ("neither chosen here"). Every household lock on the erasure path is taken with `NOWAIT`, and the whole erasure transaction is retried a bounded number of times on SQLSTATE `55P03`. The cycle it closes: the erasure holds admin `A` `FOR UPDATE` and requests household `H`, while a household writer holds `H` and `notify.Send` requests `A` `FOR KEY SHARE`. The reviewer's six conditions, verbatim:

1. "Cover every household acquisition on the erasure path, including staged-conversion helper acquisitions. A later NOWAIT does not help if lockConversionParticipants already waited on H."
2. "On 55P03, return immediately and roll back the entire transaction, releasing the contact gate and user locks before retrying."
3. "Each attempt reloads target, actor, identity sets and household facts. Do not reuse mutated model state from a rolled-back attempt."
4. "Bound attempts and elapsed time. Exhaustion returns failure with no partial erasure; never skip household work or report success."
5. "Keep audit, notices and delivery intents transactional. Retry must not duplicate committed effects."
6. "Prove both schedules with the real erased-admin recipient, PID/blocker evidence and recorded SQLSTATE. Require rollback and retry-exhaustion assertions, and no unexplained 40P01."

The reviewer's alternative, sorted recipient prelocking before `H`, is **not** chosen. The lead's reason (not the reviewer's): promotion and registration-holder recipients are written under the household lock (`api_registrations.go:521-539`, `registration_subjects.go:218-226`) and are known only after the occurrence locks, so a complete recipient set cannot be prelocked before `H`. The requirement stands as a source-derived design only: the review ran no concurrency schedule, 9.6's R4-3b-LOCK-UPGRADE recheck and its "admin recipient" barrier test remain required, and the system is not proven deadlock-free.

**Implementation flagged for R4-3b-V (lead-recorded, not a reviewer verdict).** T16's sole-subject test is S1 (no `household_accounts` row) plus S2 for one erased owner. It is evaluated by every erasure that locks the household (admin delete or tombstone), first for its own subject and then for any single soft-deleted `X` whose `PERS(X)` holds all of the household's people, so a retained household is re-evaluated when a later erasure removes the seat that kept it. No per-user marker suppresses that re-evaluation. Residue rows for R4-6e: two erased subjects' people make the household shared and it is retained; a shared household whose last living holder leaves by a non-erasure path stays retained.

**What this record does not do.** It lands no code. It does not claim R4-3b, requirements 5 or 6 or the household scrub implemented, tested or reviewed; it closes no gate (G-8, G-9, O-1, O-2, O-15 and the T07 gates are as 15.3 and 15.4 left them); it adds no product decision; and it does not declare M7.4 complete or account erasure done. Implementation status since this claim (committed locally, staged) is recorded in [15.6](#156-r4-ctx-and-r4-3b-implementation-record-staged-recorded-nov-5-2026) and 9.6.1; the dispositions above keep their wording.

### 15.6 R4-CTX and R4-3b implementation record (staged, recorded Nov 5, 2026)

This is the documentation record of task R4-3b-V. It describes what the committed code does and what the independent reviews found. It is a record, not a review signature, and it changes none of the signed dispositions of 15.5, which keep their wording. **Staged, not complete:** the three slices below are committed on the local integration branch only. Nothing is pushed, no pull request exists, nothing is merged to `master`, no gate is closed and M7.4 is not complete. The four-concern review of the landing head and the green checks on that exact head are recorded by the lead in the slice's review file and `state.json`; this note states no test result for any head.

**Commits (local).** R4-CTX `595cdfb` (Bound account deletion transactions), R4-3b-1 `3741d82` (Retry household lock contention), R4-3b-2 `66b2566` (Erase household records with accounts). The claim record is 15.5 (`9a475d0`).

**Reviews (read-only, by a non-implementer; files under the autoflow `results/` directory).**

| Slice | First review | Findings | Last review |
|---|---|---|---|
| R4-CTX | `r4ctx-review.json` | R4CTX-R01 (bounded backend lifetime dropped), R4CTX-R02 (forward-then-close could deliver the reply it promised to suppress), R4CTX-R03 (extended-query preparation mistaken for execution) | `r4ctx-recheck.json` kept R03 open for cached executions; `r4ctx-recheck2.json`: pass, no findings |
| R4-3b-1 | `r43b1-review.json` | R43B1-R01 (commit-after-retry test armed on a logged, not executed, statement), R43B1-R02 (promotion schedule did not assert the promotion), R43B1-R03 (households selected only through PERS were not covered) | `r43b1-recheck.json`: pass; `r43b1-integrated-review.json`: pass |
| R4-3b-2 | `r43b2-review.json` | R43B2-REV-01 (P1: a four-person fixture could not reach the barrier once integrity was checked), R43B2-REV-02 (P1: a person converted to U but claimed by another account's approved request passed validation) | `r43b2-recheck.json`: pass; `r43b2-integrated-review.json`: pass |

Both integrated reviews say they do not establish full-suite completion or deadlock freedom.

**R4-CTX as implemented** (`contact_lifecycle.go`, `account_erasure.go`, `api_admin.go`, `web/src/lib/errors.ts`, `guards_test.go`, `internal/testdb/pgproxy.go`).

- `contactMutation` and `contactMutationCtx` share `contactMutationOn`. The former preserves the database handle and its existing context; the latter installs the supplied context on a cloned handle and applies deadline bounds when present. With a deadline D it returns `errCommitReserve` and sends nothing when `time.Until(D)` is at most `contactCommitReserve` (2 s). Otherwise it takes one dedicated connection, sets a **session-level** `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout = I` (the time left, whole milliseconds rounded up) before BEGIN, and the first statement after BEGIN sets `statement_timeout = S` (`contactServerCap`, 20 s) and the same idle value locally. **This departs from the plan's transaction-local wording** on review finding R4CTX-R01: a statement timeout aborts the transaction and the abort reverts a transaction-local setting, which left an aborted backend connected for as long as its socket stayed open. The connection is always discarded when the call ends, so the session setting never reaches the pool. Gate and locks are released, and the backend is gone, by D + S + I whatever happens to the cancel and the socket (for the admin route D is 40 s, S is 20 s and I is at most 40 s). No code or test equates return with release.
- Errors. Before COMMIT (the gate, the callback, either reconcile, the reserve check) the transaction is rolled back and the error returned unchanged; if the ROLLBACK is not acknowledged it is a `*contactRollbackError` (`errors.Is(err, errContactRollback)`, `Unwrap` gives the cause only), and `rollbackConfirmed(err)` is true only for a pre-COMMIT error with no such wrapper. Any COMMIT error is a `*contactCommitError` (`errContactCommit`), which has no `Unwrap`, so no consumer classifies a commit failure by its cause. A context that ends between the reserve check and COMMIT is conservatively unknown; a done context without a deadline also gives `errCommitReserve`. A panic in `fn` rolls back and panics again.
- `classifyContactErr` is the one classifier: commit error first (unknown), then `errCommitReserve`, `DeadlineExceeded`, `Canceled` and SQLSTATE 57014 (timed out), else failed. `withStatementTimeout` never raises the cap: inside the bounded call the recipient `UPDATE` runs under the smaller of its own wait and S.
- `apiAdminDeleteUser` uses one `context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), adminDeleteBudget)` per request (not the request context, so a disconnect cannot abort a commit). Unknown outcome: 500 `delete_outcome_unknown` ("Fold couldn't confirm whether the account was deleted. Reload this person's page to see whether they are gone before trying again."), logged without the account or an address (`jobs.CleanError`), never nothing-changed wording. Timed out: 503 `delete_timed_out`. Both go through `jsonErrorCode` (`errorBody` rewrites every other 5xx to `server_error`), so `api_errors.go` is unchanged. The existing recipient-scrub 57014 test now expects 503 `delete_timed_out` instead of a generic 500.
- Guard R-6 at the R4-CTX stage (`TestContactRequestTextChangesStayInTheirHelpers`): the one `eraseContactRequestTextIn` call stays in the callback of `apiAdminDeleteUser`, which must now be the last argument of `a.contactMutationCtx` on that function's own App receiver, with the call itself in the function body (a `for` loop is allowed, a func literal is not). The old `a.contactMutation` form at that site, another receiver, a shadowing local, a callback that is not last and a nested `tx.Transaction` body are negative fixtures. A fixture with the `set_config` literal joins the H8 clean tree. R4-6b later adds the second, separately guarded tombstone site; the current two-site rule is recorded in 12.1 and `AGENTS.md`.
- `internal/testdb/pgproxy.go` is a test-only fault proxy (the import check keeps `testdb` out of production). It forwards the wire protocol unchanged, logs every execution (cached extended-query executions included) and each CancelRequest, Terminate and FIN as forwarded, held or dropped, and arms faults on the first connection whose statement matches (`Faults.Match`): COMMIT reply held, forwarded then closed, closed unforwarded; cancel held or dropped; Terminate and FIN withheld together, or Terminate forwarded with FIN held; ROLLBACK closed unforwarded or forwarded then closed. Its `.deadcode-allow` line is the one permanent test-support allowance.
- Tests that must pass unchanged at every later landing head: the commit-phase cases (a) to (d) in `TestAdminDeleteCommitPhaseAnswersUnknown` (reply held, forward-then-close, close-unforwarded, a test-owned deferred constraint failure: all 500 `delete_outcome_unknown`, none `delete_timed_out`), `TestContactMutationCtxRollbackCompletion`, `TestContactMutationCtxGateWaitExpiry`, `…ServerCapBelowBudget`, `…CancelDroppedTerminateWithheld`, `…CancelDelayed`, `…ReconciliationExpiry`, `…ReserveAndClassifier`, `…Helpers`, `TestAdminDeleteGateWaitAnswersTimedOut`, and the proxy proofs in `internal/testdb/pgproxy_test.go` (`TestProxyCommitModes`, `…RollbackModes`, `…CancelModes`, `…CloseModes`, `…ForwardThenCloseNeverAnswers`, `…ExtendedTransactionEnds`). R4-3b-1 adds `TestAdminDeleteHouseholdBusyThenCommitUnknown` and `TestAdminDeleteHouseholdBusyRollbackNotAcknowledged`. **Acceptance for the landing head** (the lead's to establish): all of these pass on the exact head, so the first candidate PUB-1 may publish alone never retries an unproven COMMIT or an unconfirmed ROLLBACK. An unknown COMMIT never gets nothing-deleted copy; an unacknowledged pre-COMMIT ROLLBACK keeps its applicable failure answer, without claiming locks were released.

**R4-3b-1 as implemented** (`account_erasure_households.go`, `api_admin.go`): see 9.6.1 for the census, the six conditions and the limits. The plan's `retryHouseholdBusy(ctx, attempt)` wrapper became a `householdRetry` value (`newHouseholdRetry`, `after(err)`) because guard R-6 requires the `a.contactMutationCtx` call in `apiAdminDeleteUser` itself and rejects it inside a func literal; the caller owns a `for` loop and asks after each attempt, and the tests drive the same loop through a helper. Behavior is as planned. `deleteRetryWindow` (2 s) and `householdRetryBackoff` are package variables so tests can widen them.

**R4-3b-2 as implemented, and its exact scope.** The household step (T12, T14 to T16, T28, T36) runs once, after `eraseContactRequestTextIn` and before `promoteOnDisable`, inside the locks `lockErasureHouseholdsIn` took. For one erased account it:

| Does | Does not |
|---|---|
| deletes the account's own `household_accounts` seat | touch any other account's seat |
| anonymizes the PERS person row(s), unscoped (names `''`, `birth_date` NULL, `care_notes` `''`, `photo_consent` false, soft-deleted) | write `converted_user_id`, or widen PERS by household, kind, birth date or surname |
| for a **staged** person, cancels future going, maybe and waitlisted registrations through `reconcileHouseholdPerson` (audit, a notice to the living holder, promotion), then clears `manage_token_hash`, `childcare_ages_note` and `removal_note` on every PERS registration | set `user_id` or `registered_by`, or touch an `access_*` column; a completed duplicate has no registrations of its own |
| revokes pending and confirmed guardian links of PERS, of the account as teen and of the account as guardian, one `GuardianLinkRevoked` audit row each (`reason: account_deleted`), and resolves the pending task | change `check_note`, `confirmed_by`, rejected or revoked rows, sibling links outside the explicit teen/guardian selector, `teen_consents` or any audit mirror of safeguarding prose (O-4) |
| deletes the guardian's **unused** `teen_consent` tokens whose payload `teenId` is the account | delete a used token, or another teen's |
| sets a household to `name = 'Removed household'`, `address = ''` when `householdErasedOwnerIn` proves it sole-subject; counts it | scrub a household with a living seat, a surviving person, or an owner it cannot prove (retained and counted); delete a household |
| answers `{"erasure": {householdsKept, householdsScrubbed, peopleAnonymized, guardianLinksRevoked}}` (counts only) | name a person, id or address in the report; the admin UI does not display it yet |

It aborts the whole erasure before any lock or write on a contradictory identity link (`validatePersIn`, T15). Not in this slice and unchanged: audit `meta_json` removal-note scrubbing and every audit IP (R4-4, gates G-8 and G-9), the legacy tombstone sweep that re-evaluates retained households (R4-6), `reason` review (R4-T07B), address-level opt-outs (R4-2d2) and suppression storage (R4-SC).

**O-9 and the correctness check R4-3b-V asks for.** The plan's sole-subject test is S1 plus S2 for one erased owner. For the subject, `householdErasedOwnerIn` computes exactly that and returns before any other branch: S1 is "no `household_accounts` row of any user remains" and S2 is "every `household_people` row, soft-deleted included, is in PERS(U)", with an empty household sole-subject only when it is in the subject's own seat set. The only addition is the single-owner branch for a household with at least one person and no seat, where exactly one soft-deleted account owns every person and its PERS is one person; it can only return a household the subject-first test also leaves retained, and it retains whenever two owners, an unowned person or an ambiguous PERS appear. It therefore **widens nothing for the subject** and never scrubs on a guess; a household with any seat (a living holder, a disabled account or an earlier tombstone) is retained, and re-evaluated by whichever later erasure locks it. Residue rows for R4-6e are in the O-9 row of the OPEN register. The reviewers `r43b2-recheck.json` and `r43b2-integrated-review.json` found no regression in identity validation, locking, retry, safeguarding retention, non-revival or count-only reporting.

**What this record does not do.** It claims no passing check, no merge and no publication. It closes no gate: G-8, G-9, O-1, O-2, O-15, the T07 gates and DG-9 are as 15.3 and 15.4 left them. It adds no product decision and no owner question. It adds no hand check for the first church: the slice is database and HTTP behavior proved on disposable databases, and no real-device or live-service step was found. It does not say account erasure is done or M7.4 complete; the remaining slices (R4-4, R4-SC, R4-2d2, R4-T07B, R4-6) are as listed in section 12 and the status line in `STATUS.md`.

### 15.9 T07B-1 (1b and 1c) deletion outcomes: committed locally, prior evidence and limits (Nov 6, 2026)

This is the documentation record of task T07B-1-V. It starts from clean lane HEAD `5f941e6`, changes
no code and ran no test. No push, pull request, merge to master, deployment or external contact is
recorded, and nothing here is human approval. It describes what the committed code does, what
earlier checks recorded on uncommitted trees, and what is still pending. It closes no gate: C4 is not
met, M7.4 is not complete, and G-8, G-9, DG-9, O-1, O-2, O-15 and the T07 gates are as before. The
slice reads and writes no `reason`.

**Commits (local, staged, unmerged).** `cd3ad9a` "Add deletion outcome tracking" (T07B-1b, on
integration `8989979`); `99fd0b3` merges `a1198e6` (the 00007 reason schema) into that lane; `5f941e6`
"Show tracked deletion outcomes" (T07B-1c). 1b and 1c are one release unit and land together. The
approved designs are plan rev8 (`plan-rev8.json`, T07B-1b, T07B-1c, T07B-1-V) and
`t07b-design-rev8.json` in the host evidence directory
`~/.local/state/t3-autoflow/m74-remaining-20261105/`.

**What the committed code does.**

- **Boot step.** `bootRestoreFence` (`restore_fence.go`) runs in `serve` after migrations and before
  anything serves. One transaction under the migrations advisory lock creates the `settings` row
  `restore_fence` if absent and advances it; the value becomes the in-memory floor only after an
  acknowledged COMMIT. Any failure, an unacknowledged COMMIT included, exits non-zero. `serve` also
  makes the per-process attempt key first and fails closed if it cannot.
- **Commit-aware fence.** Every fenced `a.contactMutationCtx` (`apiAdminDeleteUser` and the settle route)
  takes the restore ticket as the first statement of its callback: the one-slot turn (selected against
  the transaction's context), then the counter `UPDATE ... RETURNING` inside the transaction. Lock order
  is exclusive contact gate, then turn, then settings row. The statement right after the call raises
  the floor to the ticket's value only when the call returned nil (an acknowledged COMMIT), and only then
  releases the turn; an error, an unknown COMMIT included, raises nothing, and a deferred abandon
  releases a turn still held. The turn keeps each COMMIT-to-acknowledgment interval ahead of any later
  ticket read, which is why a delayed acknowledgment is not mistaken for a restore. A ticket at or below
  the floor, or a missing row, is 503 `restore_detected`; `requestRestart` cancels `serve`'s context
  and `serve` returns 1 so the supervisor restarts it, which runs the boot step again. The mint and
  settle routes and the deletion refuse with `restore_detected` once the flag is set.
  `TestFencedTransactionsTakeTheTicketFirst` (parse-only) pins the ticket order; `TestRestoreFenceHasTwoWriters`
  and `TestSettingsWritersRefuseTheFenceKey` keep the row's writers to the boot step and the ticket.
- **Attempt ids and the ledger** (`admin_operation_attempts.go`). `POST /api/v1/admin/operation-attempts`
  (admin, CSRF) mints an 80-hex-character id: 16 random bytes, the minting time in milliseconds since this
  process started, and a truncated HMAC over both, the kind, the admin and the subject under a 32-byte key
  made at start and kept only in memory. The id travels in the request body. The ledger is a mutex-guarded
  map with forward-only states (running, applied, rolled_back, unknown, fenced); an entry ends once, and
  a deferred guard turns an exit that recorded no outcome into unknown. The mutex makes no database call,
  channel operation, select, sleep or context wait (`TestLedgerMutexHoldsNoWait`). The submission check is
  the first step after JSON decoding, so every refusal after it ends the id `rolled_back`; the ledger is
  checked before the id's age. A deletion without an `attemptId` (an old bundle) still takes a ticket and
  the seal and gets the same deadline and error codes. The receipt row (T39) is inserted right after the
  ticket in the deletion's transaction, and a daily `admin_attempts.sweep` job deletes receipts older than
  24 hours.
- **Settle** (`POST .../settle`, admin, CSRF). Pure checks first (a bad MAC, a wrong tuple or an id older than 60
  minutes answers `history_unavailable`, touching nothing); then a read-only ledger look (a running
  attempt is waited for with no transaction, gate or lock, for at most the 10-second settle budget, then
  `checking`); then one fenced transaction whose callback is the ticket alone, with the actor decided in the
  seal, the ledger transition (an absent id becomes fenced, so it can never run) and plain reads of the
  receipt and the subject. The answer is sent only after an acknowledged COMMIT. `applied` comes from an
  acknowledged commit or from an unknown one with a receipt; `not_applied` comes only from the ledger in
  memory (rolled_back or fenced), with `subjectDeleted: true` present only when the account is already
  deleted; an unknown outcome without a receipt is `history_unavailable`; a busy seal gets at most three
  sealed transactions within 2 seconds, then `checking`.
- **Actor seal.** `contactChange.Seal` runs after `reconcileContacts` and just before the commit-reserve
  check. `currentAdminIn` is one statement, `SELECT id, status, deleted_at, role, account_type, birth_date,
  password_hash, session_version, logout_version FROM users WHERE id = ? FOR SHARE NOWAIT`, scanned into a
  fresh `models.User` and decided with `CanSignIn()` and both session versions: 401 when the row is gone,
  deleted or cannot sign in or a version differs, 403 unless the row is still an admin, and `errActorBusy`
  on SQLSTATE 55P03. It is the last locking statement, so it adds no wait-for edge. The deletion retries a
  busy seal at most three times in the R4-3b-1 window and then answers 409 `admin_account_busy` with
  nothing deleted. `TestActorSealIsLast` (parse-only), `TestCurrentAdminInFacts`, `TestActorSealBusy` and
  `TestActorSealVsConversion` cover it.
- **Deployment gate.** `recoveryPolicyLevel` is 1 in `restore_fence.go`; `deploy/recovery-policy` holds `1`;
  `fold version --json` reports `recoveryPolicy` 1; `TestRecoveryPolicyFileMatchesConstant` fails when
  they differ. R4-RP's scripted tooling refuses a target below the greater of the host's floor file and the
  pinned image's level on provision, forward upgrade and rollback. **A release carrying this code is
  level 1 and must not be published before R4-6c-R's current-release restore tooling and operator procedure
  land and are verified.** The deployment-level drill below has not run, and it would not satisfy that gate.
- **Codes.** `attempt_expired` (409), `attempt_closed` (409), `attempt_busy` (503), `restore_detected`
  (503) and `admin_account_busy` (409) have `ERROR_COPY` lines (`web/src/lib/errors.ts`) and `types.ts`
  contracts; `TestWebErrorCopyCoversEveryCode` and `TestWebTypesMatchGoJSON` cover them.
- **Panel (1c).** `DeleteUserPanel` mints a fresh id per press, keeps it in component memory and drops it
  when the panel closes (a late answer after close is ignored). A lost, aborted, unreadable (non-JSON or
  empty body on a failure status) or ambiguous answer, `delete_outcome_unknown`, `attempt_closed` and
  `attempt_expired` settle the same id with Delete off and show no "nothing was deleted" copy first. Settle
  answers: "The account was deleted."; "Nothing was deleted by this attempt." (a fresh id on the next press),
  or with `subjectDeleted` "The account is already deleted; this attempt did not delete it."; "Fold can't
  tell whether an earlier delete finished. Reload this person's page to check."; and "Still checking" with
  Check again on the same id. Definitive refusals discard the id. After `history_unavailable` or
  `restore_detected`, Delete stays off, for that person and for every other person's panel in the case of
  a restore, until a users-list load that began afterward succeeds (`lib/deleteReload.ts`). Delete's
  success shows the `householdsKept` and `activityEntriesKept` numbers only. Disable is unaffected.
- **Test-only clock.** `attempt_clock_e2e.go` (build tag `e2eclock`) and `e2e_hooks.go` (an `e2eRoutes`
  variable, nil without the tag) add an offset to the attempt clock and an admin-only, CSRF-protected
  `POST /api/v1/e2e/attempt-clock`, and only when `FOLD_E2E_CLOCK=1`. `web/e2e/run.sh` and `server.sh`
  build with the tag and set the variable. The Dockerfile, release workflow, `make test` and plain
  `go build` set neither; no test pins that, so it is checked by review.

**Stated limits.**

- An id applies at most once, only in the process that minted it, only if first submitted within 10
  minutes. It can be settled for 60 minutes and the ledger prunes entries after 61. A restart, another
  process or a different admin, account or kind cannot recognize it: submitted, it answers
  `attempt_expired` (the submission ran nothing); settled, `history_unavailable`. No outcome is promised
  after a restart or after 60 minutes. At the 100,000-entry cap a fresh id answers `attempt_busy`.
- **The fence detects a restore only in one way.** A stored counter or boot value alone detects nothing;
  detection is a ticket at or below the in-memory floor, which means the database lost a fenced COMMIT
  this process saw acknowledged. Not detected, and stated: a restore of a dump taken after the last
  acknowledged fenced commit (nothing was lost, and a settle of an earlier id still answers `applied`), a
  restore that loses only an unknown COMMIT (its receipt is gone; settle answers `history_unavailable`,
  never `not_applied`, and the id stays closed), and a restore made while the process is stopped (the
  new process has no floor to protect). `TestRestoreFenceUndetectedCases` pins the first two answers.
  A restore made by the Droplet's backup tooling or by hand is outside the paths this release protects
  and is disclosed as such; R4-6c-R and R4-6c-V own the tooling and procedure that replace the interim
  hand check in `docs/FIRST-CHURCH-CHECKS.md`.
- The lock-order and no-false-detection arguments are in the design and exercised by tests; they are not
  a proof of deadlock freedom, and the parse-only guards state their own blind spots (SQL sent around
  GORM, values built elsewhere, helper bodies they do not parse).

**Prior candidate evidence (not evidence at `5f941e6`).** Host evidence files named below are in
`~/.local/state/t3-autoflow/m74-remaining-20261105/`; each `*-evidence.json` records the command, `head`,
log path and exit code. The runs checked uncommitted working trees, so the `head` they record is the
base, not a commit containing the code.

| Candidate and recorded base | Recorded checks | Independent review |
|---|---|---|
| 1b, uncommitted tree on `8989979` (committed afterward as `cd3ad9a`) | `t07b1b-review-full2-evidence.json`: `make test`, 1,377 root tests in 8 shards, exit 0. `t07b1b-review-repeat2-evidence.json`: `-count=8` over the attempt, settle, seal, restore, outcome, deadline, ledger and recovery-policy tests, exit 0. `t07b1b-review-mutations2-evidence.json`: mutations M1 to M10 (turn released before the floor raise, floor raised on error, detection off by one, seal skipped, settle fences a running attempt, unknown recorded as rolled back, receipt not inserted, seal waits, settle window not rechecked, `subjectDeleted` always sent) all killed at `COUNT=8`. `t07b1b-review-lint2-evidence.json`: `make lint`, exit 0. Earlier `t07b1b-deadcode-evidence.json` and `t07b1b-web-evidence.json` passed at an earlier stage and were not repeated after the review fixes | `t07b1b-review.json` found six findings (R1 to R6: the settle busy window after backoff, delayed-acknowledgment variants, the shared-process server-outlives-handler test, authorization and lock matrices, settlement and lifecycle cases, conversion evidence); `t07b1b-review-fix.json` and `t07b1b-fix2.json` addressed them; `t07b1b-recheck.json`: pass, no findings, with the advisories to refresh the lock census after `a1198e6` and that the 1c landing and the publication gate remain |
| 1c, uncommitted tree on `99fd0b3` (committed afterward as `5f941e6`) | `t07b1c-fix2-web-evidence.json`: `tsc --noEmit`, `knip` and Vitest, 119 tests in 3 files (`DeleteUserPanel.test.tsx`, `api.test.ts`, `deleteReload.test.ts`), exit 0. `t07b1c-fix2-e2e-evidence.json`: Playwright `admin-delete-user.spec.ts` and `account-deletion.spec.ts`, 15 passed including the seed, none skipped. `t07b1c-fix2-layout-evidence.json`: `layout.spec.ts`, 4 parts passed. `t07b1c-fix2-guards-evidence.json`: `TestUIRatchets`, `TestTestFilesSitNextToTheirSource`, `TestDocsSayRecommendedNotSupported`, exit 0. 390 px and 1280 px screenshots of the outcome states are in `t07b1c-fix-shots/` and `t07b1c-shots/` | `t07b1c-review.json` found R01 (P1: a malformed 2xx could skip tracking or say "deleted" without settling) and R02 to R04; `t07b1c-recheck.json` found R05 to R07; `t07b1c-fix.json` and `t07b1c-fix2.json` addressed them; `t07b1c-recheck2.json`: pass, no findings; the reviewer inspected the screenshots at 390 and 1280 px and recorded no schema or production-query change |

How the evidence ties to the commits: `t07b1b-review-fix-files.sha256` (24 files, written after the last
1b run) matches the blobs in `cd3ad9a` byte for byte, and `t07b1c-fix2-files.sha256` (17 files) matches the
tree at `5f941e6`; this task checked both by hashing, which is not a test run. The merge `99fd0b3` and 1c
then changed `api.go`, `web/src/types.ts`, `guards_test.go`, `AGENTS.md` and `STATUS.md`, so the 1b checks
never ran on the combined tree.

**Gaps in that evidence, stated plainly.**

- Both candidate runs were on dirty trees; the 1b suite did not include the 00007 schema merge or 1c.
- The 1b full-suite log is timestamped about twenty minutes before the final fingerprint snapshot; no
  record ties that run to the exact final bytes, though the later repeat, mutation and lint runs cover
  most of the same files.
- The P-5 census (settings writers, writers of the session, role, status and deleted columns, and the
  users-row lockers with their modes) was produced on `8989979` plus the dirty tree and is a host file
  (`t07b1b-census/`), not committed. It has not been refreshed on the integrated head.
- The 1c browser runs were the two targeted specs on one worker with `E2E_APP_ENV=dev`, not the sharded
  `make e2e`; the full a11y spec and the `verify-ui` skill are not recorded on the fixed 1c candidate.
- Disclosed test limits: server-log capture for unexpected database errors covers the non-proxied
  conversion schedules (the proxied schedule relies on its statement log), and the pgproxy-held-COMMIT
  delayed-acknowledgment variant has the second admin deleting only.
- No Compose, real-droplet or deployment-level check of this code has run.

**Pending, in order (none is done by this record).**

1. Exact-head final checks on the landing head: the full Go suite, lint, deadcode, web checks, the
   repeat and mutation runs for the changed tests, `make e2e` and `verify-ui` with 390 px and 1280 px
   screenshots, and the lock census refreshed on that head.
2. A four-concern integration review (UI/UX and design; database and performance; security; correctness)
   by an agent other than the implementer, with each concern explicitly covered or marked not affected.
3. The deployment-level drill on the first releasable level-1 candidate, on the exact landing head:
   `deploy-drill.sh` provisioning it in image mode through the drill registry and once in build mode;
   `provision.sh` to the lane base's image and `provision.sh --allow-onbox-build` at the lane base's
   commit, `upgrade.sh --rollback` to the level-0 release recorded before the upgrade, and forward
   `upgrade.sh --commit` and `upgrade.sh --image` to the lane base, each run from `DIR/upgrade.sh` and from
   the candidate checkout, all expected to exit non-zero before any backup, release record, checkout or pin
   and to name the restore barrier; a same-level redeploy succeeding with `DIR/recovery-policy-floor`
   reading 1; and the transcript attached to the review.
4. Local landing on the integration branch with a merge commit green on the exact head, the review
   recorded and the SHA in the host state record, with nothing pushed.
5. The human first-church checks in `docs/FIRST-CHURCH-CHECKS.md` (a real-droplet restore, settling and
   resubmitting a pre-restore attempt, and a `restore_detected` restart under Compose). They need a
   disposable copy of the droplet and have not been run.
6. R4-6c-R's restore tooling and operator procedure, and R4-6c-V, before any release at level 1 or above.
